Miura operators as R-matrices from M-brane intersections
Nathan Haouzi, and Saebyeok Jeong

Abstract
We propose that Miura operators are R-matrices of certain infinite-dimensional quantum algebras. We test our proposal by realizing Miura operators of q q -deformed W W - and Y Y -algebras in terms of R-matrices of the quantum toroidal algebra of 𝔤 ​ 𝔩 ​ ( 1 ) \mathfrak{gl}(1) . Physically, the representations of this toroidal algebra arise from the algebra of local operators on M2-branes and M5-branes, in M-theory subject to an Ω \Omega -background. We associate an R-matrix to each M2-M5 brane crossing, by studying its description as a gauge-invariant intersection of a topological line defect and a holomorphic surface defect in 5-dimensional non-commutative Chern-Simons theory. The Miura transformation is engineered using multiple M2-M5 intersections, relying crucially on the properties of the underlying R-matrices. We thereby identify each R-matrix with a Miura operator. In a dual type IIB frame, the components of the Miura transformation are shown to coincide with the half-index of a 3d supersymmetric gauge theory on a Hanany-Witten system of D3-NS5 branes. As a further application, we demonstrate that q ​ q qq -characters can be algebraically constructed from the Miura transformation.



Preprint:
CERN-TH-2024-094

1
Introduction

In two-dimensional conformal field theory, the Miura transformation
1
1

1

In the present work, the Miura transformation always refers to the
quantum
Miura transformation, unless specified otherwise.
is a procedure used to construct the holomorphic generators of the theory in terms of free bosons. The most celebrated example is the construction of the
\mathcal{W}_{N}
-algebra, a higher spin generalization of the Virasoro
vertex algebra consisting of
N
generators (we add an additional free boson to the usual
W
-algebra of
\mathfrak{sl}(N)
, and call it the
W
-algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(N)
). In this case, the Miura transformation states that the generating currents
U_{k}(z)
of the
\mathcal{W}_{N}
-algebra are constructed by

({\varepsilon}_{3}\partial_{z}-{\varepsilon}_{1}{\varepsilon}_{2}J_{N}(z))({\varepsilon}_{3}\partial_{z}-{\varepsilon}_{1}{\varepsilon}_{2}J_{N-1}(z))\cdots({\varepsilon}_{3}\partial_{z}-{\varepsilon}_{1}{\varepsilon}_{2}J_{1}(z))=\sum_{k=0}^{N}U_{k}(z)\left({\varepsilon}_{3}\partial_{z}\right)^{N-k},

(1)

where
U_{0}(z)=1
. Here,
({\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2},{\varepsilon}_{3})\in\mathbb{C}^{3}
, constrained by
{\varepsilon}_{1}+{\varepsilon}_{2}+{\varepsilon}_{3}=0
, parametrize the central charge by
c=N\left(1+(N^{2}-1)\left(\frac{{\varepsilon}_{2}}{{\varepsilon}_{1}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}_{1}}{{\varepsilon}_{2}}+2\right)\right)
.
The
J_{i}(z)
is the current of the
\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1)
vertex algebra with the operator product expansion (OPE) given by
J_{i}(z)J_{j}(w)\sim-\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}_{1}{\varepsilon}_{2}}\frac{1}{(z-w)^{2}}\delta_{i,j}
, whose mode algebra is the free boson algebra. Each factor
{\varepsilon}_{3}\partial_{z}-{\varepsilon}_{1}{\varepsilon}_{2}J_{i}(z)
on the left-hand side of (
1
) is called a
Miura operator
.

The
\mathcal{W}_{N}
-algebra arises as the vertex algebra of local operators of a 6-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=(2,0)
superconformal field theory subject to the
\Omega
-background. This is the 6-dimensional origin of the celebrated correspondence between
\mathcal{W}_{N}
-algebra conformal blocks and partition functions of 4-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=2
supersymmetric
U(N)
gauge theories
[
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
]
.

In a trivial background, the 6d theory is the low energy effective field theory on
N
M5-branes in M-theory. This prompted Costello to define a twisted version of M-theory on the
\Omega
-background
[
5
]
, where
N
parallel M5-branes are reinterpreted as a surface defect in 5-dimensional non-commutative
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory.
The
\mathcal{W}_{N}
-algebra is then viewed as the vertex algebra of local operators on the surface defect whose mode algebra acts on the Hilbert space of this surface defect theory, which is identified with the equivariant cohomology of the moduli space of
U(N)
instantons on
\mathbb{R}^{4}
. The configuration can be augmented to incorporate M5-branes oriented in different directions, extending the relevant vertex algebra to the
Y
-algebra
[
6
,
7
]
.

In this
twisted
M-theory setting, it was proposed that the Miura operator for the
\mathcal{W}_{N}
-algebra should be realized as the transverse intersection of an M2-brane and an M5-brane
[
7
]
. It was observed that this intersection point supports a fermionic zero-mode, with the classical expectation value given by the distance between the two branes in the transverse holomorphic plane. Quantum corrections are then suggested to uplift this expectation value to
{\varepsilon}_{3}\partial_{z}-{\varepsilon}_{1}{\varepsilon}_{2}J(z)
, the Miura operator for the
\mathcal{W}_{N}
-algebra. This twisted M-theory approach was further extended to include
pseudo-differential
Miura operators for more general
Y
-algebras
[
8
,
7
]
, incorporating non-transverse intersections of the M2- and M5-branes that partially overlap in their worldvolume.

Meanwhile, a definition of a multiplicative uplift of the
Y
-algebra was recently proposed in
[
9
]
, in a purely algebraic framework. In particular, it was shown that Miura operators for a
q
-deformed
Y
-algebra could be defined in terms of certain
q
-difference operators, replacing the previous differential operators.
It is tempting to guess that these Miura operators should likewise have a physical interpretation as brane intersections in twisted M-theory. One of the main goals of this work is to make this intuition precise, and prove that the algebraic Miura operators of
[
9
]
are exactly supported on M2-M5 intersections in the multiplicative uplift of the twisted M-theory background considered in
[
7
]
(up to a slight redefinition we will motivate).

We will start by reviewing the twisted M-theory and 5-dimensional non-commutative Chern-Simons theory
[
5
]
. Then, we will introduce the central idea of the present work: that the M2-M5 intersection gives rise to an
R-matrix
.

1.1
Twisted M-theory and 5-dimensional Chern-Simons theory

Let us consider the M-theory defined on the 11-dimensional worldvolume
\mathbb{R}^{7}\times\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathcal{C}
is a complex two-dimensional manifold to be chosen. Three main cases are
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
;
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
; and
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. While we will mostly focus on the last case throughout the work, it is helpful to treat them all at once here to illustrate their differences.

With the flat metric on the 11-dimensional worldvolume, there is a nilpotent supercharge
\mathcal{Q}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}={\varepsilon}_{2}=0}
, under which
\mathbb{R}^{7}
is topological and
\mathcal{C}
is holomorphic in its cohomology. Now, we further introduce the 3-form background
C^{(3)}=({\varepsilon}_{1}V_{1}^{\flat}+{\varepsilon}_{2}V_{2}^{\flat})\wedge\bar{\omega}
, where
V_{i}=\partial_{\varphi_{i}}-\partial_{\varphi_{3}}
,
i=1,2
, are the vector fields generating the rotations on the three
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-planes (
\varphi_{i}
is the angular coordinate for the
i
-th
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-plane) and
V_{i}^{\flat}
are the dual 1-forms. Also,
\bar{\omega}
is a
(0,2)
-form on
\mathcal{C}
given by the complex conjugate of the holomorphic symplectic
(2,0)
-form
\omega
,

\displaystyle\bar{\omega}=\begin{cases}d\bar{x}\wedge d\bar{z}\qquad\qquad&\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}_{x}\times\mathbb{C}_{z}\\
d\bar{x}\wedge\frac{d\bar{Z}}{\bar{Z}}&\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}_{x}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}\\
\frac{d\bar{X}}{\bar{X}}\wedge\frac{d\bar{Z}}{\bar{Z}}&\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}\end{cases},

(2)

where we indicated the holomorphic coordinates on the complex planes by subscripts. At this point, let us also write the topological part of the worldvolume as
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
, where we used the subscripts to invoke the background parameters associated to the rotations on the respective
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-planes
({\varepsilon}_{3}=-{\varepsilon}_{1}-{\varepsilon}_{2})
and
t
is the coordinate on the remaining topological line
\mathbb{R}
. In the presence of the 3-form background, the preserved supercharge gets deformed to
\mathcal{Q}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}
, which squares to the isometry

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}^{2}={\varepsilon}_{1}V_{1}+{\varepsilon}_{2}V_{2}.

(3)

The M-theory subject to the
\mathcal{Q}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}
-cohomology is called the twisted M-theory
[
5
]
.

Since the supersymmetry squares to the rotations of the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-planes, the twisted M-theory localizes to an effective 5-dimensional theory on the fixed locus
\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathcal{C}
. The localization procedure can be understood in the following way. We may continuously deform the flat
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}
to the single-centered Taub-NUT space. Reducing the M-theory along the circle fiber of the Taub-NUT space, we arrive at the IIA theory with an emergent D6-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathcal{C}
at the location of the center. The effective field theory on the worldvolume of the D6-brane is the 7-dimensional supersymmetric
U(1)
gauge theory. The role of the 3-form background
C^{(3)}
is two-fold. First, it descends to a B-field which is a mixture of the back-reaction of the emergent D6-brane and the constant B-field background. The back-reaction of the D6-brane can be subtracted by taking its asymptotics at infinity, leaving
B={\varepsilon}_{2}\bar{\omega}
. Because of this constant B-field, the usual wedge product of the differential forms in the 7d effective gauge theory is deformed to the non-commutative Moyal product defined by

\displaystyle f\star_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}g=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{{\varepsilon}_{2}^{n}}{2^{n}n!}\epsilon_{i_{1}j_{1}}\cdots\epsilon_{i_{n}j_{n}}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{i_{1}}}\cdots\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{i_{n}}}f\right)\wedge\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{j_{1}}}\cdots\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{j_{n}}}g\right),

(4)

where
\epsilon_{ij}
is the anti-symmetric symbol and
z_{1}=x
or
\log X
and
z_{2}=z
or
\log Z
depending on whether the corresponding complex plane is
\mathbb{C}
or
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Second, the 3-form also descends to a RR 3-form background which, after subtracting the one sourced by the D6-brane in the presence of the constant B-field, implements the
\Omega
-background for the 7d effective gauge theory associated with the rotation on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}
.

In total, the 7-dimensional gauge theory is localized into the 5-dimensional non-commutative
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathcal{C}
, whose action is given by

\displaystyle S=\frac{1}{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathcal{C}}\omega\wedge\left(A\star_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}dA+\frac{2}{3}A\star_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}A\star_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}A\right),

(5)

where
A
is the complex-valued gauge field with three components

\displaystyle A=A_{t}dt+A_{\bar{z}_{1}}d\bar{z}_{1}+A_{\bar{z}_{2}}d\bar{z}_{2}.

(6)

Here,
\bar{z}_{1,2}
are the anti-holomorphic coordinates on
\mathcal{C}
.

. The M5-brane

Spacetime of the twisted M-theory

The M-branes in the twisted M-theory engineer defects in the 5d Chern-Simons theory. We may introduce M2-branes, yielding topological line defects; and M5-branes, producing holomorphic surface defects (see table
1
and
2
, for our main case of
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}_{X}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}_{Z}^{\times}
).

•

The M2-branes can be supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
,
c\in\{1,2,3\}
, yielding topological line defects of the 5d Chern-Simons theory on the line
\mathbb{R}_{t}
. In the most general case, the line defect descends from
(l,m,n)
M2-branes supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
,
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
, and
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
, respectively. We call this line defect
\mathcal{L}_{l,m,n}
. The local operators on this line defect form a non-commutative associative algebra defined by their OPEs, which we denote by
\text{M2}_{l,m,n}
.

•

The M5-branes can be supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times C
,
c\in\{1,2,3\}
, where
C\subset\mathcal{C}
is a holomorphic curve. They induce holomorphic surface defects of the 5d Chern-Simons theory lying on the curve
C
. In the most general case, the surface defect descends from
(L,M,N)
M5-branes supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times C
,
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times C
, and
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times C
, respectively. We call this surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}^{C}
. The local operators on this surface defect form a chiral algebra defined by their OPEs. We denote its mode algebra by
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C}
.
2
2

2

In this work, we restrict our attention to the mode algebra of local operators on the holomorphic surface defect. In our multiplicative setting, where
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, the chiral algebra on the surface defect is anticipated to be a quantum vertex algebra. Consequently, the associated surjective map
\rho_{\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C}}
is expected to extend to a quantum vertex algebra homomorphism. See footnote
20
.

The coupling of the defect is built by applying a descent procedure to a combination of local operators on the defect and local operators of the 5d Chern-Simons theory at the locus of the defect (modes of the ghost field).
3
3

3

In fact, the descent procedure for the coupling of the surface defect requires more care, since we need to take account of the back-reaction of the M5-branes sourcing a singularity of the fields. See
[
5
]
.
The BRST-invariance of this combination requires the algebra of local operators on the defect to be a representation of a certain universal associative algebra, which we generically denote by
\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}
. Namely, there exist surjective algebra homomorphisms,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\rho_{\text{M2}_{l,m,n}}:\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}\twoheadrightarrow\text{M2}_{l,m,n},\qquad\quad\rho_{\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C}}:\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}\twoheadrightarrow\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C},\end{split}

(7)

for the topological line defect from the M2-branes and the holomorphic surface defect from the M5-branes, respectively.

The same kind of defects may lie on top of each other, defining a new defect by a fusion. The coupling of the fused defect should be built by the couplings of the individual defects before the fusion operation. Since the coupling is encoded in the representation (
7
) of the universal associative algebra carried by the defect, the fusion operation implies that there must be an algebra homomorphism,

\displaystyle\Delta:\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}\to\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}},

(8)

from which the the representation assigned to the fused defect would be reconstructed by composing
\Delta
with the respective representations assigned to the individual defects before the fusion. The completed tensor product was used so that the image may be given as a sum of infinitely many terms. This algebra homomorphism, called the coproduct, equips the universal associative algebra
\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}
with a coalgebra structure (namely,
\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}
is a bialgebra).
4
4

4

The coassociativity
(\Delta\otimes\text{id})\Delta=(\text{id}\otimes\Delta)\Delta
follows from the independence of the ordering of two consecutive fusion operations. The existence of the counit
\epsilon:\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}\to\mathbb{C}
, satisfying
(\text{id}\otimes\epsilon)\Delta=(\epsilon\otimes\text{id})\Delta=\text{id}
, is guaranteed by the existence of the trivial defect.
See section
2
for more details about the fusion operations of the line defects and the surface defects in our main case
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
.

\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
;
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
; or
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, and their representations formed by the algebra of local operators on topological line defects and holomorphic surface defects from M2- and M5-branes. Here,
\mathbf{S\ddot{H}}
is the spherical double affine Hecke algebra (spherical DAHA; see section
2.1.2
), while
\mathbf{Sr\ddot{H}}
and
\mathbf{St\ddot{H}}
are its rational and trigonometric degeneration, respectively. Also,
qY
(resp.
\hbar Y
) stands for the
q
-deformed (resp.
\hbar
-deformed)
Y
-algebra. We will discuss the
\hbar
-deformed
Y
-algebra in a separate work. We mainly focus on the case of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
(the last column) in the present work.

Since the 5d Chern-Simons theory depends on the choice of the complex manifold
\mathcal{C}
, the universal associative algebra also depends on it (see table
3
). Previous studies have primarily focused on the cases where
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
and
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}

[
5
,
10
,
11
,
12
,
7
]
. For these cases, it was shown that the universal associative algebra is the 1-shifted affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
(
Y_{1}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1))
) and the affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
(
Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1))
, respectively. Correspondingly, the algebra of local operators on the line defect from M2-branes is given by the (generalized) rational (resp. trigonometric) Calogero representation of
Y_{1}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1))
(resp.
Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1))
). The algebra of local operators on the surface defect from M5-branes was shown to be the
Y
-algebra
[
6
]
.

In this work, we examine the case where
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. We propose that the universal associative algebra in this setting is the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
, denoted by
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. We claim that the algebra of local operators
\text{M2}_{l,m,n}
on the line defect, originating from M2-branes, is given by the spherical double affine Hecke algebra (spherical DAHA) and its generalization (see section
2.1
). Furthermore, we assert that the mode algebra
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C}
of local operators on the surface defect, originating from M5-branes, is the
q
-deformed
Y
-algebra (see section
2.2
).
5
5

5

A qualitative argument for the appearance of
q
-deformed algebras is as follows: after replacing
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
with
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, there is now a circle transverse to all
\text{M5}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
-branes: the radius of this circle is a finite scale which breaks the
\mathcal{N}=(2,0)
superconformal symmetry of the 6-dimensional theory on the M5-branes; the resulting theory is known as the
\mathcal{N}=(2,0)
little string theory
[
13
,
14
]
. The conformal symmetry of the chiral algebra is likewise broken by the radius scale: when all M5-branes are parallel to each other, the conformal
\mathcal{W}
-algebra was shown to deform to a
q\mathcal{W}
-algebra
[
15
,
16
]
. In our work, we will encounter an arbitrary configuration of
\text{M5}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
-branes, with different orientations, and the associated chiral
Y
-algebra is therefore expected to deform to the
qY
-algebra.

1.2
M2-M5 intersections as R-matrices and Miura operators

In the present work, we establish the Miura transformation for the algebra of local operators on the surface defect, called the
q
-deformed
W
- and
Y
- algebras, by the fusion of the intersections between a single M2-brane and multiple M5-branes. The most crucial point is to realize the M2-M5 intersection as an R-matrix of the universal associative algebra and to utilize its properties. Since the idea penetrates the central theme of the present work, we will elucidate how the M2-M5 intersection is associated with an R-matrix here.

Consider the configuration
where M2-branes and M5-branes are present simultaneously. To develop the argument in a universal manner, we denote a generic line defect engineered by the M2-branes simply as
\mathcal{L}
, and the algebra of local operators on the line defect by
M2
. Similarly, we denote a generic surface defect engineered by the M5-branes as
\mathcal{S}
, and the mode algebra of local operators on the surface defect by
M5
. The corresponding surjective algebra homomorphisms (
7
) from the universal associative algebra
\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}
are simply called
\rho_{\mathcal{L}}
and
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
. The holomorphic support of the M5-branes are assumed to be identical, say
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
, so that they define a single surface defect in the 5d Chern-Simons theory. The intersection between the line defect
\mathcal{L}
and the surface defect
\mathcal{S}
can then be visualized as shown in Figure
1
.

R
\times
0
\mathcal{S}
\mathcal{L}
\mathcal{L}
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
\mathbb{R}_{t}

Figure 1
:
M2-M5 intersection as an R-matrix. Classically, the gauge variation of the local operator
R
(black dot) at the intersection receives linear contributions from the line defect (red dots) and the surface defect (red line). The contribution from the surface defect is decomposed into a linear combination of two: one from inward and the other from outward (blue dotted lines).

The intersection supports a space of local operators. A local operator
R
at the intersection can be built from the local operators on the line defect and the surface defect, so that it is an element of the completed tensor product

\displaystyle R\in\text{M2}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\text{M5},

(9)

subject to the gauge-invariance condition. Equivalently, we may view the local operator
R
as acting on the space
\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{F}
, where
\mathcal{V}
is the Hilbert space of the line defect worldline theory attached to the boundary at the negative infinity of
\mathbb{R}_{t}
and
\mathcal{F}
is the Hilbert space of the surface defect theory attached to the boundary at the origin of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
. The result of this action is another state in the same space attached to the positive infinity of
\mathbb{R}_{t}
and the infinity of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
, respectively. Thus, we recognize that
R\in\text{End}(\mathcal{V})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\text{End}(\mathcal{F})=\text{M2}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\text{M5}
.

The gauge variation of the local operator
R
gets contributions from both the line defect and the surface defect, which have to be canceled with each other. At the classical limit
{\varepsilon}_{1}=0
, there are only linear contributions from the two defects. These contributions can be visualized by drawing a small sphere surrounding the intersection point, picking up the gauge transformations whenever the sphere intersects the defects.

Let
g\in\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}=0,{\varepsilon}_{2}}
be the element paired with a fixed mode of the ghost field. The line defect contribute
\rho_{\mathcal{L}}(g)
from both above and below, ordered in the increasing
\mathbb{R}_{t}
-direction. The surface defect contributes by a contour integral of the generating current of the chiral algebra along the small circle around the intersection point. The contour is decomposed into the linear combination of two encircling the origin, radially ordered together with the intersection point itself. Thus, the contour integral produces two modes
\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(g)
as well, acting from the inward and the outward in the radial ordering. In total, the classical gauge-invariance condition reads

\displaystyle R\left(\rho_{\mathcal{L}}(g)\otimes\text{id}+\text{id}\otimes\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(g)\right)=\left(\rho_{\mathcal{L}}(g)\otimes\text{id}+\text{id}\otimes\rho_{\mathcal{S}}(g)\right)R,

(10)

for any
g\in\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}=0,{\varepsilon}_{2}}
.

Turning on
{\varepsilon}_{1}\neq 0
, the gauge-invariance condition gets non-linear quantum corrections. We postulate that the quantum corrections respect the algebra structure, uplifting the gauge-invariance condition to
6
6

6

In the case of
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
or
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, the support of the holomorphic surface defect
\mathcal{S}
can be chosen to be
\mathbb{C}
instead of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. In this case, we may consider the local operator
R
acting on the vacuum
|\varnothing\rangle\in\mathcal{F}
of the Hilbert space of the surface defect theory; namely,
R(1\otimes|\varnothing\rangle)
. This can be thought of as the effect of capping
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
to
\mathbb{C}
. The gauge-invariance condition then reads

\displaystyle R(1\otimes|\varnothing\rangle)\rho_{\mathcal{L}}(g)=\Delta^{\text{op}}_{\text{M2},\text{M5}}(g)R(1\otimes|\varnothing\rangle).

Up to the exchange of the two factors, this recovers the gauge-invariance condition assigned to the M2-M5 intersection in
[
7
]
. We suggest that the gauge-invariance condition should be extended to (
11
), without
having
R
act on the vacuum in particular, when the support of the holomorphic surface defect is
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

\displaystyle\boxed{R\,\Delta_{\text{M2},\text{M5}}(g)=\Delta^{\text{op}}_{\text{M2},\text{M5}}(g)R,\qquad\text{for any }g\in\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}.}

(11)

Here, we defined the
mixed
coproduct by composing the coproduct (
8
) of the universal associative algebra
\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}
and the representations (
7
) assigned to the M2- and M5-branes,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Delta_{\text{M2},\text{M5}}:=(\rho_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\rho_{\mathcal{S}})\Delta\quad:\quad\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}\to\text{M2}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\text{M5},\end{split}

(12)

and
\Delta^{\text{op}}=\sigma\circ\Delta
is the opposite coproduct obtained by composing the exchange operator
\sigma(g_{1}\otimes g_{2})=g_{2}\otimes g_{1}
.

Further, let us consider the next-to-simplest configurations involving two intersections:
(a)
one line defect
\mathcal{L}
passing through two surface defects
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
(see Figure
2
);
(b)
two line defects
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
passing through one surface defect
\mathcal{S}
(see Figure
3
).

In the first case, the two surface defects
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
, located at different points in
\mathbb{R}_{t}
, can approach each other and fuse into a new surface defect
\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
. Accordingly, the intersections between the line defect and the two surface defects undergo a fusion, implying that the local operator
R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
at the intersection between the line defect and the fused surface defect should simply be the product of the local operators
R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}}
and
R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
assigned to the individual intersections before the fusion. Namely, we have

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}\circ\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}=R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}},

(13)

where the product in the right hand side is taken as elements in the algebra
M2
of local operators on
\mathcal{L}
.

R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}}
\times

\times

Figure 2

Similarly, in the second case the two line defects
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
, located at different points in
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
, can approach each other to be fused into the a new line defect
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{L}
. The intersections also fuse correspondingly, implying that the local operator
R_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}}
supported at the fused intersection is the simple product of the local operators
R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}}
and
R_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}}
attached at the individual intersections before the fusion:

\displaystyle R_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}}=R_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}}R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}},

(14)

where the product is taken as elements in the mode algebra
M5
of the local operators on the surface defect
\mathcal{S}
.

R_{\mathcal{L},\mathcal{S}}

All in all, we discover that the local operator supported at the intersection should obey the constraint (
11
) and behave as (
13
) and (
14
) under the fusion. We claim that these are precisely the manifestation of the R-matrix properties. This is most apparent when there exists the universal R-matrix
\mathcal{R}\in\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}
of the universal associative algebra, whose defining relations are

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}\Delta(g)=\Delta^{\text{op}}(g)\mathcal{R},\qquad\text{for any }g\in\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}},

(15a)

\displaystyle(\text{id}\otimes\Delta)\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}_{13}\mathcal{R}_{12}

(15b)

\displaystyle(\Delta\otimes\text{id})\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{R}_{13}\mathcal{R}_{23}.

(15c)

It is now straightforward that (
11
) is (
15a
) composed with
\rho_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
; (
13
) is (
15b
) composed with
\rho_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\rho_{\mathcal{S}}\otimes\rho_{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}
; and (
14
) is (
15c
) composed with
\rho_{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\otimes\rho_{\mathcal{L}}\otimes\rho_{\mathcal{S}}
. In our case of
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
, we suggested that the universal associative algebra is the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
,
\mathcal{A}_{{\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2}}=U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. Following from its quantum double construction, the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
does possess the universal R-matrix (see section
3.3.1
for more detail). Let us remark that the suggested relation between the R-matrix and the M2-M5 intersection can be directly verified within the perturbation theory of the 5d non-commutative Chern-Simons theory
[
17
]
(see also
[
18
,
19
,
20
,
21
]
for other works on the perturbative study of the 5d CS theory).

As we emphasized earlier, the R-matrix properties of the M2-M5 intersections established here are key to achieving the Miura transformation for the algebra
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C}
of local operators on the most general surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}^{C}
. In particular, the axiom (
15b
) implies that the R-matrix for the intersection between the line defect
\mathcal{L}_{0,0,1}
and the surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}^{C}
is factorized into the product of
L+M+N
basic R-matrices, yielding

\displaystyle\boxed{R_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C}}=R_{L+M+N}^{(c_{L+M+N})}\cdots R_{2}^{(c_{2})}R_{1}^{(c_{1})}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{m}T_{m}(X_{1})q_{3}^{-mD_{X_{1}}}}

(16)

where in the second equality we expanded the R-matrix as a series in the difference operator
q_{3}^{-D_{X_{1}}}
, which is a generator of the algebra
\text{M2}_{0,0,1}
. In section
3
, we will verify that this expansion of the R-matrix provides the Miura transformation for the algebra
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C}
. We thereby identify the basic R-matrix associated with the intersection between a single M2-brane and a single M5-brane as the Miura operator for the
q
-deformed
Y
-algebra. Moreover, we show that the change of ordering of any two basic R-matrices results in isomorphic
q
-deformed
Y
-algebras by a direct consequence of the universal Yang-Baxter equation satisfied by the universal R-matrix.

1.3
Corner,
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-web, and gauge origami

The twisted M-theory is connected to different IIB theories through string dualities. It is illuminating to examine how the intersecting M-branes are transformed via these dualities and study their implications.

Chiral algebra at the corner

The M-theory on a toric Calabi-Yau 3-fold is dual to the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-web of fivebranes in the IIB theory, where the web diagram is given by the toric diagram
[
22
]
. Regarding
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}
as the toric Calabi-Yau 3-fold for the duality, the twisted M-theory is dual to the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-web composed of one NS5-brane, one D5-brane, and one
(1,1)
-fivebrane, all of which are supported on a semi-infinite line on a
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-plane joining at a single point (see table
4
and
5
). The M5-branes are dualized to D3-branes, filling the faces between the fivebranes and ending on them. The holomorphic part of their support remains the same holomorphic curve
C\subset\mathcal{C}
.

In the case of
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, consider
(L,M,N)
M5-branes whose supports are
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
,
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, and
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, respectively. By the above duality with a proper choice of the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
frame of the IIB theory, they are mapped to the
(L,M,N)
D3-branes filling the face between the NS5-brane and the
(1,1)
-fivebrane; between the D5-brane and the
(1,1)
-fivebrane; and between the NS5-brane and the D5-brane, respectively, while sharing
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
in common. The effective field theory on each stack is the 4d GL-twisted
\mathcal{N}=4
gauge theory with the gauge group
U(L)
,
U(M)
, and
U(N)
, respectively, making interfaces across codimension-one boundaries assigned by the fivebranes. Since the path integral of the 4d GL-twisted gauge theory with a (deformed) Neumann boundary passes to that of the analytically continued 3d Chern-Simons theory
[
23
,
24
]
, the configuration boils down to an interface between two 3d Chern-Simons theories with the gauge groups
U(N|L)
and
U(M|L)
. The local operators on the two-dimensional interface supported on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
form a chiral algebra
Y_{L,M,N}
by their OPEs. This is how the
Y
-algebra was originally constructed as the vertex algebra at the corner
[
6
]
. The M2-branes, which are dualized to 1-branes lying on top of the fivebranes, provide degenerate modules for this vertex algebra.

In the present work, instead, we mainly focus on the case of
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
. The string duality to the IIB theory operates as previously described, except for the change in the worldvolume to
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
. Let us re-examine the D3-branes supported on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
in this setting. The effective 4d theory for the D3-D3 open string now contains infinite numbers of winding modes around the circle of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}\simeq\mathbb{R}_{z}\times S^{1}
, resulting in its uplift to a 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theory compactified on a circle. We expect that the interfaces of these 5d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theories provides the multiplicative uplift of the corner vertex algebra. In this sense, it is appropriate to refer to the algebra
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C}
of local operators on the holomorphic surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}^{C}
as the
q
-deformed

Y
-algebra.
7
7

7

Even though we only restrict our attention to the mode algebra, we expect that the
q
-deformed
Y
-algebra
qY_{L,M,N}
can be endowed with a
quantum
vertex algebra structure, and the surjective map
\rho_{\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{C}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow qY_{L,M,N}
would be uplifted to a quantum vertex algebra homomorphism. In this sense,
qY_{L,M,N}
would be called the
quantum
vertex algebra at the corner. See footnote
20
.

indicates semi-infinite filling.

\mathbf{(p,q)}
-web of fivebranes and 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
half-indices

Note that the holomorphic part
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
of the 11-dimensional worldvolume contains a two-torus. The twisted M-theory on that torus is the same theory as type IIB string theory on a circle, in the twisted background.
8
8

8

Note this type IIB frame was not available in the works
[
6
,
8
,
7
]
, since the M-theory background considered there is
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
or
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

The M5-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
becomes the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebrane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times l^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}\times S^{1}
, where
l^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}=\{\mathbf{q}x-\mathbf{p}z=\text{const}\}
is a line in the two-dimensional plane
\mathbb{R}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{z}
(see table
6
and
7
). We choose the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
frame of type IIB such that
\mathbf{(1,0)}=\text{NS5}
and
\mathbf{(0,1)}=\text{D5}
. When the fivebranes share the same
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}
in their worldvolume, they can make a non-transverse trivalent intersection if the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-charge is conserved. These non-transverse intersections can be consecutively joined to form the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-web of fivebranes. The M2-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
is mapped to a D3-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times S^{1}
.

In
[
25
]
, it was observed that the web of
q
-boson representations of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
, depicted based on the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-web of fivebranes, gives a reconstruction of the partition function of the topological vertices and the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
effective gauge theory realized on the fivebrane web. The contents in section
2
and section
5
clarify the physical meaning of this algebraic formulation: the vacuum expectation value of the
web
of intersecting defects in the 5d non-commutative
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
.

The M-brane configuration of the Miura transformation, namely, a single M2-brane passing through multiple M5-branes, is translated in type IIB to a single D3-brane passing through multiple NS5-branes with different orientations. This is precisely the IIB brane engineering of a 3-dimensional supersymmetric abelian quiver gauge theory
[
26
]
. The amount of supersymmetry and the matter content depends on the orientation of the various NS5-branes relative to each other as the D3-brane goes through them. when the D3-brane and an NS5-brane are supported on a common
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\epsilon_{c}}
plane, there is 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
bifundamental hypermultiplet matter from quantizing open strings at the intersection. When the D3-brane support is totally transverse to that of an NS5-brane, the matter content is that of a 1d
\mathcal{N}=4
hypermultiplet multiplet instead.

In this type IIB frame, we will show that the various components of the Miura transformation are equal to a certain
half-index
of the 3d theory on the D3 brane.
9
9

9

It was first discovered in
[
27
,
28
]
that the half-index of 3d supersymmetric gauge theories is sometimes equal to a deformed conformal blocks of a
q\mathcal{W}
-algebra, a statement closely related to the Alday-Gaiotto-Tachikawa-Wyllard correspondence
[
1
,
2
]
. The result was later reinterpreted via the representation theory of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)

[
29
,
30
,
31
]
. Our present paper directly falls under the scope of this research program.
For us, this half-index is a count of BPS states defined in the UV, for any Lagrangian 3d
{\mathcal{N}}=2
theory
[
32
,
33
,
34
,
35
]
: one replaces
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c}}
with a finite disk/hemisphere
D^{2}
, and imposes a specific set of 1/2-BPS
\mathcal{N}=(0,2)
boundary conditions at finite distance on
S^{1}(R)\times S^{1}_{D^{2}}=T^{2}
, which will flow to a superconformal point in the IR.
10
10

10

The index is sometimes called “A-twisted”, but we do not perform the topological twist of any supercharge here.

In geometry, the half-index we compute is a generating function of quasimaps
\mathbb{CP}^{1}/\{0\}\rightarrow X
of all degrees in equivariant quantum K-theory, where
X
is the Higgs branch of the 3d theory
[
36
,
37
,
38
,
39
]
; by excising the origin of
\mathbb{CP}^{1}
, we are allowing for line operator insertions supported on
\{0\}\times S^{1}
to contribute to the quasimap count (see for instance
[
40
,
41
]
); these are the 1-dimensional degrees of freedom supported at a totally transverse NS5-D3 brane intersection. In section
4
, we find:

Each component of the Miura transformation is the half-index of a 3d supersymmetric abelian (quiver) gauge theory on
D^{2}\times S^{1}
, possibly coupled to 1d line defects.

The Miura transformation admits an integral representation, whose components are recovered from the index by imposing Neumann boundary conditions for the vector multiplets and a fixed polarization for the hypermultiplets (a specific holomorphic Lagrangian splitting of the
\mathcal{N}=4
hypermultiplets). The residue sum of this Miura transformation corresponds to giving Dirichlet boundary conditions to the vector multiplets and hypermultiplets.

The fact that we are able to recover the components of the Miura transformation straight from a type IIB computation reinforces the validity of the duality we propose here.

Gauge origami and
qq
-characters

It was suggested in
[
42
]
that the IIB theory for the gauge origami
[
43
,
44
]
is also connected to the twisted M-theory by a string duality, up to certain unrefinement of the background. The gauge origami is the configuration of intersecting D3-branes in the IIB theory defined on
X\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
. The
\Omega
-background can be implemented with respect to the
U(1)^{3}\subset SU(4)
isometry of the Calabi-Yau 4-fold
X
, parametrized by the four
\Omega
-background parameters
({\varepsilon}_{1},{\varepsilon}_{2},{\varepsilon}_{3},{\varepsilon}_{4})
constrained by
{\varepsilon}_{1}+{\varepsilon}_{2}+{\varepsilon}_{3}+{\varepsilon}_{4}=0
. The D3-branes occupy real four-cycles in
X
which are invariant under the isometry, located at certain points on the transverse holomorphic plane
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
.

We set
X=\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{4}})/\mathbb{Z}_{l}
and deform the
A_{l-1}
-singularity into the
l
-centered Taub-NUT space (see table
8
and
9
). The subscript of each
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-plane denotes the
\Omega
-background parameter associated to its rotation. By unrefining the
\Omega
-background, the T-duality along the Taub-NUT circle can be performed in the simplest manner, yielding the IIA theory defined on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times S^{1}
(
{\varepsilon}_{1}+{\varepsilon}_{2}+{\varepsilon}_{3}=0
) with
l
NS5-branes supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
. We may further decompactify the circle
S^{1}
to a line
\mathbb{R}_{z}
, making one NS5-brane disappear to
infinity
in
\mathbb{R}_{z}
, and uplift to M-theory with the M-theory circle
S^{1}_{M}
, which combines into another holomorphic plane
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}

[
45
]
. In this way, we arrive at the twisted M-theory on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
.

Let us examine how two different types of D3-branes in the IIB theory of the gauge origami are transformed under the duality. The first type is the ones supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}
, each of which is assigned a
\mathbb{Z}_{l}
-charge. After the T-duality, they are mapped to the D4-branes stretched between the newly created NS5-branes in the
\mathbb{R}_{z}
-direction. The number of D4-branes between each consecutive pair of NS5-branes is determined by their
\mathbb{Z}_{l}
-charge assignment
[
46
]
. This is precisely the IIA engineering of the 5-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=1
quiver gauge theory compactified on a circle, as the effective field theory obtained from quantizing the D4-D4 open strings. Both the D4-branes and the NS5-branes become M5-branes in the twisted M-theory, sharing
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}
in their worldvolume but wrapping different holomorphic plane,
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
and
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
, respectively. By their non-transverse intersections on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
, they form a
web
of M5-branes.

The second type is supported on
(\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{4}})/\mathbb{Z}_{l}
, for which we also assign a
\mathbb{Z}_{l}
-charge. Equivalently, the D3-brane can wrap different compact
\mathbb{P}^{1}
’s after resolving the
A_{l-1}
singularity into the
l
-centered Taub-NUT space, where the choice is encoded in the
\mathbb{Z}_{l}
-charge
c\in\{0,1,\cdots,l-1\}
. Performing the T-duality, this D3-brane becomes a D2-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
, sharing the
\mathbb{R}_{z}
-coordinate with one of the NS5-branes (
l
NS5s before the decompactification or
l-1
NS5s after the decompactification), determined by the
\mathbb{Z}_{l}
-charge. At last, under the uplift to the twisted M-theory, the D2-brane becomes an M2-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
. Note that the resulting M2-brane is totally transverse to the M5-branes that we obtained above in the 11-dimensional worldvolume.

Recall that the crossed D3-brane configuration in the gauge origami involves both types of D3-branes present simultaneously. In particular, due to their transverse intersection, the second type of D3-brane provides a codimension-4 defect lying along the circle in the 5-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=1
effective gauge theory from the first type, referred to as the
qq
-characters

[
43
]
.
11
11

11

In more detail, by
qq
-character, we mean the gauge and string theoretical realization of the generating currents of the
q\mathcal{W}
-algebra (and by extension those of the
qY
-algebra as well), originally constructed algebraically by Frenkel and Reshetikhin
[
47
]
(see also
[
48
,
49
,
50
,
51
]
), and revisited recently in a wide variety of examples

[
52
,
53
,
54
,
55
,
56
,
57
]
. The relevance of
qq
-characters to the representation theory of quantum toroidal algebras was studied in
[
58
,
59
,
60
,
9
]
.
In the dual IIB theory for the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebrane web on the
\mathbb{R}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{z}
plane (see table
6
), the crossed D3-brane is mapped to a D3-brane at a point on that plane.
12
12

12

It has been suggested that
qq
-characters could be realized via
different
brane defects in a
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebrane web
[
61
,
62
]
. Unfortunately, none of these proposals coincide with the original stringy crossed instanton definition in
[
43
]
, nor are they obviously dual to it. Our construction of the
qq
-characters from transverse M2-branes is directly dual to the original construction, as explained above.
Physically, the
qq
-character is the BPS count of this brane system; equivalently, it is the Witten index of the supersymmetric quantum mechanics on instantonic D1-branes wrapping the circle, in the presence of the heavier branes
[
63
,
64
,
65
,
66
,
67
,
68
,
69
]
. Mathematically, this index is defined as the (properly regularized) integral over the crossed instanton moduli space of the generalized gauge theory, evaluated in equivariant K-theory.

We just analyzed that, in the twisted M-theory, the corresponding M-brane configuration is a single M2-brane placed upon a transverse web of M5-branes, enumerated by
c=1,2,\cdots,l-1
, which determines the M5-brane that it shares the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
-coordinate with. As we discussed earlier, the M2-M5 intersection assigns an R-matrix of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
(i.e., the Miura operator by our result in section
3
). In section
5
, we show that:

The insertion of the R-matrix associated with the M2-M5 intersection on the M5-brane web of
q
-bosons exactly reproduces the
qq
-characters.

This explicitly confirms the duality we suggest here.

Already back in the 90’s
[
47
]
, it was appreciated that
qq
-characters are traces of transfer matrices for certain TQ-integrable systems. Here, we are showing that the generating function of all the
qq
-characters is a product of R-matrices of the quantum toroidal algebra, making the integrability even more manifest. See
[
52
,
54
,
70
,
71
,
72
,
73
,
74
,
42
,
75
,
76
,
77
]
for studies of the quantum integrability using the
qq
-characters as gauge theory observables. See also
[
29
,
64
,
78
,
79
,
80
,
81
,
82
,
66
,
67
,
68
,
69
,
83
,
84
,
85
]
for related studies on the
qq
-characters.

1.4
Outline

In section
2
, we identify the algebra of local operators on the line defects and the surface defects of 5d Chern-Simons theory from the M2-branes and the M5-branes, respectively, and realize them as representations of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
. In section
3
, we solve the R-matrix between the representations assigned to a single M2-brane and a single M5-brane. We construct the Miura transformation for the
q
-deformed
Y
-algebra using the R-matrix properties of the multiple M2-M5 intersections. We also obtain the screening charges from M2-branes placed between each consecutive M5-branes, and verify that the
q
-deformed
Y
-algebra is exactly characterized as the commutants of these screening charges. In section
4
, we show that the half-index of the 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
quiver
U(1)
gauge theory with 1d defect, engineered as the effective field theory on the D3-brane passing through multiple NS5-branes, exactly matches with the components of the Miura transformations. In particular, the relevant boundary conditions are exactly identified. In section
5
, we present different incarnations of the
q
-boson representations of the quantum toroidal algebra associated to the M5-branes supported on general holomorphic curves. It is shown that their non-transverse intersections lead to the intertwiners between those
q
-boson representations. We consider the configuration where a single M2-brane passes through a web of M5-branes, and verify that the insertion of the R-matrix associated to the M2-M5 intersection precisely yields the
qq
-characters of the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
effective gauge theory realized on the web of M5-branes. We conclude with discussions in section
6
. The appendices contain generalities about the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
and the spherical double affine Hecke algebra; computational details for solving the R-matrices and the screening charges; and details of the
q
-boson intertwiners of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
.

The authors are grateful to the organizers of Simons Physics Summer Workshop 2023, where the collaboration was initiated.
