The work of SJ is supported by the Institute for Basic Science under the project IBS-R003-Y3. This work was also partly supported by CERN and CKC fellowship.

2
M-branes and representations of quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)

The twisted M-theory subject to the
\Omega
-background reduces to the 5-dimensional non-commutative Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}

[
5
]
. The M2-branes and the M5-branes descend to topological line defects and holomorphic surface defects, respectively. The BRST-invariance of the coupling of these defects require the algebra of local operators on the defects to be representations of certain universal associative algebra. In this section, we suggest this universal associative algebra is the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
,
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
, for our case. We also specify the representations of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
associated to the M2-branes and the M5-branes.

2.1
M2-branes and topological line defects

The M2-branes in the twisted M-theory can be supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
, where
c\in\{1,2,3\}
. Once reduced to the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory, they give rise to line defects lying along
\mathbb{R}_{t}
, located at points on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
. Let us focus on the case where all the M2-branes are located at a single point on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
, giving a single line defect. In the most general case, there are
l
,
m
, and
n
M2-branes wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
,
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
, and
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
, respectively. We call the resulting line defect
\mathcal{L}_{l,m,n}
. Let us define
q_{c}=e^{{\varepsilon}_{c}}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
,
c\in\{1,2,3\}
.

The local observables of the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory localized on the line
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\{\text{point}\}
form an algebra
\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}
, with the product defined by their OPEs. At the classical level (
q_{1}=1
), the algebra
\text{Obs}_{q_{1}=1,q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}
has a simple description; the solutions to the equations of motion is trivial, and the only local observables are the functions of the modes of the ghost under the Laurent expansion around the locus of the line in
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
, for which the holomorphic coordinates
X
and
Z
are subject to the non-commutativity relation

\displaystyle XZ=q_{2}ZX,

(17)

due to the B-field
B={\varepsilon}_{2}\frac{d\bar{X}}{\bar{X}}\wedge\frac{d\bar{Z}}{\bar{Z}}
.

The Laurent expansion
c=\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}c_{m,n}Z^{m}X^{n}
indicates that the gauge Lie algebra is generated by
W_{m,n}\equiv Z^{m}X^{n}
, satisfying the commutation relations
\left[W_{m,n},W_{m^{\prime},n^{\prime}}\right]=\left(q_{2}^{m^{\prime}n}-q_{2}^{mn^{\prime}}\right)W_{m+m^{\prime},n+n^{\prime}}
. This is precisely the quantum torus Lie algebra (see e.g.
[
86
]
), which we denote by
\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times})
. Note that its universal enveloping algebra is the quantum torus algebra,
U(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}))=\mathbb{C}\left\langle X^{\pm 1},Z^{\pm 1}\right\rangle/(XZ=q_{2}ZX)
. Equipped with the BRST differential, the functions of the ghost thus form the Chevalley-Eilenberg cochain complex,

\displaystyle\text{Obs}_{q_{1}=1,q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}=C^{\bullet}(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times})).

(18)

A line defect of the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory can be defined by coupling a topological quantum mechanics, whose algebra of local observables is, say,
\mathcal{B}
. Explicitly, the line defect coupling is implemented by inserting the generalized Wilson line,

\displaystyle\text{Pexp}\sum_{m,n\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{t}}dt\,P_{m,n}(A_{t})_{m,n},

(19)

into the path integral, where
(A_{t})_{m,n}
are the modes of the gauge field under the Laurent expansion in
Z
and
X
, and
P_{m,n}\in\mathcal{B}
are the local operators on the line defect. The BRST-invariance of the coupling is guaranteed if the coupling is the topological descent of a Maurer-Cartan element in
\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}\otimes\mathcal{B}
. This is, by the Koszul duality, equivalent to requiring that there is a surjective algebra homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{q_{1},q_{2}}:=\left(\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}\right)^{!}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{B},

(20)

where
\mathcal{A}_{q_{1},q_{2}}=\left(\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}\right)^{!}
is the Koszul dual of
\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}
. In this sense, the Koszul dual algebra
\mathcal{A}_{q_{1},q_{2}}
can be said to be the algebra of local observables on the
universal
line defect. It is called the universal associative algebra in short.

In the classical limit
q_{1}=1
, the standard result tells that the Koszul dual of the operator algebra (
18
) is (see
[
87
]
for a review)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{q_{1}=1,q_{2}}=\left(\text{Obs}_{q_{1}=1,q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}\right)^{!}=U(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times})).

(21)

Turning on
q_{1}\neq 1
, the quantum corrections deform the operator algebra
\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}
, and therefore its Koszul dual
\mathcal{A}_{q_{1},q_{2}}=(\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}})^{!}
, in a non-trivial way. In particular, the Koszul dual would be a deformation of the quantum torus algebra (
21
), which we can formally denote by

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{q_{1},q_{2}}=U_{q_{1}}(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times})).

(22)

The quantum corrections for
\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{\mathbb{R}_{t}}
can in principle be determined by computing the Feynman diagrams of the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
relevant to the operator products of the mode (Laurent) expansions of the ghost fields.
13
13

13

See
[
10
]
for the explicit evaluation of the Feynman diagrams relevant to the quantum corrections to the operator algebra in the case of the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(k)
Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
. In this case, the operator algebra is an
{\varepsilon}_{1}
-deformation of
C^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{gl}(k)\otimes\mathscr{O}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{2}))
, and its Koszul dual, i.e., the universal associative algebra, is proven to be the 1-shifted affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}(k)
,
U_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}(\mathfrak{gl}(k)\otimes\mathscr{O}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{2}))=Y_{1}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(k))
.
Also, the defining relations of the universal associative algebra can in principle be achieved as conditions for the anomalous Feynman diagrams to cancel each other (see
[
88
,
10
,
19
]
). We do not take such a direct approach of the perturbation theory in the present work.

Rather, we will make a natural suggestion for the universal associative algebra
\mathcal{A}_{q_{1},q_{2}}
, and assign appropriate representations to the topological line defects
\mathcal{L}_{l,m,n}
created by the M2-branes. Our proposal is that the universal associative algebra
\mathcal{A}_{q_{1},q_{2}}
is precisely the quantum toroidal algebra of

\mathfrak{gl}(1)
, namely,
\mathcal{A}_{q_{1},q_{2}}=U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
.
14
14

14

Recall that
q_{3}=q_{1}^{-1}q_{2}^{-1}
so that we could have omitted the dependence on
q_{3}
and write
U_{q_{1},q_{2}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. We write
q_{3}
explicitly only to emphasize the triality of exchanging
q_{1}
,
q_{2}
, and
q_{3}
with each other.
We review the definition and the relevant properties of the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
in appendix
A
. In the present work, we will provide evidences for our claim, leaving the exact proof of the statement through perturbative study of 5d non-commutative
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
to future work.

2.1.1
Single M2-brane and quantum torus algebra

Let us begin with a single M2-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
. In the IIB dual frame, the M2-brane passes to a fundamental string attached to the NS5-brane, ending at the junction of the fivebranes. In the worldvolume theory of the D5-brane, the fundamental string provides a boundary Wilson line of charge 1
[
11
]
. The position of the Wilson line along the junction of the fivebranes, which we denote by
(X_{1},Z_{1})\in\mathbb{C}_{X}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}_{Z}^{\times}
, are subject to the relation

\displaystyle X_{1}Z_{1}=q_{2}Z_{1}X_{1}

(23)

by the non-commutativity (
17
). They can be thought of as the generators of the algebra
\text{M2}_{0,1,0}
of local operators on this Wilson line, which is nothing but the quantum torus algebra. Namely, we have
\text{M2}_{0,1,0}=U(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}))
.

The quantum torus algebra is a representation of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
. Indeed, there is a surjective algebra homomorphism

\displaystyle\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,1,0}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M2}_{0,1,0}

(24)

given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E(X)\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{2}}\delta\left(\frac{X_{1}}{X}\right)Z_{1},\quad F(X)\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{-1}}Z_{1}^{-1}\delta\left(\frac{X_{1}}{X}\right)\\
&K^{\pm}(X)\mapsto\frac{(1-(q_{1}^{-1}X_{1}/X)^{\pm 1})(1-(q_{3}^{-1}X_{1}/X)^{\pm 1})}{(1-(X_{1}/X)^{\pm 1})(1-(q_{2}X_{1}/X)^{\pm 1})}\\
&C\mapsto 1.\end{split}

(25)

Note
(C,C^{\perp})\mapsto(1,1)
in particular. The map
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,1,0}}
can be written equivalently in terms of the modes as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{2}}X_{1}^{k}Z_{1},\quad F_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{-1}}Z_{1}^{-1}X_{1}^{k},\qquad k\in\mathbb{Z},\\
&H_{\pm r}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{\mp r}}X_{1}^{\pm r},\qquad r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\\
&C\mapsto 1,\quad C^{\perp}\mapsto 1.\end{split}

(26)

In particular, the homomorphism in fact does not depend on
q_{1}
.

For any given weight parameter
u\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, there is a faithful representation of the quantum torus algebra
\text{M2}_{0,1,0}
on the vector space
\mathcal{V}_{2}(u)=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}[u]_{i}^{(2)}
, defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}Z_{1}[u]_{i}^{(2)}=[u]_{i+1}^{(2)},\quad\quad X_{1}[u]_{i}^{(2)}=uq_{2}^{i}[u]_{i}^{(2)}.\end{split}

(27)

More concretely, the representation can be thought of as the
q_{2}
-difference operators

\displaystyle Z_{1}=q_{2}^{-D_{X_{1}}},\qquad X_{1}=X_{1}

(28)

acting on the space of Laurent polynomials

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{2}(u)=u^{-D_{X_{1}}}\mathbb{C}\left(\!\left(q_{2}^{-D_{X_{1}}}\right)\!\right)=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}q_{2}^{-\left(\frac{\log u}{\log q_{2}}+i\right)D_{X_{1}}}.

(29)

Here, we used the notation
D_{X_{1}}=X_{1}\partial_{X_{1}}
so that
q_{2}^{-D_{X_{1}}}=q_{2}^{-X_{1}\partial_{X_{1}}}
is the
q_{2}^{-1}
-shift operator for
X_{1}
(i.e.,
q_{2}^{-D_{X_{1}}}f(X_{1})=f(q_{2}^{-1}X_{1})q_{2}^{-D_{X_{1}}}
for any function
f(X_{1})
). This is precisely what is called the
vector
representation of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
(cf.
[
89
]
), factored through the quantum torus algebra. Namely, what we obtained can be summarized as
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M2}_{0,1,0}\hookrightarrow\text{End}(\mathcal{V}_{2}(u))
.

For later use, note that the grading operator
d
(see section
A.3
) acts on the generators as

\displaystyle q^{d}X_{1}=qX_{1}q^{d}\qquad q^{d}Z_{1}=Z_{1}q^{d},

(30)

for any
q\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. It follows that the representation on
\mathcal{V}_{2}
can be extended to incorporate the grading operator by regarding it as the shift operator for the module parameter:
q^{d}[u]^{(2)}_{i}=[uq^{-1}]_{i}^{(2)}
.

As stressed above, the homomorphism
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,1,0}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to\text{M2}_{0,1,0}
is not affected by tuning of the value of
q_{1}
. In this sense, the quantum torus algebra, as a representation of the quantum toroidal algebra, does not deform from
q_{1}=1
, and it is expected to still provide the M2-brane algebra with all the quantum corrections taken into account. Such a rigidity is indeed anticipated from the boundary Wilson line description for the single M2-brane.

Now, let us turn to the single M2-brane wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
(resp.
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
). These M2-branes can simply be obtained by shuffling the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-planes in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}
, which is equivalent to exchanging the parameters
q_{1}
,
q_{2}
, and
q_{3}
with each other. Therefore, the algebra of local operators on this M2-brane is expected to be the quantum torus algebra
\text{M2}_{1,0,0}=U(\mathscr{O}_{q_{1}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}))
(resp.
\text{M2}_{0,0,1}=U(\mathscr{O}_{q_{3}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}))
) with the non-commutativity of the generators controlled by
q_{1}
(resp.
q_{3}
) instead of
q_{2}
.

Indeed, as apparent from its defining relations, the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
is invariant under the triality of permuting
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
. The algebra homomorphisms to the other two quantum torus algebras thus exist, obtained simply by replacing
q_{2}
by
q_{1}
or
q_{3}
in the map
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,1,0}}
(
26
). We denote these algebra maps by
\rho_{\text{M2}_{1,0,0}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to\text{M2}_{1,0,0}
and
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to\text{M2}_{0,0,1}
, respectively. We can define faithful representations of these quantum torus algebras on the vector spaces
\mathcal{V}_{c}(u)=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}[u]_{i}^{(c)}
,
c\in\{1,2,3\}
, simply by permuting
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
on (
27
).

2.1.2
Parallel M2-branes and spherical DAHA

Next, we examine multiple M2-branes wrapping the same
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
,
c\in\{1,2,3\}
. We will explain that the two cases,
c=1
and
2
, independently lead to the same conclusion that the algebra of local operators on the parallel M2-branes is given by the spherical subalgebra of the double affine Hecke algebra (spherical DAHA). Then, we will show that the fusion of the parallel M2-branes is compatible with the coproduct of the quantum toroidal algebra.

M2-branes on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
and boundary Wilson lines

As studied in the previous section, the
m
M2-branes supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
reduce to the
m
boundary Wilson lines on the worldvolume theory of the D5-brane. We denote the algebra of local operators on these M2-branes by
\text{M2}_{0,m,0}
.

Let
(X_{i},Z_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
be the positions of the
m
Wilson lines on the holomorphic surfaces
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
. Just as the case of single M2-brane, these holomorphic coordinates are the generators of the algebra of local operators, subject to the non-commutativity (
17
). In the classical limit
q_{1}=1
, there is no quantum correction coming from the interaction between these Wilson lines. The algebra of local operators is thus simply given by the symmetric product of the
m
quantum torus algebras,
\text{M2}_{0,m,0}^{q_{1}=1}=U(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}\left(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}\right))^{\otimes m}/S_{m}
, each of which is from individual Wilson line. As discussed earlier, the universal associative algebra at
q_{1}=1
is the quantum torus algebra (
21
), and there is indeed a natural homomorphism

\displaystyle U(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}))\twoheadrightarrow\frac{U(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}\left(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}\right))^{\otimes m}}{S_{m}},

(31)

which encodes the coupling of the line defect in the classical limit
q_{1}=1
.

Turning on
q_{1}\neq 1
, we suggested that the universal associative algebra gets deformed to the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
,
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))=U_{q_{1}}(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}))
. The algebra
\text{M2}_{0,m,0}
of local operators on the line defect should also be a
q_{1}
-deformation of
\text{M2}_{0,m,0}^{q_{1}=1}=U(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}))^{\otimes m}/S_{m}
, and the coupling of the line defect would be encoded in a
q_{1}
-deformation of the surjective homomorphism (
31
), which we may write as

\displaystyle\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m,0}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M2}_{0,m,0}.

(32)

There is indeed a natural
q_{1}
-deformation of the symmetric product of quantum torus algebras: the spherical subalgebra of the double affine Hecke algebra (spherical DAHA) of
GL(m)
, which we denote by
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},q_{1}}
. The spherical DAHA can be presented as the algebra generated by the difference operators,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&P_{0,r}^{(m)}=q_{2}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}^{r},\qquad P_{0,-r}^{(m)}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}^{-r},\qquad r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\\
\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}&P_{1,k}^{(m)}=q_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\prod_{j\neq i}\frac{q_{1}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)X_{i}^{k}q_{2}^{-D_{X_{i}}}\\
&P_{-1,k}^{(m)}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\prod_{j\neq i}\frac{q_{1}^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)q_{2}^{D_{X_{i}}}X_{i}^{k}\end{split},\qquad k\in\mathbb{Z},\end{split}

(33)

acting on the space of symmetric polynomials
\mathbb{C}(q_{1},q_{2})[X_{1}^{\pm 1},\cdots,X_{m}^{\pm 1}]^{S_{m}}
. We review the definition and the relevant properties of the spherical DAHA of
GL(m)
in appendix
B
.

Correspondingly, the algebra homomorphism (
31
) admits a
q_{1}
-deformation,

\displaystyle U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},q_{1}},

(34)

given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{q_{2}(1-q_{2})}P_{1,k}^{(m)}=\frac{1}{1-q_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\prod_{j\neq i}\frac{q_{1}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)X_{i}^{k}q_{2}^{-D_{X_{i}}},\\
&F_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{-1}}P_{-1,k}^{(m)}=\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{-1}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\prod_{j\neq i}\frac{q_{1}^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)q_{2}^{D_{X_{i}}}X_{i}^{k},\\
\end{split}\qquad k\in\mathbb{Z},\\
\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}&H_{\pm r}\mapsto\pm\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{r}}P_{0,\pm r}^{(m)}=\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{\mp r}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}^{\pm r},\qquad r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\\
&C\mapsto 1,\quad C^{\perp}\mapsto 1.\end{split}\end{split}

(35)

This is a surjective algebra homomorphism for each
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}

[
90
]
. Note that in the classical limit
q_{1}=1
, the spherical DAHA simplifies to the symmetric product
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},1}=U\left(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times})\right)^{\otimes m}/{S_{m}}
and the algebra homomorphism reduces to (
31
), as promised (compare with (
26
)). At the same time, for
m=1
we simply recover the algebra map (
26
) for the single M2-brane,
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,1,0}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to\text{M2}_{0,1,0}
, which is rigid under the
q_{1}
-deformation. Therefore, we suggest that the algebra of local operators on the parallel
m
M2-branes wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
is given by the spherical DAHA,
\text{M2}_{0,m,0}=\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},q_{1}}
, and the algebra map (
34
) is nothing but the representation
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m,0}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to\text{M2}_{0,m,0}
that determines the coupling of the associated line defect
\mathcal{L}_{0,m,0}
.

Recall that the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
is invariant under the triality of permuting
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
. As explicit from (
35
), the spherical DAHA
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},q_{1}}
as a representation of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
partially breaks this triality. However, the symmetry of exchanging
q_{1}
and
q_{3}(=q_{1}^{-1}q_{2}^{-1})
remains to be unbroken. Indeed, the relations imposed on the generators of the spherical DAHA
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},q_{1}}
are explicitly symmetric under the exchange of
q_{1}
and
q_{3}
, while
q_{2}
plays a distinct role. Moreover, at the level of its representation in
q_{2}
-difference operators (
35
), it is straightforward to show that the conjugation by

\displaystyle g(X_{1},X_{2}\cdots,X_{m})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}^{\frac{(m-i)\log\frac{q_{3}}{q_{1}}}{\log q_{2}}}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq m}\frac{\left(q_{3}^{-1}\frac{X_{j}}{X_{i}};q_{2}\right)_{\infty}}{\left(q_{1}^{-1}\frac{X_{j}}{X_{i}};q_{2}\right)_{\infty}}

(36)

yields exactly the same
q_{2}
-difference operators except that
q_{1}
is replaced by
q_{3}
, due to the relation
q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}=1
. Thus, the conjugation establishes the isomorphism
\text{M2}_{0,m,0}=\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},q_{1}}\simeq\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},q_{3}}
. Note that such a breaking pattern of the triality is precisely what we would expect from the associated brane configuration in the twisted M-theory, since the support of the M2-branes distinguishes
q_{2}
out of the triple
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
but still there is a remaining symmetry of exchanging the two transverse topological planes
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}
.

M2-branes on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
and Hilbert scheme of points in
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}

So far, we have investigated the algebra of local operators on the parallel M2-branes supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
. To study parallel M2-branes, we can alternatively take a stack of
l
M2-branes wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
. A crucial point of this consideration is the following. On one hand, such a change merely amounts to swapping the topological planes
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}
in the twisted M-theory setup compared to the previous case, so that the algebra of local operators should be again given by the spherical DAHA of
GL(l)
, albeit with the roles of
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
exchanged:
\text{M2}_{l,0,0}=\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(l)}_{q_{1},q_{2}}(\simeq\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(l)}_{q_{1},q_{3}})
. On the other hand, the IIA reduction of the twisted M-theory seemingly breaks the triality, so that the emergence of the spherical DAHAs, with the
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
exchanged, from the two different parallel M2-branes is a non-trivial consistency check.
15
15

15

When the holomorphic surfaces are
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
, the appearance of the spherical rational DAHA
\mathbf{Sr\ddot{H}}
from two different parallel M2-branes, with the roles of
{\varepsilon}_{1}
and
{\varepsilon}_{2}
swapped,
was discussed in
[
11
]
. What we discuss here is the multiplicative uplift of this manifestation of the triality, obtained by replacing the holomorphic surfaces
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
by
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

Reducing the twisted M-theory along the Taub-NUT circle, we arrive at a configuration of
l
D2-brane wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
lying on top of
1
D6-brane wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
. The effective 3-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=4
theory is obtained by quantizing the D2-D2 open string and the D2-D6 open string, where the former contains infinite numbers of winding modes around the two circles of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
. The Higgs branch of this 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
theory is then given by the Hilbert scheme of
l
points on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, which can be constructed as the multiplicative quiver variety for the Jordan quiver (Figure
4
)
[
91
,
92
,
93
,
94
]
.

1
l

Figure 4
:
Jordan quiver

The multiplicative quiver variety
\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}
for the Jordan quiver is constructed as follows. Let us consider the space of quadruples
(A,B,I,J)\in\text{End}(\mathbb{C}^{l})\times\text{End}(\mathbb{C}^{l})\times\text{Hom}(\mathbb{C},\mathbb{C}^{l})\times\text{Hom}(\mathbb{C}^{l},\mathbb{C})
satisfying the relation,

\displaystyle AB-q_{2}BA+IJ=0\qquad(\text{equivalently,}\;\;\text{rk}(AB-q_{2}BA)=1).

(37)

There is a natural
GL(l)
-action on this space given by,

\displaystyle(A,B,I,J)\mapsto(gAg^{-1},gBg^{-1},gI,Jg^{-1}),\qquad g\in GL(l).

(38)

It is known that the action is free for generic
q_{2}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, so that the quotient is a smooth variety
[
93
]
. The multiplicative quiver variety
\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}
for the Jordan quiver is given by this quotient. It is straightforward to see
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}=2l
.

The multiplicative quiver variety
\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}
is equipped with a Poisson structure
[
95
,
96
]
, which furnishes a complete integrable structure associated to the classical trigonometric Ruijsenaars-Schneider model (the relativistic uplift of the trigonometric Calogero-Moser model) of
GL(l)
-type. The precise definition of the Poisson structure will not be very important to us, and we only present it here by giving the associated Darboux coordinates on an open dense subset
\left(\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}\right)^{\circ}=\{A\text{ is invertible}\}\subset\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}
, following
[
96
]
. Let us define

\displaystyle(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{l}_{\text{reg}}=\{(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{l})\in(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{l}\;|\;x_{i}\neq x_{j},\;x_{i}\neq q_{2}x_{j}\,\text{ for any }i,j=1,2,\cdots,l\}.

(39)

Then we may parameterize
\left(\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}\right)^{\circ}
by the map

\displaystyle\xi:\left((\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{l}_{\text{reg}}\times(\mathbb{C}^{\times})^{l}\right)/S_{l}\longrightarrow\left(\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}\right)^{\circ},

(40)

defined by
\xi\left((x_{i})_{i=1}^{l},(p_{i})_{i=1}^{l}\right)=(A,B)
with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&A=\text{diag}(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{l})\\
&B=(B_{ij})_{i,j=1}^{l},\quad B_{ij}=p_{j}^{-1}\frac{x_{j}(1-q_{2})}{x_{i}-q_{2}x_{j}}\prod_{k\neq j}\frac{q_{2}x_{j}-x_{k}}{x_{j}-x_{k}}.\end{split}

(41)

Here,
S_{l}
acts by the simultaneous permutation of
(x_{i})_{i=1}^{l}
and
(p_{i})_{i=1}^{l}
. When
\left((x_{i})_{i=1}^{l},(p_{i})_{i=1}^{l}\right)
gets permuted, the image
(A,B)
is conjugated by the matrix of that permutation. Thus, the coordinates are well-defined on
\left(\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}\right)^{\circ}
. This is a Darboux coordinate system on
\left(\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}\right)^{\circ}
, in the sense that their Poisson brackets are given by

\displaystyle\{x_{i},x_{j}\}=\{p_{i},p_{j}\}=0,\quad\{p_{i},x_{j}\}=p_{i}x_{i}\delta_{i,j},\quad i,j=1,2,\cdots,l.

(42)

It is straightforward from (
41
) and (
42
) that the trace invariants
\text{Tr}\,A^{k}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, are mutually Poisson-commuting. It can also be shown that
\text{Tr}\,B^{k}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, are mutually Poisson-commuting. By taking suitable linear combinations of the products of
\text{Tr}\,B^{-k}
with
k=1,2,\cdots,l
, we obtain

\displaystyle H_{r}=q_{2}^{-\frac{r(r-1)}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\{1,2,\cdots,l\}\\
|I|=r\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in I\\
j\notin I\end{subarray}}\frac{q_{2}^{-1}x_{i}-x_{j}}{x_{i}-x_{j}}\right)\prod_{i\in I}p_{i},\qquad r=1,2,\cdots,l,

(43)

which in turn also Poisson-commute with each other,
\{H_{r},H_{s}\}=0
,
r,s=1,2,\cdots,l
. They are precisely the classical Hamiltonians of the trigonometric Ruijsenaars-Schneider model of
GL(l)
-type.

In the classical limit
q_{1}=1
, the effective field theory on the worldvolume of the M2-branes is the 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
theory subject to the Rozansky-Witten twist, which flows to the 3d topological sigma model with the target space being the Higgs branch
\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}

[
97
]
. The algebra
\text{M2}_{l,0,0}^{q_{1}=1}
of local operators of this theory is thus the commutative algebra of holomorphic functions on the space
\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}
. It was shown in
[
93
]
that this algebra is identical to
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(l)}_{1,q_{2}}
, namely, the
q_{1}=1
limit of the spherical DAHA
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(l)}_{q_{1},q_{2}}
. Note that this limit is different from the other limit to the symmetric product of quantum torus algebras we observed earlier, due to the crucial exchange of the
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
parameters.

Turning on
q_{1}\neq 1
, the Rozansky-Witten twisted 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
theory on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
gets
\Omega
-deformed, localized onto the line
\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
. The local operators then form a non-commutative associative algebra
\text{M2}_{l,0,0}
, which is a deformation of the algebra of functions on
\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}

[
98
]
. The quantization takes place in the way that the canonical Poisson brackets (
42
) are uplifted to the canonical commutation relations,

\displaystyle[X_{i},X_{j}]=\left[q_{1}^{D_{X_{i}}},q_{1}^{D_{X_{j}}}\right]=0,\qquad q_{1}^{D_{X_{i}}}X_{j}=q_{1}^{\delta_{i,j}}X_{j}q_{1}^{D_{X_{i}}},\qquad i,j=1,2,\cdots l.

(44)

The so-obtained quantization of the multiplicative quiver variety
\mathcal{M}_{q_{2}}^{(l)}
precisely yields the spherical DAHA
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(l)}_{q_{1},q_{2}}

[
94
,
99
,
100
]
. The construction involves the quantum Hamiltonian reduction of the algebra of quantum differential operators on
GL(l)
. In particular, the classical Hamiltonians (
43
) uplift to the mutually commuting Macdonald operators,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{r}=q_{2}^{-\frac{r(r-1)}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\{1,2,\cdots,l\}\\
|I|=r\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in I\\
j\notin I\end{subarray}}\frac{q_{2}^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)\prod_{i\in I}q_{1}^{D_{X_{i}}},\qquad r=1,2,\cdots,l,

(45)

furnishing the quantum Hamiltonians of the trigonometric Ruijsenaars-Schneider model of
GL(l)
-type. Thus, we conclude that the algebra of local operators on the parallel M2-branes is given by the spherical DAHA,
\text{M2}_{l,0,0}=\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{(l)}
.

By the trialiy of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
, the surjective algebra homomorphism from the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
to the spherical DAHA
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{(l)}
is obtained simply by exchanging
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
in (
35
). For completeness, we write out here the surjective algebra homomorphism

\displaystyle\rho_{\text{M2}_{l,0,0}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M2}_{l,0,0}

(46)

in terms of the modes as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{l}\left(\prod_{j\neq i}\frac{q_{2}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)X_{i}^{k}q_{1}^{-D_{X_{i}}},\\
&F_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{1}^{-1}}\sum_{i=1}^{l}\left(\prod_{j\neq i}\frac{q_{2}^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)q_{1}^{D_{X_{i}}}X_{i}^{k},\\
\end{split}\qquad k\in\mathbb{Z},\\
\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}&H_{\pm r}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{1}^{\mp r}}\sum_{i=1}^{l}X_{i}^{\pm r},\qquad r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\\
&C\mapsto 1,\quad C^{\perp}\mapsto 1.\end{split}\end{split}

(47)

In particular, the Macdonald operators (
45
) are achieved by taking suitable combinations of
F_{0}
and the commutators of
\text{ad}^{i-1}_{F_{0}}F_{\pm 1}
with
i=1,2,\cdots,l
, and picking up the images under the map
\rho_{\text{M2}_{l,0,0}}
.

Recall that the Higgs branch of the effective 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
theory on the stack of
l
D2-branes, wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
, placed upon
1
D6-brane, wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
, is the Hilbert scheme of
l
points on
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
, which is given by the Nakajima quiver variety for the same Jordan quiver (Figure
4
). The algebra of local operators on the stack of D2-branes is the quantized algebra of holomorphic functions on this quiver variety, which turns out to be the spherical subalgebra of the rational double affine Hecke algebra (spherical rational DAHA,
\mathbf{Sr\ddot{H}}
) of
GL(l)

[
10
,
12
]
. This is precisely what was called the (rational) Calogero representation of the 1-shifted affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
(
Y_{1}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1))
) in
[
7
]
. What we have here is its multiplicative uplift
-
the spherical DAHA of
GL(l)
as a representation of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)

-
established by replacing the complex manifold
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
by
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
(see table
3
).
16
16

16

The intermediate step, namely, the spherical trigonometric DAHA
\mathbf{St\ddot{H}}
as a representation of the affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
(
Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1))
), can also be achieved as the quantization of the Hilbert scheme of points in
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. See
[
101
]
, for instance. The associated twisted M-theory background is thus expected to have the complex manifold
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
(see table
3
). See
[
102
,
11
]
for an approach from the 3d mirror dual side.
See also
[
12
,
103
]
for another incarnation of the spherical DAHA as the quantized Coulomb branch of the 4d
\mathcal{N}=2^{*}
theory compactified on a circle.

Fusion of parallel M2-branes and coproduct of quantum toroidal algebra

Having identified the algebra of local operators on the parallel M2-branes with the spherical DAHA, let us examine how the statement is compatible with the fusion operation of the line defects. Due to the triality, we may restrict to the case of the M2-branes supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
without losing generality.

Let us first remind that the fusion operation for universal line defects implies the existence of the coproduct of the universal associative algebra
[
7
]
, since the fusion exhibits a representation of the universal associative algebra in the tensor product with itself.
17
17

17

Recall that the coassociativity,
(\Delta\otimes\text{id})\Delta=(\text{id}\otimes\Delta)\Delta
, follows from the fact that changing the ordering of two consecutive fusion operations results in the same line defect. We also remind that the counit
\epsilon:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to\mathbb{C}
is provided by the trivial line defect, i.e.,
\epsilon=\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,0}}
, since the fusion with a trivial line defect is the identity operation:
(\text{id}\otimes\epsilon)\Delta=(\epsilon\otimes\text{id})\Delta=\text{id}
.
In our case, the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
is indeed equipped with the coproduct
\Delta:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\,\widehat{\otimes}\,U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
given by (
233
).

fusion
\mathcal{L}_{0,m,0}
\mathcal{L}_{0,m^{\prime},0}
\mathcal{L}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}
\text{M2}_{0,m,0}
\text{M2}_{0,m^{\prime},0}
\text{M2}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}
\iota_{m,m^{\prime}}
\widehat{\otimes}

Figure 5
:
Fusion of line defects and algebra embedding induced by the coproduct

Then, we specialize to the line defect
\mathcal{L}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}
produced by
m+m^{\prime}
parallel M2-branes (
m,m^{\prime}\geq 0
). We may split them into two groups composed of
m
and
m^{\prime}
M2-branes, yielding two line defects
\mathcal{L}_{0,m,0}
and
\mathcal{L}_{0,m^{\prime},0}
lying on top of each other. The original line defect
\mathcal{L}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}
should be recovered once the two line defects
\mathcal{L}_{0,m,0}
and
\mathcal{L}_{0,m^{\prime},0}
are fused together (Figure
5
). Thus, the coupling of the composite line defect
\mathcal{L}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}
is built from the couplings of the base line defects
\mathcal{L}_{0,m,0}
and
\mathcal{L}_{0,m^{\prime},0}
. This implies there is an embedding of the algebra
\iota_{m,m^{\prime}}:\text{M2}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}\hookrightarrow\text{M2}_{0,m,0}\otimes\text{M2}_{0,m^{\prime},0}
, induced from the coproduct
\Delta
through the surjective algebra maps associated to these line defects. Namely, we have an isomorphism,

\displaystyle(\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m,0}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m^{\prime},0}})\Delta\simeq\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}},

(48)

which can also be represented by the commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\,{\widehat{\otimes}}\,U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m+m^{\prime})}_{q_{2},q_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},q_{1}}{\widehat{\otimes}}\,\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m^{\prime})}_{q_{2},q_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m,0}}\otimes\,\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m^{\prime},0}}}

\circlearrowleft

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{m,m^{\prime}}}

(49)

It is straightforward to verify that the suggested relation (
48
) holds. The left hand side can be computed using the explicit forms of the
q_{2}
-difference operators (
35
) for the
Macdonald representation
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m,0}}
and the coproduct
\Delta
(
233
) of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. The resulting
q_{2}
-difference operators is not symmetric under the action of the extended permutation group
S_{m+m^{\prime}}
, but they can be easily mapped to the symmetric ones by conjugating

\displaystyle\tilde{g}(\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m},\{X_{j}\}_{j=m+1}^{m+m^{\prime}})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}^{m^{\prime}\frac{\log q_{1}}{\log q_{2}}}\prod_{j=m+1}^{m+m^{\prime}}\frac{\left(q_{1}^{-1}\frac{X_{j}}{X_{i}};q_{2}\right)_{\infty}}{\left(\frac{X_{j}}{X_{i}};q_{2}\right)_{\infty}}.

(50)

The outcome is precisely the
q_{2}
-difference operators (
35
) for
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}}
, i.e., the right hand side of (
48
). In other words, the conjugation by
\tilde{g}
establishes the desired algebra embedding
\iota_{m,m^{\prime}}:\text{M2}_{0,m+m^{\prime},0}\hookrightarrow\text{M2}_{0,m,0}\otimes\text{M2}_{0,m^{\prime},0}
.

2.1.3
Non-parallel M2-branes and generalized Macdonald representation

Finally, let us examine the most generic case for the topological line defects in the 5d Chern-Simons theory created by non-parallel M2-branes. Namely, we put
l+m+n
M2-branes, where
l
of them wrap
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
;
m
of them wrap
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
; and
n
of them wrap
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
. We denote the algebra of local operators on this line defect by
\text{M2}_{l,m,n}
. Again by the Koszul duality, there must be a surjective homomorphism from the universal associative algebra

\displaystyle\rho_{\text{M2}_{l,m,n}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M2}_{l,m,n},

(51)

which controls the coupling of the line defect
\mathcal{L}_{l,m,n}
.

The line defect
\mathcal{L}_{l,m,n}
can be constructed by the consecutive fusion of three line defects
-

\mathcal{L}_{l,0,0}
,
\mathcal{L}_{0,m,0}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{0,0,n}

-
each of which originates from the M2-branes wrapping three different topological planes. Algebraically, the fusion induces a reconstruction of the above algebra map (
51
) as the composition of the coproducts of the universal associative algebra with the tensor product of three representations for these parallel M2-branes (surjective algebra maps to spherical DAHAs
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{(l)}
,
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}_{q_{2},q_{3}}^{(m)}
, and
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}_{q_{3},q_{1}}^{(n)}
). Namely,
18
18

18

Due to the coassociativity of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
,
\Delta^{2}:=(\text{id}\otimes\Delta)\Delta=(\Delta\otimes\text{id})\Delta
.

\displaystyle\rho_{\text{M2}_{l,m,n}}=(\rho_{\text{M2}_{l,0,0}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,m,0}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,n}})\Delta^{2}.

(52)

The homomorphism obtained in this way can be presented in terms of the modes as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{l}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{l}\frac{q_{2}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{q_{1}X_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}{X_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\frac{q_{1}X_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}{X_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}X_{i}^{k}q_{1}^{-D_{X_{i}}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{1-q_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\prod_{j=1}^{l}\frac{q_{2}X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}}{X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{m}\frac{q_{3}X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}{X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\frac{q_{2}X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}{X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}(X_{i}^{\prime})^{k}q_{2}^{-D_{X^{\prime}_{i}}}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{1-q_{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{l}\frac{q_{3}X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}}{X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{q_{3}X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}{X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{n}\frac{q_{1}X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}{X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}(X_{i}^{\prime\prime})^{k}q_{3}^{-D_{X^{\prime\prime}_{i}}}\\
&F_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{1}^{-1}}\sum_{i=1}^{l}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{l}\frac{q_{2}^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{q_{2}^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}{q_{1}q_{2}^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\frac{q_{3}^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}{q_{1}q_{3}^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}q_{1}^{D_{X_{i}}}X_{i}^{k}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{-1}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\prod_{j=1}^{l}\frac{q_{1}^{-1}X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}}{q_{1}^{-1}q_{2}X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{m}\frac{q_{3}^{-1}X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}{X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\frac{q_{3}^{-1}X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}{q_{2}q_{3}^{-1}X^{\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}q_{2}^{D_{X^{\prime}_{i}}}(X_{i}^{\prime})^{k}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{1-q_{3}^{-1}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{l}\frac{q_{1}^{-1}X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}}{q_{1}^{-1}q_{3}X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\frac{q_{2}^{-1}X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}{q_{2}^{-1}q_{3}X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=1\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}^{n}\frac{q_{1}^{-1}X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}{X^{\prime\prime}_{i}-X_{j}^{\prime\prime}}q_{3}^{D_{X^{\prime\prime}_{i}}}(X_{i}^{\prime\prime})^{k},\end{split}\qquad k\in\mathbb{Z},\\
\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}&H_{\pm r}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{1}^{\mp r}}\sum_{i=1}^{l}X_{i}^{\pm r}+\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{\mp r}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}(X_{i}^{\prime})^{\pm r}+\frac{1}{1-q_{3}^{\mp r}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}^{\prime\prime})^{\pm r},\qquad r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},\\
&C\mapsto 1,\quad C^{\perp}\mapsto 1.\end{split}\end{split}

(53)

Here, we conjugated the right hand side of (
52
) by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hat{g}(\{X_{i}\}_{i=1}^{l},\{X^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m},\{X^{\prime\prime}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n})\\
&=\prod_{i=1}^{l}X_{i}^{m+n}\prod_{j=1}^{m}(X_{j}^{\prime})^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{l}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left(1-\frac{q_{1}^{-1}X_{j}^{\prime}}{X_{i}}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{m}\prod_{k=1}^{n}\left(1-\frac{q_{2}^{-1}X_{k}^{\prime\prime}}{X_{j}^{\prime}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{l}\prod_{k=1}^{n}\left(1-\frac{q_{1}^{-1}X_{k}^{\prime\prime}}{X_{i}}\right)\end{split}

(54)

to put the resulting
q
-difference operators in a symmetric fashion as above. Since this generalizes the Macdonald representation
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,n}}
, we call it the generalized Macdonald representation of the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
.

What we established is the multiplicative uplift of the generalized (rational) Calogero representation of the 1-shifted affine Yangian of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
obtained in
[
7
]
, as a result of changing the complex manifold from
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}
to
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. In this regard, we may refer to it as the generalized trigonometric Ruijsenaars-Schneider representation of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
as well.

2.2
M5-branes and holomorphic surface defects

The M5-branes in the twisted M-theory can be supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
, where
c\in\{1,2,3\}
and
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}=\{X^{\mathbf{q}}Z^{-\mathbf{p}}=\text{const}\}\subset\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
is a holomorphic curve. Passing to the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory, the M5-branes descend to a holomorphic surface defect supported on the curve
C^{(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})}
. Fixing the holomorphic part of the support as
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
, the most generic configuration comprises
L
,
M
, and
N
M5-branes wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
,
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
, and
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
, respectively. We call the induced holomorphic surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
.
19
19

19

We emphasize that the
c
-th subscript of the surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
indicates the number of M5-branes wrapping the
(c\pm 1)
-th topological planes, whereas the
c
-th subscript of the line defect
\mathcal{L}_{l,m,n}
denotes the number of M2-branes wrapping the
c
-th topological plane.

The local operators of the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory localized at the holomorphic curve
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
are the modes of the ghost field under the Laurent expansion along the transverse holomorphic direction. They form a chiral algebra, say
\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}}
, by their OPEs. The BRST-invariant coupling of a surface defect, carrying a chiral algebra
\mathcal{V}
of local operators, is expected to be classified by certain universal chiral algebra
\left(\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}}\right)^{!}
and surjective homomorphisms
\left(\text{Obs}_{q_{1},q_{2}}^{C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}}\right)^{!}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{V}

[
104
]
. We anticipate this universal chiral algebra is again given by the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
, enhanced by a proper chiral algebra structure.
20
20

20

More precisely, we expect that the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
is equipped with a
quantum
vertex algebra structure
[
105
,
106
]
, and that the surjective map
\rho_{\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
is uplifted to a quantum vertex algebra homomorphism. For instance, the
q
-boson algebra for a single M5-brane and the
q
-deformed
W
-algebras for multiple parallel M5-branes are known to admit the quantum vertex algebra structure
[
105
]
. Nevertheless, the quantum vertex algebra structure for the whole quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
is not well understood, and we will restrict our discussion to the mode algebras in this work. See
[
107
]
for a study of quantum vertex algebra structure for higher-rank quantum toroidal algebras.

In principle, we can derive the OPEs of local operators in the following way. Let us take the example of
C^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
. We can explicitly write the surface defect coupling as

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}}W^{(m)}(A_{\bar{X}})_{m},

(55)

where
(A_{\bar{X}})_{m}
are the modes of the gauge field under the Laurent expansion along the transverse plane
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
and
W^{(m)}
are local operators on the surface defect. Then, the OPEs of the local operators can be deduced as conditions for the anomalous Feynman diagrams to cancel each other (see
[
104
]
for the case of surface defect in holomorphic Chern-Simons theory). We will not conduct such a perturbative analysis of the 5d Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
in the present work. Instead, we will restrict our attention to the mode algebra, referred to as
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
, of the local operators on the surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
. We will suggest natural candidates for them and the surjective algebra homomorphisms
\rho_{\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
.

2.2.1
Single M5-brane and free
q
-boson

In this section, we will only consider M5-branes whose holomorphic part of the support is
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
(namely,
C^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
). We will come back to M5-branes supported on general holomorphic curves
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
in section
5
. Consider a single M5-brane wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
. Deforming
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}
to the one-centered Taub-NUT space and reducing the twisted M-theory along the Taub-NUT circle, the M5-brane becomes a D4-brane which intersects the emergent single D6-brane along the holomorphic surface
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
.

If the holomorphic plane transverse to the D4-brane were
\mathbb{C}
, the D4-D4 open string would give rise to the 5d
\mathcal{N}=2

U(1)
gauge theory, coupled to the chiral fermions living on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
arising from the D4-D6 open string
[
108
,
109
]
. Due to the
\Omega
-background, the 5d gauge theory is localized to the 3d
U(1)
Chern-Simons theory
[
110
,
111
,
112
]
. Therefore, the algebra of local operators on the surface defect of the 5d Chern-Simons theory is given by the algebra of chiral fermions coupled to the 3d Chern-Simons theory, which is identified to be the free boson algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1)

[
5
]
.

In our case, however, the open strings may wind around the compact circle of the plane
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}\simeq\mathbb{R}_{z}\times S^{1}
transverse to the D4-brane, producing infinite numbers of winding modes. The effective 5d gauge theory for the D4-D4 open string now contains infinite numbers of Kaluza-Klein modes, which can be uplifted to the 6d
\mathcal{N}=(1,1)

U(1)
gauge theory compactified on a circle, as a result. Due to the
\Omega
-background, the 6d gauge theory is localized into the 4d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}

[
113
]
. The chiral fermions on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
couple to the 4d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory, and the quantum corrections would uplift the algebra of local operators to a representation of the quantum affine algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
, namely the free
q
-boson algebra
U_{q}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1))
.
21
21

21

We stress, however, that this claim regarding the quantum corrections to the algebra of local operators on the holomorphic surface defect in the 4d Chern-Simons theory is an independent statement that needs to be proven within its perturbation theory, just as was done for the surface defect in the holomorphic Chern-Simons theory in
[
104
]
. To the best of our knowledge, such a perturbative analysis for the holomorphic surface defect has not yet been established in the 4d Chern-Simons theory, unlike the studies for topological line defects in
[
88
]
and for topological surface defects in
[
114
]
.
Since the representation has to be non-trivial, it should be the
q
-boson algebra itself. Therefore, we suggest that the algebra
\text{M5}_{0,1,0}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
of local operators on the surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{0,1,0}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
is the free
q
-boson algebra.

From the triality of the twisted M-theory, we expect that a single M5-brane on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
,
c\in\{1,2,3\}
, would also support a
q
-boson algebra
\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
with the permutation on
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
. We used the short-hand notation
(\delta_{c,a})_{a=1}^{3}\equiv c
for the subscript only for the cases with single M5-brane. The free
q
-boson algebra
\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
is the algebra generated by the modes
a_{r}^{(c)}
,
r\in\mathbb{Z}
, subject to the commutation relation

\displaystyle[a_{r}^{(c)},a_{s}^{(c)}]=-\delta_{r+s,0}\frac{r}{\kappa_{r}}(q_{c}^{r/2}-q_{c}^{-r/2})^{3}.

(56)

Meanwhile, we suggested that the universal associative algebra for the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
is the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
, from which there is a surjective algebra homomorphism to each
\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
. Indeed, it is straightforward to see that two of the defining relations (
224c
) and (
224d
) for
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
are equivalent to the
q
-boson commutation relation satisfied by
H_{r}
,

\displaystyle[H_{r},H_{s}]=\delta_{r+s,0}\frac{r}{\kappa_{r}}(C^{r}-C^{-r}).

(57)

At
(C,C^{\perp})=\left(q_{c}^{1/2},1\right)
where
c\in\{1,2,3\}
, this vertex representation extends to the whole quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. Namely, we have surjective algebra homomorphisms

\displaystyle\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}},

(58)

given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E(X)\mapsto-e^{a_{0}^{(c)}\frac{\log q_{c+1}\log q_{c-1}}{\log q_{c}}}\frac{1-q_{c}}{\kappa_{1}}\eta_{c}(X),\quad F(X)\mapsto e^{-a_{0}^{(c)}\frac{\log q_{c+1}\log q_{c-1}}{\log q_{c}}}\frac{1-q_{c}^{-1}}{\kappa_{1}}\xi_{c}(X),\\
&H_{r}\mapsto\frac{a_{r}^{(c)}}{q_{c}^{r/2}-q_{c}^{-r/2}},\qquad(C,C^{\perp})\mapsto\left(q_{c}^{1/2},1\right),\end{split}

(59)

where we used the indices in cyclical manner so that
\{c-1,c,c+1\}=\{1,2,3\}
. Here, the vertex operators are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\eta_{c}(X)=\exp\left(-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{1}{(q_{c}^{r/2}-q_{c}^{-r/2})^{2}}a_{-r}^{(c)}X^{r}\right)\exp\left(-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{q_{c}^{-{r/2}}}{(q_{c}^{r/2}-q_{c}^{-r/2})^{2}}a_{r}^{(c)}X^{-r}\right)\\
&\xi_{c}(X)=\exp\left(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{q_{c}^{r/2}}{(q_{c}^{r/2}-q_{c}^{-r/2})^{2}}a_{-r}^{(c)}X^{r}\right)\exp\left(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{1}{(q_{c}^{r/2}-q_{c}^{-r/2})^{2}}a_{r}^{(c)}X^{-r}\right).\end{split}

(60)

For later convenience, we also denote the image of
K^{\pm}(X)
by vertex operators
\varphi^{\pm}_{c}(X)
,

\displaystyle K^{\pm}(X)\mapsto\varphi_{c}^{\pm}(X)\equiv\exp\left(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{a_{\pm r}^{(c)}}{q_{c}^{r/2}-q_{c}^{-r/2}}X^{\mp r}\right).

(61)

We may represent the
q
-boson algebra (
56
) on the Fock space
\mathcal{F}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v)=\bigoplus_{\{\lambda\}}\mathbb{C}|v,\lambda\rangle^{(c)}
, where the basis elements are enumerated by a single Young diagram
\lambda=(\lambda_{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}
,
\lambda_{i}\geq\lambda_{i+1}\geq 0
. The whole Fock space can be generated by acting the creation operators
a_{-r}^{(c)}
,
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
on the vacuum
|v,\varnothing\rangle^{(c)}\in\mathcal{F}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v)
satisfying

\displaystyle a_{r}^{(c)}|v,\varnothing\rangle^{(c)}=0,\quad r>0,\qquad a_{0}^{(c)}|v,\varnothing\rangle^{(c)}=\frac{\log q_{c}}{\log q_{c+1}\log q_{c-1}}\log v|v,\varnothing\rangle^{(c)},

(62)

where
v\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is a module parameter. The seemingly unusual normalization of the zero-mode
a_{0}^{(c)}
will be explained soon. This is a faithful representation of the
q
-boson algebra,
\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
. Thus, what we found for single M5-brane can be summarized into
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\hookrightarrow\text{End}(\mathcal{F}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v))
where
c\in\{1,2,3\}
.

For later use, we stress that the grading operator
d^{\perp}
plays the role of the conjugate variable to the zero-mode
a_{0}^{(c)}
. Indeed, from its definition (
234
) it follows that

\displaystyle[d^{\perp},a_{m}^{(c)}]=\frac{\log q_{c}}{\log q_{c+1}\log q_{c-1}}\delta_{m,0},\qquad m\in\mathbb{Z}.

(63)

This implies that the Fock representation on
\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{c}
can be extended to incorporate the grading operator
d^{\perp}
by regarding it as the shift operator for the module parameter:
q^{d^{\perp}}|v,\lambda\rangle^{(c)}=|vq^{-1},\lambda\rangle^{(c)}
for any
q\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

Note that the representations (
58
) break the triality of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
, in a way that the duality of exchanging
q_{c+1}
and
q_{c-1}
is still preserved. This is indeed to be expected from the corresponding M-brane configuration, since the support of the M5-brane distinguishes two parameters
q_{c\pm 1}
out of the triple
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
.

2.2.2
Parallel M5-branes and
q
-deformed
W
-algebra

Next, let us consider multiple parallel M5-branes all of which are supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
. By triality, it is enough to consider one of the three cases
c\in\{1,2,3\}
, and let us fix
c=3
and the number of M5-branes to be
N
. The resulting holomorphic surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
carries the algebra
\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
of local operators.

Under the reduction along the circle of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\simeq\mathbb{R}_{x}\times S^{1}
, the
N
M5-branes become
N
D4-branes supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}
. Due to the
\Omega
-background, the local operators form a non-commutative associative algebra
\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
on the fixed locus
\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}_{x}
of the isometry. Since there are infinite numbers of winding modes along the transverse circle, the effective 5d
U(N)
gauge theory for the D4-D4 open string contains infinite numbers of Kaluza-Klein modes. Its Hilbert space is the equivariant K-theory of the moduli space of
U(N)
instantons, on which the algebra
\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
of local operators can act. It is indeed known that the equivariant K-theory of the moduli space of
U(N)
instantons admits an action of the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))

[
115
]
, which would factor through a surjective algebra homomorphism
\rho_{\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
.

\mathcal{S}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{0,0,N}
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{0,0,N^{\prime}}
\mathcal{S}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{0,0,N+N^{\prime}}
fusion
\text{M5}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{0,0,N}
\text{M5}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{0,0,N^{\prime}}
\text{M5}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{0,0,N+N^{\prime}}
\delta_{N,N^{\prime}}
\otimes

Figure 6
:
Fusion of surface defects and algebra embedding induced by the coproduct

In section
3
, we will show that this algebra
\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
is precisely the
q
-deformed
W
-algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(N)

[
116
,
48
,
49
,
50
]
, which we denote by
q\mathcal{W}_{N}
. To verify this, it is crucial to study the fusion of the surface defects and its implications. First, note that the surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{0,0,N+N^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
can be obtained by fusing two surface defects
\mathcal{S}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{0,0,N^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
on top of each other (see Figure
6
). Just as in the case of the topological line defects, the fusion implies that the coupling of the fused surface defect can be built from the couplings of the individual surface defects before the fusion. Namely, there is an algebra embedding
\delta_{N,N^{\prime}}:\text{M5}_{0,0,N+N^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\hookrightarrow\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\otimes\text{M5}_{0,0,N^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
induced by the coproduct of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
, which can be represented by the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\,{\widehat{\otimes}}\,U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q\mathcal{W}_{N+N^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q\mathcal{W}_{N}\,{\otimes}\,q\mathcal{W}_{N^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\text{M5}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{0,0,N+N^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\text{M5}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{0,0,N}}\otimes\,\rho_{\text{M5}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{0,0,N^{\prime}}}}

\circlearrowleft

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{N,N^{\prime}}}

(64)

Then, by consecutively applying this diagram, we achieve an embedding of the algebra

\displaystyle\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\hookrightarrow\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}},

(65)

into the tensor product of free
q
-boson algebras. The fusion of surface defects thus provides the free field realization of the M5-brane algebra
\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
.

In section
3
, we will show that this free field realization exactly matches with that of the
q
-deformed
W
-algebra, given in
[
49
,
50
]
. The proof is given in two steps. First, we establish the Miura transformation for the algebra
\text{M5}_{0,0,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
which explicitly realizes the commutative diagram (
64
) by the product of newly defined Miura operators. Then, we prove that the algebra generated by such a Miura transformation is completely characterized as the commutants of screening charges in the tensor product of
q
-bosons, which precisely recovers the ones in
[
49
,
50
]
.

We emphasize that our result therefore derives the Miura transformation for the
q
-deformed
W
-algebra, which can be regarded as an independent result within itself.

2.2.3
Non-parallel M5-branes and
q
-deformed
Y
-algebra

We further study more general configurations of non-parallel M5-branes, which leads to an extension of the result for the parallel case. Here, by
non-parallel
, we indicate that the topological part of the support may differ for different M5-branes. This should not be confused with the holomorphic part of the support, which is chosen to be
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}=C^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
for all the M5-branes. We will return to the investigation of M5-branes supported on generic holomorphic curves
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
in section
5
.

Let us be given with
L+M+N
M5-branes which share the common holomorphic part of the support
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
. The topological part of the support is determined by the function
c:I\mapsto c_{I}\in\{1,2,3\}
,
I\in\{1,2,\cdots,L+M+N\}
, where the topological part of the support of the
I
-th M5-brane is assigned to be
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c_{I}+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c_{I}-1}}
. Let us set
(|c^{-1}(1)|,|c^{-1}(2)|,|c^{-1}(3)|)=(L,M,N)
. We call the induced holomorphic surface defect in the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
, and the algebra of local operators on this surface defect is denoted by
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
.

Just as in the parallel case, the fusion operation of surface defects implies that the coproduct of the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
induces the algebra embedding

\displaystyle\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\hookrightarrow\text{M5}_{c_{1}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\otimes\text{M5}_{c_{2}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\text{M5}_{c_{L+M+N}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}

(66)

into the tensor product of
q
-boson algebras. Thus, the algebra
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
admits a free field realization.

In section
3
, we show that this free field realization is implemented by a Miura transformation, with newly defined Miura operators. We also show that the algebra generated by the Miura transformation can be completely characterized as the commutants of properly defined screening charges in the tensor product of
q
-bosons. Due to the equivalence that we prove, the latter may be employed as the definition of the algebra
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
. In particular, we verify that changing the ordering of the
q
-boson algebras in the tensor product produces an isomorphic algebra, as a consequence of the universal Yang-Baxter equation for the universal R-matrix of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. Hence, the algebra of local operators on the fused surface defect
\mathcal{S}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
can be denoted as
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
without ambiguity.

The algebra
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
can be thought of as the multiplicative uplift of the
Y
-algebra studied in
[
6
]
. As we will explain, the multiplicative uplift is achieved by replacing
\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
by
\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}
for the holomorphic part of the worldvolume of the twisted M-theory. In this sense, we call
\text{M5}_{L,M,N}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
the
q
-deformed
Y
-algebra as well, which may be denoted by
q{Y}_{L,M,N}
.

3
M2-M5 intersections, R-matrices, and Miura operators

So far, we have introduced the algebra of local operators on M2- and M5-branes and surjective homomorphisms from the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. In this section, we study the intersections of M2-branes and M5-branes. Passing to the 5d Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
, the M2-M5 intersections descend to the intersections between topological line defects and holomorphic surface defects. Note that the M2-brane wraps one, say
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c}}
, of the three topological planes, while an M5-brane wraps two, say
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c^{\prime}+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c^{\prime}-1}}
, of them. Depending on the relative orientation, we distinguish two possibilities:

•

If
c=c^{\prime}
, the two defects are completely transverse so that, when visualized in the 3-dimensional worldvolume that they span, the line defect can only pass through the surface defect. By slightly abusing the terminology, we call this configuration the
transverse
M2-M5 intersection.

•

If
c\neq c^{\prime}
, the line defect can end on the surface defect at the
non-transverse
M2-M5 intersection. Thus the line defect may terminate at the intersection, or may continue across the intersection. In the former case, the M2-brane lies on a semi-infinite line in
\mathbb{R}_{t}
, stretched from the intersection point to either positive or negative infinity.

We will investigate these two cases separately.

In this section, we will restrict to the case where the holomorphic part of the support of M5-branes is
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}=C^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
. We will come back to more generic M5-brane configurations in section
5
.

3.1
R-matrix for transverse M2-M5 intersection

Let us begin with the cases where there are a single M2-brane and a single M5-brane. Due to the triality, we may fix the M2-brane to be supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
. Then, the M5-brane wrapping
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
is totally transverse to the M2-brane. Even though they do not intersect at all in the 11-dimensional worldvolume, we shall call such a configuration a transverse M2-M5 intersection.

The transverse M2-M5 intersection supports a space of local operators. We can write a generic element of this space as

\displaystyle R^{(3)}=R_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}\in\text{M2}_{0,0,1}\widehat{\otimes}\,\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}.

(67)

Under the condition that the support of the M2-brane is
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
and the holomorphic part of the support of the M5-brane is
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}=C^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
, we use the abbreviation
R^{(3)}
where the superscript indicates the topological part of the M5-brane support
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}
(by
\{1,2\}=\{1,2,3\}\setminus\{3\}
). Passing to the 5d
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory, the transverse M2-M5 intersection should descend to a gauge-invariant intersection between a line defect and a surface defect. The local operators at the intersection are thereby constrained by the gauge invariance condition.

As we discussed in section
1.2
, the fully quantum-corrected gauge-invariance condition is expected to be given by

\displaystyle R^{(3)}\Delta_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}(g)=\Delta_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}^{\text{op}}(g)R^{(3)},\quad\text{for any }g\in U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1)),

(68)

where
\Delta_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}
is the mixed coproduct, obtained by composing the coproduct with the respective representations,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}=(\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}})\Delta\quad:\quad U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to\text{M2}_{0,0,1}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}},

(69)

and
\Delta^{\text{op}}=\sigma\circ\Delta
is the opposite coproduct.

R^{(3)}
\times
0
\mathcal{S}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
\mathcal{L}_{0,0,1}
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
\mathbb{R}_{t}

Figure 7
:
Transverse M2-M5 intersection

The constraint (
68
) can be solved exactly. We show in appendix
C
that it fully determines
R^{(3)}
up to an overall scaling. The solution is obtained as the product:

\displaystyle R^{(3)}=\bar{R}^{(3)}K^{(3)},

(70)

with the Cartan part given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}K^{(3)}=\left(q_{3}^{\frac{d}{2}}\otimes 1\right)\exp\left[\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{X_{1}^{-r}}{1-q_{3}^{r}}\otimes\frac{a_{r}^{(3)}}{q_{3}^{r/2}-q_{3}^{-r/2}}\right]=\left(q_{3}^{\frac{d}{2}}\otimes 1\right)V_{3}(X_{1}),\end{split}

(71)

where we defined the vertex operator
V_{3}(X_{1}):=\prod_{j=0}^{\infty}\varphi_{3}^{+}(q_{3}^{-j}X_{1})=\exp\left[\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{X_{1}^{-r}}{1-q_{3}^{r}}\otimes\frac{a_{r}^{(3)}}{q_{3}^{r/2}-q_{3}^{-r/2}}\right]
. The rest is given by

\displaystyle\bar{R}^{(3)}=1-e^{-a_{0}^{(3)}\frac{\log q_{1}\log q_{2}}{\log q_{3}}}q_{3}^{-1}\xi_{3}(X_{1})q_{3}^{-D_{X_{1}}}

(72)

Note that
X_{1}\in\text{M2}_{0,0,1}
commutes with itself so that the vertex operators with the argument
X_{1}
are well-defined.

3.2
Non-transverse M2-M5 intersections

We investigate the intersection between a single M2-brane and a single M5-brane that share one topological
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-plane in their supports. We fix the support of the M2-brane as
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
. All other cases follow simply by the triality. The holomorphic part of the support of the M5-brane is chosen as
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
.

Since we are assuming the support of the M5-brane shares
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}
, the only two options are
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
with
c\in\{1,2\}
. We also use the notation
\{\bar{c}\}=\{1,2\}\setminus\{c\}
.

3.2.1
Intertwiners from intersections with ends

Unlike the transverse case, the M2-brane can end on the M5-brane from either direction of the line
\mathbb{R}_{t}
. We show such non-transverse M2-M5 intersections with an end are associated to intertwiners of the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
.

First, we consider the case of the M2-brane ending on the M5-brane from the negative direction of
\mathbb{R}_{t}
. In other words, the M2-brane is stretched between the negative infinity and the intersection point in
\mathbb{R}_{t}
. It is helpful to view the other semi-infinite line as being occupied by a trivial line defect, so that the local operator supported at the intersection is an element of
22
22

22

Compare with the transverse R-matrix
R^{(3)}
(
67
), where both semi-infinite lines above and below from the intersection point support the same line defect
\mathcal{L}_{0,0,1}
. There, we had
\text{M2}_{0,0,1}=\text{End}(\mathcal{V}_{3}(u))
in turn.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Phi_{3,c}\in\text{Hom}(\mathcal{V}_{3}(u),\mathbb{C})\otimes\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}},\qquad c\in\{1,2\}.\end{split}

(73)

The intersection must be gauge-invariant. As argued in section
1.2
for the line defect passing through the surface defect, the classical gauge-invariance constraint for the local operator
\Phi_{3,c}
reads

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,c}\left(\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}(g)\otimes\text{id}+\text{id}\otimes\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}(g)\right)=\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}(g)\Phi_{3,c}

(74)

for any
g\in U\left(\mathscr{O}_{q_{2}}(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times})\right)
.

Turning on
q_{1}\neq 1
, the gauge-invariance condition for intersection gets quantum corrections, which are non-linear in the generators. By a similar argument for the transverse M2-M5 intersection, we expect that the full quantum gauge-invariance condition reads

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,c}\Delta_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}(g)=\rho_{\text{M5}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}_{c}}(g)\Phi_{3,c},\qquad\text{for any }g\in U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1)),

(75)

for each
c\in\{1,2\}
.
23
23

23

Using the counit
\epsilon=\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,0}}
associated to the trivial line defect (see footnote
17
), the gauge transformation on right hand side of (
75
) can be rephrased as
(\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,0}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}})\Delta^{\text{op}}(g)=(1\otimes\rho_{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}})(\epsilon\otimes\text{id})\Delta^{\text{op}}(g)=\rho_{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}(g)
.
The gauge-invariance condition for all the other cases
\Phi_{c,c^{\prime}}\in\text{Hom}(\mathcal{V}_{c},\mathbb{C})\otimes\text{M5}_{c^{\prime}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
,
c\neq c^{\prime}
simply follows by the triality. In this sense, the non-transverse M2-M5 intersection provides the intertwiner between the representations
\text{M2}_{0,0,1}
and
\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. The constraint (
75
) determines
\Phi_{3,c}
up to an overall scaling, yielding
[
117
]

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Phi_{3,c}&=e^{\frac{\log q_{\bar{c}}}{\log q_{c}}\left({\log X_{1}}\otimes a_{0}^{(c)}\right)}\left(1\otimes q_{\bar{c}}^{d^{\perp}}\right)\exp\left[-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{q_{3}^{\frac{r}{2}}}{\left(q_{c}^{\frac{r}{2}}-q_{c}^{-\frac{r}{2}}\right)^{2}\left(q_{3}^{\frac{r}{2}}-q_{3}^{-\frac{r}{2}}\right)}X_{1}^{r}\otimes a_{-r}^{(c)}\right]\\
&\qquad\times\exp\left[\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{q_{3}^{-\frac{r}{2}}q_{c}^{-\frac{r}{2}}}{\left(q_{c}^{\frac{r}{2}}-q_{c}^{-\frac{r}{2}}\right)^{2}\left(q_{3}^{\frac{r}{2}}-q_{3}^{-\frac{r}{2}}\right)}X_{1}^{-r}\otimes a_{r}^{(c)}\right].\end{split}

(76)

Note that
\log X_{1}
makes sense as an element of
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{V}_{3}(u),\mathbb{C})
since
X_{1}
acts on
\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)
as an element of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. Noting that
\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)^{*}=\text{Hom}(\mathcal{V}_{3}(u),\mathbb{C})
, we may also expand
\Phi_{3,c}
in the dual basis
\{[u]_{i}^{(3)*}\;|\;i\in\mathbb{Z}\}
satisfying
[u]_{i}^{(3)*}([u]_{j}^{(3)})=\delta_{i,j}
as
\Phi_{3,c}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(\Phi_{3,c})_{i}[u]^{(3)*}_{i}
, where the coefficients
(\Phi_{3,c})_{i}
is obtained simply by replacing
X_{1}
by
uq_{3}^{i}
due to its action on
[u]_{i}^{(3)}\in\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)
.

It should be stressed that
\Phi_{3,c}
provides a reconstruction of a vertex operator in the
q
-boson quantum vertex algebra in the realm of the representation theory of the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. Moreover, in the degeneration limit of the
q

\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1)
vertex algebra, it reduces to the usual degenerate vertex operator. The degenerate limit is
R\to 0
where
q_{a}^{R{\varepsilon}_{a}}
, in which the
q
