{\mathcal{S}}^{3}_{1,1}\;:\;\;\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{1})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{1}\,q^{-1}_{2})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{2}\,q_{2})\;,

(157)

and the dual intertwiner

\Phi_{3,1}^{*}\;:\;\;\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{2}\,q_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)\otimes\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{2})\;.

(158)

Above,
\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{1})
labels the Fock space of the first
\text{NS5}_{1,0,0}
brane (the one the D3-brane ends on), and
\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{2})
labels the Fock space of the second intersecting
\text{NS5}_{1,0,0}
brane (the notation was introduced above equation (
62
)). Moreover, the state in
\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)
is annihilated by the dual state
[u]^{*}_{0}
from the vacuum: if we expand
\Phi^{*}_{3,1}
in terms of basis elements as

\Phi^{*}_{3,1}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(\Phi^{*}_{3,1})_{i}[u]_{i}^{(3)}
, then the insertion of the vacuum state
{[u]^{(3)*}_{0}}
results in
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(\Phi^{*}_{3,1})_{i}\,{[u]^{(3)*}_{0}}\,[u]_{i}^{(3)}=(\Phi^{*}_{3,1})_{0}
, by orthonormality of the basis.

Equivalently, the half-index is also the vev of an R-matrix, as pictured on the bottom of Figure
14
. Namely, the integrand can also be understood as the
composition of two different homomorphisms: the dual intertwiner

\Phi_{3,1}^{*}\;:\;\;\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{1})\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)\otimes\mathcal{F}_{1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{1}\,q^{-1}_{2})\;.

(159)

and the non-transverse R-matrix

R^{(1)}\;:\;\;\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)\otimes\mathcal{F}_{2}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{2})\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)\otimes\mathcal{F}_{2}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{2})\;.

(160)

Explicitly, define an integrand

R^{(1)}(X_{1})=\oint\frac{dX}{2\pi i\,X}\,{\mathcal{R}}^{(1)}(X,X_{1})\;,

(161)

for the integral presentation (
89
) of the R-matrix
R^{(1)}
; then the half-index is the vev

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf N},+)}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}-\text{NS5}_{1,0,0}}=\left\langle\oint\frac{dX}{2\pi i\,X}\,{\mathcal{R}}^{(1)}(X,X_{1})\,(\Phi_{3,1}^{*}(X)\otimes 1)\right\rangle\;.

(162)

4.2.2
Index for non-transverse
\text{D3}_{0,0,1}
-
\text{NS5}_{0,1,0}
intersection

We start again with a semi-infinite D3
0,0,1
brane ending on an NS5
1,0,0
brane. This time, we make the D3-brane intersect a new NS5
0,1,0
brane, located some distance away from NS5
1,0,0
along
\mathbb{R}_{t}
. We would like to understand the half-index of the resulting configuration.

Quantizing the zero modes of strings at the D3
0,0,1
-NS5
0,1,0
intersection yields a 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
hypermultiplet as before. In the index, this is the contribution (
142
), with a swap of fugacities
q_{2}\leftrightarrow q_{1}
:

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\sqrt{q_{3}/q_{1}}\;X/X_{1}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}{\left(\sqrt{q_{3}q_{1}}\;X/X_{1}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}\;.

(163)

Quantizing the zero modes of strings starting and ending on the D3-brane segment, between the NS5
1,0,0
and NS5
0,1,0
branes, the supersymmetry is broken from 8 to 4 supercharges, and the surviving degrees of freedom are those of a 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
vector multiplet.
Indeed, the NS5
0,1,0
brane is rotated in the
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}
plane compared to the NS5
1,0,0
brane; this is a complex mass deformation which explicitly breaks 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetry to
\mathcal{N}=2

[
137
,
138
]
. The
x^{3}
and
x^{4}
moduli for the position of the D3 brane on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}
are lifted. After the mass deformation, the
\mathcal{N}=2
adjoint chiral multiplet inside the
\mathcal{N}=4
vector multiplet no longer contributes to the index, and only the
\mathcal{N}=2
vector multiplet remains.

Imposing boundary conditions
{\bf N}
on this multiplet, the index contribution is

\left(q_{3};q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\oint\frac{dX}{2\pi i\,X}\;.

(164)

Note the absence of the factor
\left(q^{-1}_{2}\,;q_{3}\right)^{-1}_{\infty}
, which stood for the contribution of the adjoint chiral multiplet in (
147
).

In our abelian setup, the 3d gauge theory has no gauge anomaly, but it has a
U(1)_{R}
R-symmetry anomaly: the
\mathcal{N}=2
gaugino contribution to the boundary anomaly polynomial is
+\frac{1}{2}f^{2}_{R}
, with
f_{R}
the curvature of
U(1)_{R}
.
34
34

34

For
K
D3-branes and a
U(K)
gauge group, there would now be a gauge anomaly
+K\text{Tr}(f^{2})-(\text{Tr}f)^{2}
to cancel: if we do not introduce additional matter (as new 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
chiral multiplets and 2d
\mathcal{N}=(0,2)
chiral multiplets at the boundary), one possibility is to turn on bare Chern-Simons levels in the bulk. In fact, for a
U(K)
gauge group, we are allowed to turn on two levels; for the
SU(K)
part of the gauge group, a Chern-Simons level
k_{1}
contributes
k_{1}\text{Tr}(f^{2})
to the anomaly polynomial. For the
U(1)
part, an effective Chern-Simons level
k_{1}+k_{2}
contributes
\frac{k_{2}}{K}(\text{Tr}f)^{2}
to the anomaly polynomial. We would therefore choose
k_{1}=-K
and
k_{2}=K
.
We will ignore this contribution as it factors out of the index integral. The remaining mixed anomalies are as before, again after swapping
q_{2}\leftrightarrow q_{1}
.
It follows that the index evaluates to

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf N},+)}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}\text{-}\text{NS5}_{0,1,0}}=\left(q_{3}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\oint\frac{dX}{2\pi i\,X}\,e^{-\frac{\ln(q_{1})\ln(X/X_{1})}{\ln(q_{3})}}\,e^{-\frac{\ln(v)\ln(X/X_{1})}{\ln(q_{3})}}\frac{\left(\sqrt{q_{3}/q_{1}}\;X/X_{1}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}{\left(\sqrt{q_{3}q_{1}}\;X/X_{1}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}\;.

(165)

This is the vev of a different screening current (
124
) and a dual intertwiner,

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf N},+)}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}\text{-}\text{NS5}_{0,1,0}}=\left\langle\oint\frac{dX}{2\pi i\,X}\left(1\otimes\Phi_{3,1}^{*}(X_{1})\right)\,{\mathcal{S}}^{3}_{1,2}(X)\,\right\rangle\;,

(166)

where the bracket notation labels the vacuum

\left\langle\ldots\right\rangle={[u]^{(3)*}_{0}}\otimes\langle v_{1}\,q_{2}^{-1},\varnothing|\otimes\langle v_{2},\varnothing|\;\;\ldots\;\;|v_{1},\varnothing\rangle\otimes|v_{2},\varnothing\rangle\;.

(167)

Equivalently, this is the vev of an R-matrix: if we define

R^{(2)}(X_{1})=\oint\frac{dX}{2\pi i\,X}\,{\mathcal{R}}^{(2)}(X,X_{1})\;,

(168)

where
R^{(2)}
is the R-matrix integral (
89
), we obtain

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf N},+)}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}-\text{NS5}_{0,1,0}}=\left\langle\oint\frac{dX}{2\pi i\,X}\,{\mathcal{R}}^{(2)}(X,X_{1})\,(\Phi_{3,2}^{*}(X)\otimes 1)\right\rangle\;.

(169)

4.2.3
Index for transverse
\text{D3}_{0,0,1}
-
\text{NS5}_{0,0,1}
intersection

The index is further able to capture the contribution of open strings at a fully transverse D3-NS5-brane intersection. For concreteness, we consider a D3
0,0,1
brane and a NS5
0,0,1
brane; this configuration is the type IIB frame dual to the M-theory setup of section
3.1
. As before, it will be convenient to make the D3
0,0,1
end on one end on a NS5
1,0,0
brane, separated from the NS5
0,0,1
brane in the
\mathbb{R}_{t}
-direction.

When
q_{c}=1
for
c=1,2,3
, the brane configuration only preserves a 1d
\mathcal{N}=4
quantum mechanics on the circle
S^{1}(R)
, by which we mean a reduction of 2d
\mathcal{N}=(0,4)
supersymmetry to one dimension. The supersymmetry is not broken further by turning on the
\Omega
-background,
q_{c}\neq 1
.
35
35

35

Explicit expressions for the preserved supercharges can be found in
[
139
]
. A detailed string theory derivation of the preserved supersymmetry is beyond the scope of this paper, but can be derived following the analysis of
[
63
]
.

The BPS states counted by the index are those of this
\mathcal{N}=4
quantum mechanics. If we view the expression (
140
) as the Witten index of this quantum mechanics, it is straightforward to count the degrees of freedom directly from the relevant 1-dimensional BPS multiplets. In fact, we could have proceeded that way already for the previous 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
brane intersections, after decomposing the 3d multiplets in terms of 1d multiplets, and identifying the 3d supercharges
Q_{+}
and
\overline{Q}_{+}
as the ones contributing to the quantum mechanical index. In the type IIB picture, this is done by explicitly introducing dynamical D1-branes, and quantizing the zero modes of strings stretching between them and the remaining heavier D-branes.

Instead, we chose to interpret (
140
) from a 3-dimensional point of view.
36
36

36

For further details on the equivalence between the indices of the 3d theory and its vortex quantum mechanics, see for instance
[
140
,
68
,
69
,
141
]
.
In this spirit, we will count the 1d
\mathcal{N}=4
multiplets of the D3-NS5 intersection as arising from a coupled 3d/1d half-index, by which we mean the half-index of a 3d bulk theory supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times S^{1}(R)
, coupled to a codimension-2 line defect along
S^{1}(R)
.

From this perspective, the D3
0,0,1
-NS5
1,0,0
and D3
0,0,1
-NS5
0,1,0
intersections from section
4.2.1
and
4.2.2
are both captured by a 3d/1d index whose sole contributions come from the 3d bulk, while the defect is trivial and contributes the identity. By contrast, the present D3
0,0,1
-NS5
0,0,1
intersection will be captured instead by a 3d/1d index whose 1d contributions are nontrivial, while the 3d bulk ones are essentially trivial (up to charge 0
U(1)
contributions and boundary mixed ’t Hooft anomalies).

We denote by
X
and
X_{1}
the (complexified) positions of the D3
0,0,1
brane and the NS5
0,0,1
brane along
\mathbb{R}_{x}
, respectively. These are both flavor symmetry masses from the point of view of the 1d quantum mechanics. We couple this 1d theory to the bulk by gauging these flavor symmetries with 3d background vector multiplets. Correspondingly, in the 3d/1d index, the 1d masses are turned into the scalars of the corresponding 3d vector multiplets. In what follows, we choose the scalar
X
to be a modulus, meaning the associated vector multiplet is dynamical. Meanwhile, we choose the scalar
X_{1}
to remain fixed as a mass even in 3d, meaning the associated vector multiplet is non-dynamical.

The fastest way to identify the contribution of the D3
0,0,1
-NS5
0,0,1
strings is to note that because the intersection is fully transverse, the modulus
X
should really be
frozen
to the locus
X=X_{1}
. If we choose Neumann boundary conditions on the 3d
U(1)
gauge field, this is easily achieved by considering the integral

\oint{dX}\;\frac{1}{1-X_{1}/X}\,\;\ldots

(170)

That is, we force
X=X_{1}
via the contribution of a 1d
\mathcal{N}=2
chiral multiplet.
Because the preserved supersymmetry is
\mathcal{N}=4
, this cannot be the full story: the 1d
\mathcal{N}=2
chiral multiplet must pair up with another 1d
\mathcal{N}=2
chiral multiplet to restore
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetry.

With the support of the D3
0,0,1
brane along
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}
, this second chiral multiplet will have
U(1)_{J_{3}}
charge -1 (for a derivation, see for instance
[
139
]
). Then, after including the contribution of the bulk mixed topological-gauge anomaly, we obtain the index of the D3
0,0,1
-NS5
0,0,1
intersection:

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf N})}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}-\text{NS5}_{0,0,1}}=\oint{dX}\,e^{-\frac{\ln(v)\ln(X/X_{1})}{\ln(q_{3})}}\frac{1}{\left(1-X_{1}/X\right)\left(1-q^{-1}_{3}\,X_{1}/X\right)}\;.

(171)

Correspondingly, the contour is chosen to enclose not only the simple pole at
X=X_{1}
, as we argued classically, but also the second simple pole at
X=X_{1}\,q^{-1}_{3}
, understood as a quantum contribution. For another derivation of the contribution (
171
), see the work
[
142
]
.

Up to overall normalization, we recognize the vev of the totally transverse R-matrix: if we define
{\mathcal{R}}^{(3)}(X,X_{1})
as the integrand,

R^{(3)}(X_{1})=\oint\frac{dX}{2\pi i\,X}\,{\mathcal{R}}^{(3)}(X,X_{1})\;,

(172)

of the integral expression (
94
) for the R-matrix
R^{(3)}
, we obtain
37
37

37

We stress that
\Phi_{3,3}^{*}(X)
is
not
an intertwiner, as explained in section
3.2.2
.

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf N})}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}\text{-}\text{NS5}_{0,0,1}}=\left\langle\oint\frac{dX}{2\pi i\,X}\,{\mathcal{R}}^{(3)}(X,X_{1})\,(\Phi_{3,3}^{*}(X)\otimes 1)\right\rangle\;.

(173)

The bracket notation labels the vacuum

\left\langle\ldots\right\rangle=[u]_{0}^{(3)*}\otimes\langle v_{1}\,q_{2}^{-1},\varnothing|\otimes\langle v_{2},\varnothing|\;\;\ldots\;\;|v_{1},\varnothing\rangle\otimes|v_{2},\varnothing\rangle\;.

(174)

Remark

In an S-dual frame, our D3
0,0,1
-NS5
0,0,1
configuration turns into a D3
0,0,1
-D5
0,0,1
configuration, with 8 Dirichlet-Neumann directions. This is T-dual to the well-studied D0-D8 system with (0,8) supersymmetry, where the massless open string modes are known to be chiral fermions
[
143
,
144
,
145
,
109
,
146
,
63
]
. The index for this intersection would therefore count degrees of freedom found in
\mathcal{N}=2
Fermi multiplets, instead of our chiral multiplets. It would be interesting to generalize this observation to more general brane configurations, and systematically analyze the behavior of the 3d/1d half-index under S-duality, in the spirit of
[
147
]
.

4.3
Index as a sum and Dirichlet boundary conditions

In the above, we imposed Neumann boundary conditions
{\bf N}
on the 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge multiplet, and showed that the resulting half-index was a vev of the Miura R-matrix (or a product of such R-matrices in general), presented as a contour integral. In the present section, we will instead impose Dirichlet boundary conditions
{\bf D}
on the gauge multiplet, and show that the half-index is again the same vev, but presented as a sum over monopole charges.

First, recall that in the Neumann case, the gauge symmetry was unbroken at the boundary, which is why the half-index took the form of an integral, projecting to gauge-invariant operators on the boundary of the 3-manifold.
In contrast, the Dirichlet boundary condition sets
A_{\mu|\partial}=0
for the gauge field, which implies the local gauge symmetry on the boundary is broken to a global symmetry
G_{|\partial}=U(1)_{\partial}
. We introduce a fugacity
u\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
and a generator
\mathfrak{t}_{\partial}
for this
U(1)_{\partial}
symmetry. Explicitly, the index now reads

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf D})}={\rm Tr}\left[(-1)^{F}\,{q_{3}}^{J_{3}+\frac{V}{2}}\;{q_{2}}^{\frac{V-A}{2}}\;u^{\mathfrak{t}_{\partial}}\;X^{\mathfrak{t}_{H}}\;v^{\mathfrak{t}_{C}}\right]\;\;.

(175)

In fact, we will seek to break
U(1)_{\partial}
entirely, as follows: we will generate a flow along the boundary to a new boundary condition by giving one of the 3d chiral fields a vev
\mathfrak{c}\neq 0
.
At the level of the index, the weight of the corresponding field must be set to 1 to preserve supersymmetry. In the terminology of
[
32
]
, we say that the chiral field is given deformed Dirichlet boundary condition
\text{D}_{\mathfrak{c}}
.

To see how this works in practice, consider again the non-transverse brane configuration of section
4.2.1
, with two NS5
1,0,0
branes of the same type and a semi-infinite D3
0,0,1
brane ending on one of them while intersecting the other. Recall that the supersymmetry preserved by this configuration is 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
, and that the low energy effective theory is a
U(1)
gauge theory with a single hypermultiplet. Imposing the Dirichlet boundary conditions on the
\mathcal{N}=4
vector multiplet, the index contribution is

\frac{\left(q_{3}\,q_{2}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}{\left(q_{3}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}\;.

(176)

The denominator is the contribution of the
\mathcal{N}=2
vector multiplet with boundary conditions
{\bf D}
. The gauge symmetry is broken along the boundary, but there is a residual gauge symmetry orthogonal to it, and the gauge-invariant operators there (and their
n
-th derivatives) generate the
\overline{Q}_{+}
-cohomology.
38
38

38

In detail, the 3d gauge-invariant linear multiplet decomposes on the boundary as a
(0,2)
gauge multiplet, whose top component is the gaugino, and a
(0,2)
chiral multiplet, whose top component is
\sigma+i\,A_{\perp}
, where
\sigma
is a real scalar and
A_{\perp}
is the gauge field component perpendicular to the boundary. The index with boundary condition
{\bf N}
counts the contributions of the gauge-invariant operators built out of the gaugino, while the index with boundary condition
{\bf D}
counts the contributions of gauge-invariant operators built out of
\sigma+i\,A_{\perp}
.
They contribute to the index with charge

(\text{vector},{\bf D}):\;\;\;\left(\text{adj},n+1,0,0\right)\;\;\text{under}\;\;U(1)_{\partial}\times U(1)_{J_{3}}\times U(1)_{R}\times U(1)_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\;.

(177)

The numerator is the contribution of the
\mathcal{N}=2
adjoint chiral multiplet with boundary conditions D: the gauginos (and their
n
-th derivatives) are counted in
\overline{Q}_{+}
-cohomology with charge

(\text{adj chiral},{\bf D}):\;\;\;\left(\text{adj},n+\dfrac{1}{2},1,1\right)\;\;\text{under}\;\;U(1)_{\partial}\times U(1)_{J_{3}}\times U(1)_{R}\times U(1)_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\;.

(178)

The
\mathcal{N}=4
hypermultiplet decomposes into a pair of
\mathcal{N}=2
chiral multiplets
(\Phi^{+},\Phi^{-})
. Here, we decide to impose Dirichlet boundary conditions for both
\Phi^{+}
and
\Phi^{-}
, with index contribution

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{q_{3}/q_{2}}\;X_{1}/u\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\,\left(\sqrt{q_{3}/q_{2}}\;u/X_{1}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\;.

(179)

We will justify this choice below. The first factor is the contribution of
\Phi^{+}
with boundary conditions D: boundary fermions (and their
n
-th derivatives) are counted in
\overline{Q}_{+}
-cohomology with charge

(\text{bif chiral},\text{D}):\;\;\;\left(-1,1,n+\frac{1}{2},0,\frac{-1}{2}\right)\;\;\text{under}\;\;U(1)_{\partial}\times U(1)_{X_{1}}\times U(1)_{J_{3}}\times U(1)_{R}\times U(1)_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}.

(180)

The second factor is the contribution of
\Phi^{-}
with boundary conditions D: boundary fermions (and their
n
-th derivatives) are counted in
\overline{Q}_{+}
-cohomology with charge

(\overline{\text{bif}}\;\text{chiral},\text{D}):\;\;\;\left(1,-1,n+\frac{1}{2},0,\frac{-1}{2}\right)\;\;\text{under}\;\;U(1)_{\partial}\times U(1)_{X_{1}}\times U(1)_{J_{3}}\times U(1)_{R}\times U(1)_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}.

(181)

Crucially, the contribution (
179
) receives non-perturbative corrections at the boundary coming from BPS monopole operators. Given a 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
theory, a monopole operator in the bulk is a disorder operator whose profile is a singular solution to

F=\star D\sigma\,,\qquad\;D\star\sigma=0\;,

(182)

where
\sigma
is the real scalar of footnote
38
, and
F
is the field strength of the gauge field. A monopole operator at the origin will be surrounded by a hemisphere
D^{2}
, with boundary
\partial(D^{2})=S^{1}_{D^{2}}
. Because the scalar
\sigma
and field strength component
F_{z\bot}
and are left unconstrained at the boundary (
z
is a holomorphic coordinate on
D^{2}
), this monopole is compatible with the Dirichlet boundary condition
{\bf D}
, which we impose to trivialize the
U(1)
-bundle at the boundary
S^{1}_{D^{2}}
. After this trivialization, the Dirac quantization condition takes the form:

\frac{1}{2\pi}\oint_{D^{2}}F+\frac{1}{2\pi}\oint_{S^{1}_{D^{2}}}A=m\in\mathbb{Z}\;.

(183)

But the boundary condition
A_{\mu|\partial}=0
implies
\oint_{S^{1}_{D^{2}}}A=0
, so the monopole charge
m
is nothing but the quantized flux through the hemisphere:
39
39

39

More generally, for a non-abelian gauge group
G
, we embed the Dirac monopole solution into
G
, meaning
m
is an element of the cocharacter lattice
m\in\Lambda_{cochar}=\text{Hom}(U(1),T_{G})
, the space of maps to the maximal torus of
G
. In our abelian setup,
\text{Hom}(U(1),U(1))=\mathbb{Z}
and
m
is just an integer.

\frac{1}{2\pi}\oint_{D^{2}}F=m\in\mathbb{Z}\;.

(184)

In the end, the half-index takes the form of a sum over all magnetic fluxes
m
through the hemisphere
D^{2}
. Moreover, all electrically charged states acquire spin, meaning the fugacity
u
for the boundary symmetry
U(1)_{\partial}
is shifted everywhere as
u\rightarrow q_{3}^{m}\,u

[
32
]
:

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf D},\text{D},\text{D})}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}-\text{NS5}_{1,0,0}}=\frac{\left(q_{3}\,q_{2}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}{\left(q_{3}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\;\left(q_{3}^{-m}\sqrt{q_{3}/q_{2}}\;X_{1}/u\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\,\left(q_{3}^{m}\sqrt{q_{3}/q_{2}}\;u/X_{1}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\;\ldots\;.

(185)

The
``\ldots"
stands for the 1-loop contributions of boundary ’t Hooft anomalies, which we will come back to shortly.

In order to implement the deformed Dirichlet boundary condition
\text{D}_{\mathfrak{c}}
, we generate an RG flow along the boundary to a new boundary condition, where a chiral field acquires a vev
\mathfrak{c}\neq 0
in the supersymmetric vacuum. If this chiral field has charge
(1,-1,1)
under
U(1)_{V}\times U(1)_{\partial}\times U(1)_{X_{1}}
, then the linear combinations
V+\mathfrak{t}_{\partial}\equiv V^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{t}_{X_{1}}+\,\mathfrak{t}_{\partial}\equiv\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{X_{1}}
are preserved along this flow; the generators of the new vector R-symmetry and flavor symmetry on the boundary are denoted by
V^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}_{X_{1}}
in the index (
175
), respectively. Concretely, the weight of the corresponding chiral field is set to 1, which translates to a constraint
u^{-1}\,q_{3}^{1/2}\,q_{2}^{1/2}\,X_{1}\,=1
on
u
. Put differently, we simply substitute
u=q_{3}^{1/2}\,q_{2}^{1/2}\,X_{1}
in the entire summand (
185
). We obtain

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf D},\text{D},\text{D}_{\mathfrak{c}})}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}-\text{NS5}_{1,0,0}}=\frac{\left(q_{3}\,q_{2}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}{\left(q_{3}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}\sum^{\infty}_{m=0}\;\left(q_{3}^{-m}q^{-1}_{2}\;\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\,\left(q_{3}^{1+m}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\;\ldots\;,

(186)

Note the summation index
m
only runs over the positive integers now, because of the factor
\left(q_{3}^{1+m}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}
.

Remark

An alternative way to motivate the deformed Dirichlet boundary condition is as follows: consider again the hypermultiplet contribution to the index (
179
), where we rescale
u
by the fugacity
q^{1/2}_{3}
out of convenience:

\left(q_{3}^{-m}\sqrt{1/q_{2}}\;X_{1}/u\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\,\left(q_{3}^{1+m}\sqrt{1/q_{2}}\;u/X_{1}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\;.

(187)

In the
U(1)
3d gauge theory, the flavor symmetries acts by Hamiltonian isometries on the complex scalars of the chiral multiplets
(\Phi^{+},\Phi^{-})
as

U(1)_{X_{1}}:\;(\phi^{+},\phi^{-})\;\rightarrow(e^{+i\theta}\phi^{+},e^{-i\theta}\phi^{-})\;,\qquad U(1)_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}:\;(\phi^{+},\phi^{-})\;\rightarrow(e^{+i\theta/2}\phi^{+},e^{+i\theta/2}\phi^{-})\;,

(188)

and the gauge symmetry acts as

U(1):\;(\phi^{+},\phi^{-})\;\rightarrow(e^{-i\theta}\phi^{+},e^{+i\theta}\phi^{-})\;.

(189)

Correspondingly, the complex and real superpotentials are

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}=\varphi\,\phi^{+}\,\phi^{-}

\displaystyle W=\left(-\sigma+x_{1}+\frac{{\varepsilon}_{2}}{2}\right)|\phi^{+}|^{2}+\left(\sigma-x_{1}+\frac{{\varepsilon}_{2}}{2}\right)|\phi^{-}|^{2}+\xi\,\sigma\;,

(190)

where
\sigma
and
\phi
are the real and complex scalars of the
\mathcal{N}=4
vector multiplet, while
\xi
and
m
are the real FI parameter and hypermultiplet mass, respectively. The (classical) vacua are critical points of these superpotentials, which are solutions of the moment map equations

\displaystyle\left(|\phi^{+}|^{2}-|\phi^{-}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\xi\;,

\displaystyle\phi^{+}\,\phi^{-}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(191)

\displaystyle(-\sigma+x_{1}+\frac{{\varepsilon}_{2}}{2})\,\phi^{+}

\displaystyle=0\;,

\displaystyle\varphi\,\phi^{+}

\displaystyle=0\;,

(192)

\displaystyle(+\sigma-x_{1}+\frac{{\varepsilon}_{2}}{2})\,\phi^{-}

\displaystyle=0\;,

\displaystyle\varphi\,\phi^{-}

\displaystyle=0\;.

(193)

We are interested in the Higgs vacuum where either
\phi^{+}
or
\phi^{-}
acquires a nonzero vev. When
\xi>0
, the vev is
|\phi^{+}|^{2}=\xi
(with
\phi^{-}=0=\varphi
), and
-\sigma+x_{1}+\frac{{\varepsilon}_{2}}{2}=0
. When
\xi<0
, the vev is
|\phi^{-}|^{2}=-\xi
, with
\sigma-x_{1}+\frac{{\varepsilon}_{2}}{2}=0
.
We choose to work with
\xi>0
throughout
40
40

40

With this convention, the index has a positive radius of convergence as a power series in the complexified FI parameter
v=e^{-R\left(\xi+i\,A^{\theta}_{\xi}\right)}
around 0.
, and therefore set
\sigma=x_{1}+\frac{{\varepsilon}_{2}}{2}
.

Because the 3d theory is compactified on
S^{1}(R)
, we likewise impose
A^{\theta}_{\sigma}=A^{\theta}_{x_{1}}+\frac{A^{\theta}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}}{2}
for the holonomies.
In the end, substituting
u=q^{1/2}_{2}\,X_{1}
for the complexified variables in (
187
), we recover the summand of the index (
194
).

Imposing Dirichlet boundary conditions on both
\mathcal{N}=2
chiral multiplets of the hypermultiplet has introduced a boundary anomaly at 1-loop, meaning there is now an
effective
Chern-Simons coupling at level 1. Correspondingly, a bare monopole operator of charge
m
will induce a nontrivial electric charge
m
and spin
\frac{1}{2}m^{2}
in the theory, meaning an insertion
u^{m}\,q^{\frac{m^{2}}{2}}_{3}
inside the summand of the index. After performing the deformed Dirichlet substitution
u=q_{3}^{1/2}\,q_{2}^{1/2}\,X_{1}
, and redefining the topological fugacity
v\rightarrow-v\,X^{-1}_{1}
, we find:

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf D},\text{D},\text{D}_{\mathfrak{c}})}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}-\text{NS5}_{1,0,0}}=\frac{\left(q_{3}\,q_{2}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}{\left(q_{3}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}\sum^{\infty}_{m=0}(-1)^{m}\,q^{m}_{2}\,q^{\frac{m(m+1)}{2}}_{3}\;v^{m}\;\left(q_{3}^{-m}q^{-1}_{2}\;\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\,\left(q_{3}^{1+m}\,;q_{3}\right)_{\infty}\;.

(194)

Up to normalization by perturbative contributions outside the summand, we recognize the vev of the R-matrix (
88
) (for
c=1
), composed with a dual intertwiner:

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf D},\text{D},\text{D}_{\mathfrak{c}})}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1}-\text{NS5}_{1,0,0}}=\left\langle{R}^{(1)}\,(\Phi_{3,1}^{*}\otimes 1)\right\rangle\;.

(195)

Above,
\Phi^{*}_{3,1}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\Phi^{*}_{3,1}(u\,q^{i}_{3})\,[u]_{i}^{(3)}
is understood as an expansion in the basis elements
[u]_{i}^{(3)}
. The bracket once again denotes the vacuum (
156
), so the sum over
i\in\mathbb{Z}
reduces to a single term, by orthonormality of the basis.

In fact, up to the perturbative normalization, the above Dirichlet index coincides with the residue sum of the Neumann index (
155
), where the contour encloses “
\infty
” and the infinite number of poles at

X=q^{-m}_{3}\,X_{1}\;,\qquad\;\;\;m=0,1,2,\ldots

(196)

For instance, the residues of the mixed topological-gauge anomaly contribution are

e^{-\frac{\ln(v)\ln(q^{-m}_{3}\,X_{1}/X_{1})}{\ln(q_{3})}}=v^{m}\;,

(197)

which we recognize as the correct factor in the summand of the Dirichlet index. See also section 3.4.2 of
[
32
]
.

4.4
Arbitrary D3-NS5 intersections

In summary, for all configurations considered so far, a D3
0,0,1
brane ends on a NS5
1,0,0
brane on one end, and intersects another NS5-brane in a supersymmetric way:

•

A non-transverse D3
0,0,1
-NS5
1,0,0
intersection, preserving 3d
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetry.

•

A non-transverse D3
0,0,1
-NS5
0,1,0
intersection, preserving 3d
\mathcal{N}=2
supersymmetry.

•

A transverse D3
0,0,1
-NS5
0,0,1
intersection, preserving 1d
\mathcal{N}=4
supersymmetry.

These building blocks can be combined to study more general configurations of D3- and NS5-branes. Namely, consider again a semi-infinite D3
0,0,1
brane ending on a NS5
1,0,0
brane on one end, and intersecting
L
NS5
1,0,0
,
M
NS5
0,1,0
, and
N
NS5
0,0,1
branes on the other end. The low energy effective theory is now an abelian quiver gauge theory
[
26
]
, of 3d gauge group
U(1)^{L+M+N}
, with
L+M
3d hypermultiplets and
N
1d hypermultiplets (coupled via line defects).

It follows that if we give boundary conditions
{\bf N}
for the vector multiplets, and polarization “
+
” to all
L+M
hypermultiplets, the 3d/1d half index of this configuration is the integral

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf N},+,\ldots,+)}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1},\text{NS5}_{L,M,N}}=\oint\prod_{I=1}^{L+M+N}\frac{dX^{(I)}}{2\pi iX^{(I)}}e^{-\sum_{I\in c^{-1}\{1,2\}}\frac{\log q_{\bar{c}_{I}}}{\log q_{3}}\log\frac{X^{(I)}}{X^{(I+1)}}}\;e^{-\sum_{I=1}^{L+M+N}\frac{\log X^{(I)}}{\log q_{3}}\log\frac{v_{I}}{v_{I+1}}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{I\in c^{-1}\{1,2\}}\frac{\left(q_{\bar{c}_{I}}^{-1}\frac{X^{(I)}}{X^{(I+1)}};q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}{\left(\frac{X^{(I)}}{X^{(I+1)}};q_{3}\right)_{\infty}}\prod_{I\in c^{-1}(3)}\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{X^{(I+1)}}{X^{(I)}}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{3}^{-1}X^{(I+1)}}{X^{(I)}}\right)}\;,\end{split}

(198)

up to overall normalization by perturbative contributions outside the integral. We used the notation
(|c^{-1}(1)|,|c^{-1}(2)|,|c^{-1}(3)|)=(L,M,N)
, and declare
X^{(L+M+N+1)}\equiv X_{1}
, as well as
v_{L+M+N+1}=v_{X_{1}}
.

We recognize the vev of a product of R-matrices (
111
), or equivalently the vev of the Miura transformation:

{\mathcal{Z}}^{({\bf N},+,\ldots,+)}_{\text{D3}_{0,0,1},\text{NS5}_{L,M,N}}=\left\langle\oint\prod_{I=1}^{L+M+N}\frac{dX^{(I)}}{2\pi iX^{(I)}}\,{\mathcal{R}}^{(c_{L+M+N})}_{L+M+N}\ldots\,{\mathcal{R}}^{(c_{2})}_{2}\,{\mathcal{R}}^{(c_{1})}_{1}\,(\Phi_{3,1}^{*}(X^{(0)})\otimes 1)\right\rangle\;.

(199)

In the bracket
\left\langle\ldots\right\rangle
for the vev, the in-state on the right stands for

|v_{0},\varnothing\rangle\otimes|v_{1},\varnothing\rangle\otimes|v_{2},\varnothing\rangle\otimes\ldots\otimes|v_{L+M+N},\varnothing\rangle\;.

(200)

and the out-state on the left stands for

{[u]^{(3)*}_{0}}\otimes\langle v_{0}\,q_{2}^{-1},\varnothing|\otimes\langle v_{1},\varnothing|\otimes\langle v_{2},\varnothing|\otimes\ldots\otimes\langle v_{L+M+N},\varnothing|\;.

(201)

To close this section, recall that the brane configurations we studied all had in common a semi-infinite D3
0,0,1
brane ending on a NS5
1,0,0
brane. Choosing different orientations for this D3-brane and NS5-brane is easily achieved by simply permuting the parameters
q_{1}
,
q_{2}
and
q_{3}
in the index we have already computed. We can also relax the semi-infinite requirement for the D3-brane: making the brane infinite simply increases the number of intersections with NS5-branes by 1, which results in one additional hypermultiplet in the gauge theory, and one additional R-matrix insertion in the index. In that way, any arbitrary configuration of D3-branes intersecting NS5-branes in
\Omega
-deformed type IIB can be studied via our R-matrix formalism.

5
qq
-characters from M2-branes on web of M5-branes

So far, we have restricted our discussion to the M5-branes with the holomorphic part of the support being
C^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
. In this section, we introduce the M5-branes supported on more general holomorphic curves
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
. We explain the gauge-invariant non-transverse intersections of M5-branes provide intertwiners between the
q
-boson representations. We elucidate the M2-branes, which transversally intersect the M5-brane web, give rise to the
qq
-characters of the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
effective gauge theory on the worldvolume of the M5-brane web.

5.1
Non-transverse intersections of M5-branes

We discuss the M5-branes whose holomorphic part of the support is generic holomorphic curve
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
. We find
q
-boson representations associated to them, and study their intersections in terms of the intertwiners of the
q
-boson representations.

5.1.1
M5-branes on holomorphic curves and
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
-dual
q
-bosons

In section
2.2.1
, we have argued that the worldvolume theory on single M5-brane, supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
, gets localized to a free
q
-boson on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
. For the single M5-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
, the only modification is the holomorphic support, so that the algebra of local operators should again be the
q
-boson algebra supported on the holomorphic curve
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
.

In fact, there exists a symmetry that exchanges the M5-branes supported on different holomorphic curves
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
with one another: the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
symmetry of reparametrization of the holomorphic surface
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
. Specifically, let us reparametrize this complex two-dimensional space by
X^{\prime}=X^{a}Z^{b}
and
Z^{\prime}=X^{c}Z^{d}
. If
ad-bc=1
, the 3-form background of the twisted M-theory is invariant under the reparametrization since
\frac{d\bar{X}^{\prime}}{\bar{X}^{\prime}}\wedge\frac{d\bar{Z}^{\prime}}{\bar{Z}^{\prime}}=\frac{d\bar{X}}{\bar{X}}\wedge\frac{d\bar{Z}}{\bar{Z}}
. This
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
symmetry maps the holomorphic curve
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
to
C^{\mathbf{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}}
, where

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{p^{\prime}}\\
\mathbf{q^{\prime}}\end{pmatrix}=g\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{p}\\
\mathbf{q}\end{pmatrix},\qquad g=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in SL(2,\mathbb{Z}).

(202)

Thus, the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
maps the M5-branes supported on those holomorphic curves accordingly. After the dimensional reduction and T-dualization, this precisely matches with the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
symmetry of the IIB theory, which maps the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebrane to the
\mathbf{(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})}
-fivebrane.

The
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
symmetry of the twisted M-theory descends to the 5d non-commutative
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
Chern-Simons theory on
\mathbb{R}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
. Thus, the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
symmetry should be reflected on the universal associative algebra as its automorphism group. Indeed, the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
is known to possess an
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
automorphism group
[
148
]
. Then, the
q
-boson representations associated to the M5-branes supported on different holomorphic curves should be mapped to one another under the action of this
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
automorphism group.

The
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
group is generated by two elements,

\displaystyle T=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad S=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}.

(203)

Let us first consider the subgroup generated by
T
. When acted on
C^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
, the subgroup generates all the holomorphic curves of the form
C^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}
,
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
. The
q
-boson representations associated to the M5-brane on these curves can thus be obtained by applying the
T
-transformations to the
q
-boson representation
\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}
(
58
),

\displaystyle\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}\hookrightarrow\text{End}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}_{c}(v)).

(204)

This turns out to be a slight modification of the
q
-boson representation
\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}
(
58
), given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E(X)\mapsto-e^{a_{0}^{(c)}\frac{\log q_{c+1}\log q_{c-1}}{\log q_{c}}}\frac{1-q_{c}}{\kappa_{1}}\left(\frac{q_{c}^{1/2}}{X}\right)^{\mathbf{n}}\eta_{c}(X),\\
&F(X)\mapsto e^{-a_{0}^{(c)}\frac{\log q_{c+1}\log q_{c-1}}{\log q_{c}}}\frac{1-q_{c}^{-1}}{\kappa_{1}}\left(\frac{X}{q_{c}^{1/2}}\right)^{\mathbf{n}}\xi_{c}(X),\\
&K^{\pm}(X)\mapsto q_{c}^{\mp\mathbf{n}/2}\varphi^{\pm}_{c}(X),\end{split}

(205)

Note
(C,C^{\perp})\mapsto\left(q_{c}^{1/2},q_{c}^{\mathbf{n}/2}\right)
, in particular.

Meanwhile, the
q
-boson representation associated to the M5-brane wrapping
C^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
can be understood in a different way. Recall that one way of obtaining the
q
-boson representation is reducing the twisted M-theory along the circle of
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}\simeq\mathbb{R}_{z}\times S^{1}
, after which this M5-brane becomes a D4-brane. The
q
-boson representation is viewed as the algebra of local operators on the line
\mathbb{R}_{z}
acting on the equivariant K-theory of the moduli space of
U(1)
instantons. Thus, the
q
-boson representation arising in this case is the one where the representation constructed in
[
127
,
115
]
directly applies. Namely, we have

\displaystyle\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}}:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}\hookrightarrow\text{End}(\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}_{c}(a)).

(206)

Let us recall the basis states of the Fock representation
\mathcal{F}_{c}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}(a)=\bigoplus_{\{\lambda\}}\mathbb{C}|a,\lambda\rangle^{(c)}
are enumerated by a single Young diagram
\lambda
. The action of the generating currents on the state
|a,\lambda\rangle^{(c)}
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E(X)|a,\lambda\rangle^{(c)}=\frac{1-q_{c}}{\kappa_{1}}\sum_{\Box\in\partial_{+}\lambda}\delta(\chi_{\Box}/X)\underset{X=\chi_{\Box}}{\text{Res}}{X^{-1}\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda}^{(c)}(X,a)^{-1}}|a,\lambda+\Box\rangle^{(c)},\\
&F(X)|a,\lambda\rangle^{(c)}=-\frac{1-q_{c}^{-1}}{\kappa_{1}}q_{c}^{-1/2}\sum_{\Box\in\partial_{-}\lambda}\delta(\chi_{\Box}/X)\underset{X=\chi_{\Box}}{\text{Res}}X^{-1}\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda}^{(c)}(Xq_{c}^{-1},a)|a,\lambda-\Box\rangle^{(c)},\\
&K^{\pm}(X)|a,\lambda\rangle^{(c)}=q_{c}^{-1/2}\left(\frac{\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda}^{(c)}(Xq_{c}^{-1},a)}{\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda}^{(c)}(X,a)}\right)_{\pm}|a,\lambda\rangle^{(c)},\qquad C|a,\lambda\rangle^{(c)}=|a,\lambda\rangle^{(c)},\end{split}

(207)

where we defined
\chi_{\Box_{(i,j)}}=aq_{c+1}^{i-1}q_{c-1}^{j-1}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{(c)}_{\lambda}(X,a)=\left(1-\frac{a}{X}\right)\prod_{\Box\in\lambda}\frac{\left(1-\frac{q_{c+1}\chi_{\Box}}{X}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c-1}\chi_{\Box}}{X}\right)}{\left(1-\frac{\chi_{\Box}}{X}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c}^{-1}\chi_{\Box}}{X}\right)}=\frac{\prod_{\Box\in\partial_{+}\lambda}\left(1-\frac{\chi_{\Box}}{X}\right)}{\prod_{\Box\in\partial_{-}\lambda}\left(1-\frac{q_{c}^{-1}\chi_{\Box}}{X}\right)}.

(208)

Here,
\partial_{+}\lambda
(resp.
\partial_{-}\lambda
) is the set of boxes that can be added to (resp. removed from) the Young diagram
\lambda
. Also,
f(X)_{+}
(resp.
f(X)_{-}
) is the expansion of
f(X)
in the domain
|X|>\!\!>1
(resp.
|X|<\!\!<1
). Note that
(C,C^{\perp})\mapsto(1,q_{c}^{1/2})
, in particular.
41
41

41

In some literature, (
204
) is referred to as the
horizontal
Fock representation, while (
206
) is referred to as the
vertical
Fock representation. Since the terminology may cause confusion when compared to the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebrane web, we will avoid using this terminology.

It was indeed shown that the
q
-boson representations
\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}
and
\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}}
are exchanged with each other under the action of the
S
-transformation, also known as the Miki automorphism
[
148
]
, of the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))

[
149
]
. This is consistent with the corresponding reparametrization swapping the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
-plane and the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
-plane.

5.1.2
q
-boson intertwiners for non-transverse M5-brane intersections

The M5-branes supported on different holomorphic curves
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
can make transverse or non-transverse intersections. Since it is difficult to visualize this web of M5-branes directly, it is helpful to pass to the IIB dual frame where the M5-brane supported on the curve
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
becomes the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebrane (see section
1.3
). Recall that
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebranes are supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c+1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{c-1}}\times l^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}\times S^{1}
, where
l^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}=\{\mathbf{q}x-\mathbf{p}z=\text{const}\}
is a line on the two-dimensional plane
\mathbb{R}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{z}
.

When the two fivebranes only share one of the three topological planes in their supports (namely,
c_{1}\neq c_{2}
), the two fivebranes cannot end on each other as they pass through along the mutually transverse directions. By a similar argument for the transverse M2-M5 intersection, the local operator at such a
transverse
intersection of M5-branes would provide the R-matrix between the associated
q
-boson representations; explicitly,
R_{\text{M5}_{c_{1}}^{\mathbf{(p_{1},q_{1})}},\text{M5}_{c_{2}}^{\mathbf{(p_{2},q_{2})}}}=(\rho_{\text{M5}_{c_{1}}^{\mathbf{(p_{1},q_{1})}}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M5}_{c_{2}}^{\mathbf{(p_{2},q_{2})}}})\mathcal{R}
. We will not discuss this transverse M5-brane intersection further in this work.

When the two fivebranes share two of the three topological planes (namely,
c_{1}=c_{2}
), they can make an intersection at a point on
\mathbb{R}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{z}
if the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
charge is conserved
[
150
]
. From now on, we will focus on non-transverse M5-brane intersections. Let us fix
c=3
so that all the fivebranes have
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}
as the topological part of their supports. By consecutively joining their intersections, we can form
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-web of fivebranes. The basic building blocks for the web are the trivalent intersections depicted in Figure
15
.

\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,n+1)}}
\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}
\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}
\Psi^{\mathbf{(n)}}
\mathbb{R}_{x}
\mathbb{R}_{z}

(a)

\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,n+1)}}
\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}
\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}
\Psi^{*\mathbf{(n)}}

(b)

Figure 15
:
Basic non-transverse intersections of fivebranes and associated intertwiners of
q
-boson representations. We call (a) the first kind; (b) the second kind.

The local operators on the fivebranes are ordered in the increasing
\mathbb{R}_{x}
-direction, which is equivalent to the radial-ordering on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
in the M-theory setting. These local operators can act on the Hilbert space attached to each fivebrane, formed as the Fock space
\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}_{3}(v)
for the associated
q
-boson representation
\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
. In this sense, the local operators supported at the first kind of the trivalent intersection provides a map,

\displaystyle\Psi^{\mathbf{(n)}}(v,v^{\prime}):\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}_{3}(v^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}_{3}(v)\to\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,n+1)}}_{3}(-vv^{\prime}),

(209)

between the Fock spaces attached to the three fivebranes. By an argument similar to that of the non-transverse M2-M5 intersection (see section
3.2.2
), we expect that the gauge-invariance condition for this intersection at full quantum level reads

\displaystyle\rho_{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,n+1)}}}(g)\Psi^{\mathbf{(n)}}=\Psi^{\mathbf{(n)}}\Delta_{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}},\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}}(g).

(210)

In other words, the local operator at the trivalent intersection provides an intertwiner between the
q
-boson representations of the quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
. In fact, this constraint can be solved to uniquely determine the intertwiner, up to an overall scaling
[
25
]
. We will not review the derivation of this intertwiner here, but only present its expression in appendix
E
.

Similarly, let us consider the local operator supported at the second kind of the trivalent intersection. The local operator gives a map,

\displaystyle\Psi^{*\mathbf{(n)}}(v,v^{\prime}):\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,n+1)}}_{3}(-vv^{\prime})\to\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}_{3}(v^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}_{3}(v),

(211)

between the Fock spaces attached to the three M5-branes. The gauge-invariance condition at the quantum level is expected to be

\displaystyle\Delta^{\text{op}}_{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}},\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,n)}}}(g)\Psi^{*\mathbf{(n)}}=\Psi^{*\mathbf{(n)}}\rho_{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,n+1)}}}(g)

(212)

The constraint exactly determines the intertwiner up to an overall scaling
[
25
]
. We give the expression for this intertwiner in appendix
E
.

5.2
qq
-characters from Miura operators on web of
q
-bosons

Having introduced the
q
-boson representations associated to M5-branes supported on holomorphic curves
C^{\mathbf{(p,q)}}
and the intertwiners associated to their non-transverse intersections, now we can compose these M5-brane intersections to form a brane web engineering 5-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=1
gauge theories compactified on a circle. Moreover, we can introduce an M2-brane which is transverse to the M5-branes. We have studied such a transverse M2-M5 intersection giving rise to the R-matrix between the
q
-bosons and the quantum torus algebra (namely, a product of Miura operators). Here, we will show that the insertion of the R-matrix in the web of
q
-bosons establishes the
qq-characters

[
43
]
.

We will only exemplify the simplest case: the 5-dimensional
\mathcal{N}=1
pure
SU(2)
gauge theory. In the IIB dual frame, the associated brane web is given as in Figure
16
. Note, however, that the principle directly generalizes to arbitrary M5-brane web. The
qq
-characters of the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
effective gauge theory on the worldvolume of the M5-brane web would arise by the additional insertion of transverse M2-branes. We emphasize that the correspondence between the transverse M2-M5 intersections and the
qq
-characters is anticipated due to a string duality. Specifically, the original crossed D-brane configuration for the
qq
-characters
[
43
,
44
]
transforms into the M2-branes transversally intersecting the M5-brane web through T-duality and uplift to M-theory (see section
1.3
). The example we establish below is confirmation of this expected correspondence.

\otimes
NS5
D5
D3
\mathbf{(1,1)}
\mathbf{(1,-1)}
\mathbb{R}_{x}
\mathbb{R}_{z}
\mathbb{R}_{t}

(a)

\otimes
\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}
\text{M2}_{0,0,1}
\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,1)}}
\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,-1)}}
\Psi^{\mathbf{(0)}}
\Psi^{*\mathbf{(-1)}}
\Psi^{*\mathbf{(0)}}
\Psi^{\mathbf{(-1)}}

(b)

Figure 16
:
(a)
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebrane web of IIB theory engineering the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
pure
SU(2)
gauge theory. The
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebranes are supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\{\text{line on the figure}\}\times S^{1}
. The
qq
-character as a codimension-4 defect of the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
gauge theory is introduced by a transverse D3-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}\times S^{1}
; (b) Web of
q
-bosons and intertwiners. At the transverse intersection with the D3-brane, the R-matrix between the
q
-bosons and the quantum torus algebra
\text{M2}_{0,0,1}
is assigned.

In our example, there are four trivalent intersections of the fivebranes, each of which is assigned with an intertwiner of the associated
q
-boson representations. Let us break the
\mathbf{(p,q)}
-fivebrane web into the lower half and the upper half, splitting the intertwiners into two groups. For the lower half of the web, we can compose the two intertwiners through the space
\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}_{3}
of states of the intermediate M5-brane as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sigma\left(\Psi^{\mathbf{(-1)}}(v_{2},a_{1})\otimes\Psi^{*\mathbf{(0)}}(v_{1}a_{2},a_{1})\right)\sigma\\
&\qquad:\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,1)}}_{3}(-v_{1}a_{1}a_{2})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,-1)}}(v_{2})\to\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{1}a_{2})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(-v_{2}a_{1}),\end{split}

(213)

where we swapped the ordering of the Fock spaces in the tensor product using the exchange operators. Similarly, for the upper half of the web we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sigma\left(\Psi^{\mathbf{(0)}}(-v_{2}a_{1},a_{2})\otimes\Psi^{*\mathbf{(-1)}}(-v_{1},a_{2})\right)\sigma\\
&\qquad:\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(v_{1}a_{2})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}(-v_{2}a_{1})\to\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,-1)}}(-v_{1})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,1)}}_{3}(v_{2}a_{1}a_{2}),\end{split}

(214)

Then, the web as a whole provides a
correlation function
of intertwiners,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{T}:=\sigma\left(\Psi^{\mathbf{(0)}}(-v_{2}a_{1},a_{2})\otimes\Psi^{*\mathbf{(-1)}}(-v_{1},a_{2})\right)\circ\left(\Psi^{\mathbf{(-1)}}(v_{2},a_{1})\otimes\Psi^{*\mathbf{(0)}}(v_{1}a_{2},a_{1})\right)\sigma\\
&\qquad:\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,1)}}_{3}(-v_{1}a_{1}a_{2})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,-1)}}(v_{2})\to\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,-1)}}(-v_{1})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,1)}}_{3}(v_{2}a_{1}a_{2}).\end{split}

(215)

We compute its vacuum expectation value in the
q
-boson representations by evaluating it at the respective vacua,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(a;\mathfrak{q}):=(\langle\varnothing|\otimes\langle\varnothing|)\mathcal{T}(|\varnothing\rangle\otimes|\varnothing\rangle).

(216)

By normal-ordering all the vertex operators in (
215
), we can explicitly compute this vacuum expectation value. The outcome is (see appendix
E
for notations)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{Z}(a;\mathfrak{q})=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{1-loop}}(a)\sum_{\{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\}}\mathfrak{q}^{|\lambda_{1}|+|\lambda_{2}|}\frac{1}{\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1,2}N_{\lambda_{\alpha}\lambda_{\beta}}(a_{\alpha}/a_{\beta};q_{1},q_{2})}\\
&\mathcal{Z}^{\text{1-loop}}(a)=\exp\left[-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{(a_{1}/a_{2})^{r}}{r}\frac{1+q_{3}^{-r}}{(1-q_{1}^{r})(1-q_{2}^{r})}\right]\end{split},

(217)

where we introduced
\mathfrak{q}=q_{3}^{-1}\frac{v_{2}}{v_{1}}
. This exactly reproduces the partition function of the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
pure
SU(2)
gauge theory on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times S^{1}
, with the complexified gauge coupling
\mathfrak{q}
and the Coulomb parameter
a=a_{1}/a_{2}
. Therefore, we establish the correspondence between the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
gauge theory partition function and the vacuum expectation value of the
web
of holomorphic surface defects in the 5d Chern-Simons theory, as anticipated by string duality (see section
1.3
). We stress that this reconstruction is not computationally new, but provides a physical explanation of the algebraic formulation obtained in
[
25
]
.

Now, let us introduce an M2-brane supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
on top of the M5-brane web. The resulting transverse M2-M5 intersection assigns the R-matrix
R_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{0,0,2}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}\in\text{M2}_{0,0,1}\otimes\text{M5}_{0,0,2}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
. Recalling that
{\text{M5}_{0,0,2}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}\hookrightarrow{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}\otimes{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}\simeq\text{End}(\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}})\otimes\text{End}(\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}})
, this R-matrix is inserted in the middle between the lower and the upper halves of the web, producing

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{X}&:=\left(\sigma(\Psi^{\mathbf{(0)}}\otimes\Psi^{*\mathbf{(-1)}})\sigma\right)R_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{0,0,2}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}\left(\sigma(\Psi^{\mathbf{(-1)}}\otimes\Psi^{*\mathbf{(0)}})\sigma\right)\\
&\in\text{M2}_{0,0,1}\otimes\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,1)}}_{3}(-v_{1}a_{1}a_{2})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,-1)}}(v_{2}),\mathcal{F}_{3}^{\mathbf{(1,-1)}}(-v_{1})\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(1,1)}}_{3}(v_{2},a_{1}a_{2})\right).\end{split}

(218)

We can also define its vacuum expectation value by evaluating it at the vacua of the
q
-bosons and the basis elements of
\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{X}(u)\right\rangle_{k}:=\left([uq_{3}^{-1}]_{i+k}^{*}\otimes\langle\varnothing|\otimes\langle\varnothing|\right)\mathcal{X}\left([u]_{i}\otimes|\varnothing\rangle\otimes|\varnothing\rangle\right).

(219)

Even though
i\in\mathbb{Z}
enumerating the states
[u]_{i}\in\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)
is left arbitrary, the non-trivial dependence is only on the
distance

k\in\mathbb{Z}
between the vector
[u]_{i}
and the dual vector
[uq_{3}^{-1}]_{i+k}^{*}
, not on the specific choice of
i
. Let us call the vacuum expectation value
\left\langle\mathcal{X}\right\rangle_{k}
the
k
-th fundamental
qq-character
.

We can explicitly evaluate the vacuum expectation values. As we studied in section
3.3.2
, the R-matrix is given by the product of Miura operators as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&R_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{0,0,2}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}=R_{2}^{(3)}R_{1}^{(3)}\\
&=\left[1-q_{3}^{-1}\left(e^{-a_{0}^{[2]}\frac{\log q_{1}\log q_{2}}{\log q_{3}}}\xi^{[2]}(X_{1})+e^{-a_{0}^{[1]}\frac{\log q_{1}\log q_{2}}{\log q_{3}}}\xi^{[1]}(q_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{1})\varphi^{+,[2]}(q_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{1})\right)Z_{1}\right.\\
&\quad\left.+q_{3}^{-2}e^{-(a_{0}^{[1]}+a_{0}^{[2]})\frac{\log q_{1}\log q_{2}}{\log q_{3}}}\xi^{[1]}(q_{3}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X_{1})\xi^{[2]}(X_{1})\varphi^{+,[2]}(q_{3}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X_{1})Z_{1}^{2}\right]q_{3}^{d|_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}}V^{[1]}(X_{1})V^{[2]}(q_{3}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X_{1}).\end{split}

(220)

Here the square bracket indicates which of the two
q
-bosons the operators are valued in. Plugging this expression into (
219
), it is apparent that
\langle\mathcal{X}\rangle_{k}\neq 0
only if
k=0,1,2
since the R-matrix is a degree-two polynomial in
Z_{1}
. Also note that the module parameter
u\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
for
\mathcal{V}_{3}(u)
gets shifted by
q_{3}^{-1}
due to
q_{3}^{d|_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}}
, as appearing in (
219
).

The
0
-th fundamental
qq
-character turns out to be exactly identical to the partition function
\mathcal{Z}
itself,
\left\langle\mathcal{X}\right\rangle_{0}=\mathcal{Z}
, since the terms emergent from normal-ordering
V^{[1]}(X_{1})
and
V^{[2]}(q_{3}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X_{1})
to the right cancel with each other. The second fundamental
qq
-character, though seemingly more complicated, also yields more or less trivial expression since the terms appearing from normal-ordering
\xi^{[1]}(q_{3}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X_{1})
and
\xi^{[2]}(X_{1})\varphi^{+,[2]}(q_{3}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X_{1})
also cancel with each other:
\left\langle\mathcal{X}\right\rangle_{2}=-q_{3}^{-2}v_{1}^{-1}v_{2}^{-1}a_{1}^{-1}a_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{Z}
.

Finally, let us compute the first fundamental
qq
-character, which is the only one that gives a non-trivial expression in our example.
42
42

42

To have more than one non-trivial fundamental
qq
-characters, we need to consider the M5-brane web which engineers quiver gauge theories. If the quiver has
n
gauge nodes, the transverse M2-brane assigns the R-matrix which is a product of
n+1
Miura operators. It is a degree-
(n+1)
polynomial in
Z_{1}
, yielding
\langle\mathcal{X}\rangle_{k}\neq 0
only if
k=0,1,\cdots,n+1
. Moreover,
k=0
and
k=n+1
give trivial expressions, so that there are
n
non-trivial fundamental
qq
-characters. This exactly matches with the number of fundamental
qq
-characters for the linear quiver gauge theory with
n
gauge nodes (see section
1.3
, where
n=l-2
)
[
43
,
44
]
.
A straightforward computation gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\langle\mathcal{X}(X_{1})\right\rangle_{1}&=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{1-loop}}(a)\sum_{\{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\}}\mathfrak{q}^{|\lambda_{1}|+|\lambda_{2}|}\frac{1}{\prod_{\alpha,\beta=1,2}N_{\lambda_{\alpha}\lambda_{\beta}}(a_{\alpha}/a_{\beta};q_{1},q_{2})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\frac{q_{3}^{-1/2}}{v_{2}}\left(\frac{q_{3}^{-1}}{a_{1}a_{2}}X_{1}\mathcal{Y}(X_{1}q_{3}^{-1/2})+\mathfrak{q}\frac{1}{X_{1}\mathcal{Y}(X_{1}q_{3}^{1/2})}\right),\end{split}

(221)

where
\mathcal{Y}(X):=\prod_{\alpha=1,2}\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{\alpha}}(X,a_{\alpha})
. We abused the notation and denoted
uq_{3}^{i}
simply by
X_{1}
(recall the former is the eigenvalue of the latter on the state
[uq_{3}^{-1}]_{i+1}
). Up to an overall constant, this precisely recovers the fundamental
qq
-character as an observable of the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
pure
SU(2)
gauge theory
[
43
]
. Thus, we establish the simplest example of our general prescription: the Miura operators inserted on the web of
q
-bosons, realized by the transverse M2-branes passing through the web of M5-branes, give rise to the
qq
-characters as codimension-4 defect observables of the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
effective gauge theory on the worldvolume of the M5-brane web.

Remark

Note that the degree-one term in the R-matrix (
220
), which is responsible for the non-trivial
qq
-character, is proportional to
\Delta_{\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}},\text{M5}_{0,0,1}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}(F(X_{1}))
. It was previously observed in
[
59
]
that the
qq
-characters are realized by inserting such generators into the web of
q
-bosons. In this sense, at the computational level, our findings do not introduce new information. The emphasis, however, is on the origin of the generator insertion. In our prescription, the generators are not simply inserted by hand, but appear as a part of the R-matrix (i.e., product of Miura operators) which is present due to the M2-brane transversally intersecting the M5-brane web. In the perspective of the 5d Chern-Simons theory, the
qq
-characters are re-interpreted as the vacuum expectation values of
network
of line and surface defects. As we emphasized earlier, our construction of the
qq
-characters by the M2-M5 intersection is compatible with the original crossed D-brane configuration in the gauge origami
[
43
,
44
]
, related to each other by a string duality (see section
1.3
).

6
Discussion

There are many interesting directions that deserve further studies.

MacMahon representations

We may replace
\mathcal{C}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}\times\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
in the twisted M-theory background by a (multiplicative)
A_{P}
singularity, that is to say a
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{Z}
fibration over
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{X}
, where the fiber degenerates over
P
points on the base. In the type IIB web, this is dual to adding
P
D7 branes supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times S^{1}(R)\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
at points on
\mathbb{R}_{x}
. It has been suggested that such branes are natural candidates to be MacMahon representations of the quantum toroidal algebra
[
31
]
. It would be important to make this point precise, and ultimately further our understanding of 7-brane physics via the representation theory of quantum toroidal algebras. See
[
11
]
for the case of
\mathcal{C}=(\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C})/\mathbb{Z}_{P+1}
.

Miki automorphism and 5-branes

In section
4
, we saw that the Miura R-matrices were captured by crossings of D3 and NS5-branes in type IIB. In an S-dual frame, we would study crossings of D3 and D5-branes instead, related to the previous setup by action of the Miki automorphism, see section
5.1.1
. At such crossings, we would construct S-dual Miura operators at such crossings, where quantum toroidal vector representations are taken as before, while the Fock representations are no longer “horizontal”
q
-boson vertex representations, but instead the “vertical” representations (
206
). In a fully generic S-dual frame, the Miura operator will be realized via crossing of a D3-brane with a
\mathbf{(p,q)}
5-brane. We leave the derivation of such S-dual operators to future work.

3d non-abelian gauge theory

We only considered only a single D3-brane in the type IIB web; for an arbitrary number of NS5-branes, the low energy 3d theory was a quiver whose gauge content was a product of abelian groups. We can increase the total number of D3-branes, which increases the rank of the gauge group(s) in the quiver. This non-abelian case brings about new subtleties with regards to the cancellation of the gauge anomaly, which generically requires turning on bare Chern-Simons terms, but the computation of the half-index remains straightforward: in the simple case of
K
semi-infinite D3-branes intersecting a single NS5-brane (and ending on another NS5-brane), the half-index is a product of
K
R-matrices of the form (
129
); it follows from section
2.1.2
that if we expand this product as a series in
q
-shift operators, the coefficients can all be expressed in terms of generators of spherical DAHA. It would be nice to work out nontrivial examples directly from the type IIB frame.

Geometric Langlands Program

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a simple Lie algebra. The quantum
q
-Langlands correspondence is the statement on the Riemann surface
\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, there exists an isomorphism of spaces of
q
-conformal blocks for two different algebras :

{\cal W}_{q_{1},q_{2}}(\mathfrak{g})\;\leftrightarrow\;U_{\hbar}(\widehat{{}^{L}\mathfrak{g}}_{{}^{L}k})\;,

(222)

where the parameters
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
on the left-hand side are related to the quantization parameter
\hbar
and the affine Kac-Moody level
{}^{L}k
on the right-hand side, all taken complex and not equal to roots of unity.
At the present time, the correspondence has been shown to hold only for blocks valued in tensor products of fundamental representations
[
41
]
and Verma representations
[
151
,
152
]
. It is a tantalizing possibility that the quantum
q
-Langlands correspondence could be embedded into the quantum toroidal algebra, at least for
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}(r)
. Indeed, in that case, the algebra on the left-hand side is the one governing the Fock representations of
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
at level
r
, and each
q
-conformal block likewise has an elegant interpretation as the vev of specific operators in
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
, following section
4.1
. The puzzle lies in the appearance of the algebra
U_{\hbar}(\widehat{{}^{L}\mathfrak{g}}_{{}^{L}k})
on the right-hand side; in particular, the interpretation of its
q
-conformal blocks will require a representation theoretic understanding of K-theoretic stable envelopes
[
39
]
. Taking the degeneration limit, it would also be desirable to relate our study of M-brane intersections and R-matrices to the 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theory formulation of the (
\hbar
-)Langlands correspondence
[
72
,
42
,
76
]
. This is work in progress.

Appendix A
Quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)

A.1
Definition

The quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
, denoted by
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
, is an associative algebra with the generators
E_{k},F_{k},K_{\pm r}^{\pm}

(k\in\mathbb{Z},\,r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})
and
C
. We introduce the generating currents as

\displaystyle E(X)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}E_{k}X^{-k},\quad F(X)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{k}X^{-k},\quad K^{\pm}(X)=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}K_{\pm r}^{\pm}X^{\mp r}.

(223)

In terms of these generating currents, the defining relations are

\displaystyle C\text{ is central}

(224a)

\displaystyle 1=K^{+}_{0}K^{-}_{0}=K^{-}_{0}K^{+}_{0}

(224b)

\displaystyle K^{\pm}(X)K^{\pm}(X^{\prime})=K^{\pm}(X^{\prime})K^{\pm}(X)

(224c)

\displaystyle\frac{g(C^{-1}X,X^{\prime})}{g(CX,X^{\prime})}K^{-}(X)K^{+}(X^{\prime})=\frac{g(X^{\prime},C^{-1}X)}{g(X^{\prime},CX)}K^{+}(X^{\prime})K^{-}(X)

(224d)

\displaystyle g(X,X^{\prime})E(X)E(X^{\prime})+g(X^{\prime},X)E(X^{\prime})E(X)=0

(224e)

\displaystyle g(X^{\prime},X)F(X)F(X^{\prime})+g(X,X^{\prime})F(X^{\prime})F(X)=0

(224f)

\displaystyle g(X,X^{\prime})K^{\pm}(C^{(1\mp 1)/2}X)E(X^{\prime})+g(X^{\prime},X)E(X^{\prime})K^{\pm}(C^{(1\mp 1)/2}X)=0

(224g)

\displaystyle g(X^{\prime},X)K^{\pm}(C^{(1\pm 1)/2}X)F(X^{\prime})+g(X,X^{\prime})F(X^{\prime})K^{\pm}(C^{(1\pm 1)/2}X)=0

(224h)

\displaystyle[E(X),F(X^{\prime})]=\tilde{g}\left\{\delta\left(C\frac{X^{\prime}}{X}\right)K^{+}(X)-\delta\left(C\frac{X}{X^{\prime}}\right)K^{-}(X^{\prime})\right\},

(224i)

augmented by the Serre relations

\displaystyle 0=\text{Sym}_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}[E_{i_{1}},[E_{i_{2}+1},E_{i_{3}-1}]]

(225a)

\displaystyle 0=\text{Sym}_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}[F_{i_{1}},[F_{i_{2}+1},F_{i_{3}-1}]].

(225b)

Here,
g(X,X^{\prime})
is the structure function defined by

\displaystyle g(X,X^{\prime}):=\prod_{a=1}^{3}(X-q_{a}X^{\prime}).

(226)

Note that
\tilde{g}
only affects the relative normalization of the currents
E(X)
,
F(X)
, and
K^{\pm}(X)
. We will always choose it to be
\tilde{g}=\kappa_{1}^{-1}
, where we defined

\displaystyle\kappa_{n}=\prod_{i=1}^{3}(q_{i}^{\frac{n}{2}}-q_{i}^{-\frac{n}{2}})=\prod_{i=1}^{3}(1-q_{i}^{-n})=-\kappa_{-n}.

(227)

The quantum toroidal algebra
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
is parametrized by two complex numbers,
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})\in\left(\mathbb{C}^{\times}\right)^{\times 3}
constrained by
q_{1}q_{2}q_{3}=1
. They enter in the definition through (
226
) and (
227
). Note that there is an explicit triality of permuting
q_{1}
,
q_{2}
, and
q_{3}
.

We reorganize
\left(K^{\pm}_{\pm r}\right)_{r=0}^{\infty}
in terms of
C^{\perp}
and
\left(H_{\pm r}\right)_{r=0}^{\infty}
by

\displaystyle K^{\pm}(X)=(C^{\perp})^{\mp 1}\exp\left(\pm\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}H_{\pm r}X^{\mp r}\right),

(228)

where
K^{-}_{0}=(K^{+}_{0})^{-1}=C^{\perp}
is a central element.

It is helpful to recast the defining relations in terms of the modes. It is straightforward to see (
224c
) and (
224d
) are equivalent to the
q
-boson commutation relation satisfied by
H_{r}
,

\displaystyle[H_{r},H_{s}]=\delta_{r+s,0}\frac{r}{\kappa_{r}}(C^{r}-C^{-r}).

(229)

Also, the relations (
224e
), (
224f
), and (
224i
) read

\displaystyle[E_{k+2},E_{l-1}]-(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})E_{k+1}E_{l}+(q_{1}^{-1}+q_{2}^{-1}+q_{3}^{-1})E_{l}E_{k+1}+(k\leftrightarrow l)=0

(230a)

\displaystyle[F_{k+2},F_{l-1}]-(q_{1}^{-1}+q_{2}^{-1}+q_{3}^{-1})F_{k+1}F_{l}+(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})F_{l}F_{k+1}+(k\leftrightarrow l)=0

(230b)

\displaystyle[E_{k},F_{l}]=\tilde{g}\left(C^{-l}K^{+}_{k+l}\theta_{k+l\geq 0}-C^{-k}K^{-}_{k+l}\theta_{k+l\leq 0}\right),

(230c)

where in the last relation we used the notation

\displaystyle\theta_{\{\text{condition}\}}=\begin{cases}1\qquad\text{if the condition is true}\\
0\qquad\text{if the condition is false}\end{cases}.

(231)

Finally, the relations (
224g
) and (
224h
) are equivalent to

\displaystyle[H_{r},E_{k}]=C^{(r-|r|)/2}E_{k+r}

(232a)

\displaystyle[H_{r},F_{k}]=-C^{(r+|r|)/2}F_{k+r}.

(232b)

Again, note the explicit invariance under the triality of exchanging
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
.

A.2
Coproduct

The quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
is a Hopf algebra. In particular, it has a (formal) coproduct
\Delta:U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\,\widehat{\otimes}\,U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
defined by

\displaystyle\Delta(E(X))=E(X)\otimes 1+K^{-}(C_{1}X)\otimes E(C_{1}X)

(233a)

\displaystyle\Delta(F(X))=1\otimes F(X)+F(C_{2}X)\otimes K^{+}(C_{2}X)

(233b)

\displaystyle\Delta(K^{+}(X))=K^{+}(X)\otimes K^{+}(C_{1}^{-1}X)

(233c)

\displaystyle\Delta(K^{-}(X))=K^{-}(C_{2}^{-1}X)\otimes K^{-}(X)

(233d)

\displaystyle\Delta(C)=C\otimes C,

(233e)

where we used the notation
C_{1}=C\otimes 1
and
C_{2}=1\otimes C
. The image of the formal coproduct contains infinite summations of elements, and thus has to be understood as lying in the completed tensor product. The infinite summation is expected to truncate to a finite summation when represented on appropriate modules over
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))
.

A.3
Grading operators

The quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
is equipped with a
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
-grading with two grading operators
d
and
d^{\perp}
, which act by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&[d,E(X)]=-X\partial_{X}E(X),\quad[d,F(X)]=-X\partial_{X}F(X),\quad[d,K^{\pm}(X)]=-X\partial_{X}K^{\pm}(X),\\
&[d^{\perp},E(X)]=E(X),\quad[d^{\perp},F(X)]=-F(X),\quad[d^{\perp},K^{\pm}(X)]=0,\\
&[d,C]=[d^{\perp},C]=0.\end{split}

(234)

We define
\text{deg}=(d^{\perp},d):U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\to\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, so that we have

\displaystyle\deg E_{k}=(1,k),\quad\deg F_{k}=(-1,k),\quad\deg H_{r}=(0,r),\quad\deg C=\deg C^{\perp}=(0,0).

(235)

In particular, for any parameter
q\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
it follows from (
234
) that

\displaystyle q^{d}E(X)=E(q^{-1}X)q^{d},\quad q^{d}F(X)=F(q^{-1}X)q^{d},\quad q^{d}K^{\pm}(X)=K^{\pm}(q^{-1}X)q^{d}.

(236)

Appendix B
Spherical double affine Hecke algebras

B.1
Double affine Hecke algebras

The double affine Hecke algebra (DAHA) of
GL(m)
, denoted by
\mathbf{\ddot{H}}^{(m)}_{q,t^{-1}}
, is an associative algebra generated by
T_{i}^{\pm 1}
(
i=1,2,\cdots,m-1
),
X_{i}^{\pm 1}
, and
Y_{i}^{\pm 1}
(
i=1,2,\cdots,m
) subject to the following relations:

\displaystyle(T_{i}-t^{\frac{1}{2}})(T_{i}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}})=0,\qquad T_{i}T_{i+1}T_{i}=T_{i+1}T_{i}T_{i+1}

(237a)

\displaystyle T_{i}T_{j}=T_{j}T_{i}\qquad\text{if}\;\;|i-j|>1

(237b)

\displaystyle X_{i}X_{j}=X_{j}X_{i},\qquad Y_{i}Y_{j}=Y_{j}Y_{i}

(237c)

\displaystyle T_{i}X_{i}T_{i}=X_{i+1},\qquad T_{i}^{-1}Y_{i}T_{i}^{-1}=Y_{i+1}

(237d)

\displaystyle T_{i}X_{j}=X_{j}T_{i},\qquad T_{i}Y_{j}=Y_{j}T_{i}\qquad\text{if}\;\;j\neq i,i+1

(237e)

\displaystyle Y_{1}X_{1}\cdots X_{m}=qX_{1}\cdots X_{m}Y_{1}

(237f)

\displaystyle X_{1}^{-1}Y_{2}=Y_{2}X_{1}^{-1}T_{1}^{-2}.

(237g)

Here, the subscripts are understood modulo
m
. The DAHA is parameterized by two complex numbers
q,t^{-1}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
(we intentionally use
t^{-1}
instead of
t
, since it is
q
and
t^{-1}
which get identified with two out of the triple
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
, parametrizing the quantum toroidal algebra of
\mathfrak{gl}(1)
under the algebra homomorphism).

The DAHA is endowed with the automorphism group given by the braid group on three strands,

\displaystyle B_{3}=\left\langle\left.\tau_{\pm}\;\right|\;\tau_{+}\tau_{-}^{-1}\tau_{+}=\tau_{-}^{-1}\tau_{+}\tau_{-}^{-1}\right\rangle.

(238)

The action on the generators is given by

\displaystyle\tau_{+}:\begin{cases}T_{i}\mapsto T_{i}\\
X_{i}\mapsto X_{i}Y_{i}(T_{i-1}\cdots T_{1})(T_{1}\cdots T_{i-1})\\
Y_{i}\mapsto Y_{i}\end{cases},\quad\tau_{-}:\begin{cases}T_{i}\mapsto T_{i}\\
X_{i}\mapsto X_{i}\\
Y_{i}\mapsto Y_{i}X_{i}(T_{i-1}^{-1}\cdots T_{1}^{-1})(T_{1}^{-1}\cdots T_{i-1}^{-1})\end{cases}.

(239)

Note that

\displaystyle\sigma:=\tau_{+}\tau_{-}^{-1}\tau_{+}=\tau_{-}^{-1}\tau_{+}\tau_{-}^{-1}:\begin{cases}T_{i}\mapsto T_{i}\\
X_{i}\mapsto Y_{i}^{-1}\\
Y_{i}\mapsto Y_{i}X_{i}Y_{i}^{-1}\end{cases}.

(240)

The braid group
B_{3}
is an extension of
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
by
\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle 0\to\mathbb{Z}\to B_{3}\to SL(2,\mathbb{Z})\to 1,

(241)

where the kernel of the projection
B_{3}\to SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
is generated by
(\tau_{+}\tau_{-}^{-1}\tau_{+})^{4}
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\tau_{+}

\displaystyle\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad\tau_{-}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
1&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad\sigma\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}.

(242)

There is a faithful representation
\varphi_{m}
of
\ddot{\mathbf{H}}_{q,t^{-1}}^{(m)}
on the space
\mathbb{C}(q,t^{\frac{1}{2}})[X_{1}^{\pm 1},\cdots,X_{m}^{\pm 1}]
defined by
[
153
]

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{m}(X_{i})=X_{i},\\
&\varphi_{m}(T_{i})=t^{\frac{1}{2}}s_{i}+\frac{t^{\frac{1}{2}}-t^{-\frac{1}{2}}}{X_{i}/X_{i+1}-1}(s_{i}-1),\\
&\varphi_{m}(Y_{i})=\varphi_{m}(T_{i})\cdots\varphi_{m}(T_{m-1})\omega\varphi_{m}(T_{1}^{-1})\cdots\varphi_{m}(T_{i-1}^{-1}),\end{split}

(243)

where
s_{i}
stands for the transposition
X_{i}\leftrightarrow X_{i+1}
;
\omega=s_{m-1}\cdots s_{1}q^{D_{X_{1}}}
; and
q^{D_{X_{i}}}
is the difference operator
q^{D_{X_{i}}}f(X_{j})=f(q^{\delta_{i,j}}X_{j})
.

B.2
Spherical double affine Hecke algebras

Let
s_{i}\in S_{m}
be the transposition
s_{i}=(i,i+1)
, and let
l:S_{m}\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
be the length function. For a reduced decomposition
w=s_{i_{1}}\cdots s_{i_{r}}
of
w\in S_{m}
, we define
T_{w}=T_{i_{1}}\cdots T_{i_{r}}
. It is easy to show that

\displaystyle S=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1-t}{1-t^{i}}\right)\sum_{w\in S_{m}}t^{\frac{l(w)}{2}}T_{w}

(244)

is an idempotent element in
\mathbf{\ddot{H}}^{(m)}_{q,t^{-1}}
, i.e.,
S^{2}=S
. Also, for any
i
we have
T_{i}S=ST_{i}=t^{\frac{1}{2}}S
. The spherical DAHA is defined to be the subalgebra

\displaystyle\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q,t^{-1}}=S\mathbf{\ddot{H}}^{(m)}_{q,t^{-1}}S.

(245)

The spherical DAHA is generated by

\displaystyle\begin{split}&P^{(m)}_{0,r}=S\sum_{i=1}^{m}Y_{i}^{r}S,\qquad P^{(m)}_{0,-r}=q^{r}S\sum_{i=1}^{m}Y_{i}^{-r}S,\\
&P^{(m)}_{r,0}=q^{r}S\sum_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}^{r}S,\quad P^{(m)}_{-r,0}=S\sum_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}^{-r}S,\end{split}\qquad r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0},

(246)

with the relations following from (
237
). The action of the braid group
B_{3}
on
\mathbf{\ddot{H}}^{(m)}_{q,t^{-1}}
preserves the subalgebra
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q,t^{-1}}
. Moreover, the corresponding action factors through the projection
B_{3}\to SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
; namely,
(\tau_{+}\tau_{-}^{-1}\tau_{+})^{4}
acts trivially on
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q,t^{-1}}
. The remaining
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
-action on
\mathbf{S\ddot{H}}^{(m)}_{q,t^{-1}}
is given by

\displaystyle P^{(m)}_{a,b}\mapsto P^{(m)}_{g(a,b)},\qquad g\in SL(2,\mathbb{Z}).

(247)

Some useful formulas for the other generators obtained by the action of
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
are

\displaystyle\begin{split}&P^{(m)}_{1,k}=q\frac{1-t^{m}}{1-t}SX_{1}Y_{1}^{k}S,\quad P^{(m)}_{-1,k}=\frac{1-t^{-m}}{1-t^{-1}}SY_{1}^{k}X_{1}^{-1}S,\\
&P^{(m)}_{k,1}=\frac{1-t^{-m}}{1-t^{-1}}SY_{1}X_{1}^{k}S,\quad P^{(m)}_{k,-1}=q\frac{1-t^{m}}{1-t}SX_{1}^{k}Y_{1}^{-1}S\end{split}\qquad k\in\mathbb{Z}.

(248)

The polynomial representation (
243
) of DAHA,
\mathbf{H}_{q,t^{-1}}^{(m)}
, restricts to a faithful representation of the spherical DAHA,
\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}_{q,t^{-1}}^{(m)}
, on the space of symmetric polynomials
\mathbb{C}(q,t)[X_{1}^{\pm 1},\cdots,X_{m}^{\pm 1}]^{S_{m}}
, given by
[
154
]
(after redefining the difference operators by a simple function of
(X_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\varphi_{m}(Se_{r}(X)S)=e_{r}(X),\\
&\varphi_{m}(Se_{r}(Y)S)=\hat{H}_{r}:=t^{\frac{r(r-1)}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\{1,2,\cdots,m\}\\
|I|=r\end{subarray}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in I\\
j\notin I\end{subarray}}\frac{tX_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)\prod_{i\in I}q^{D_{X_{i}}},\end{split}

(249)

where
e_{r}(X)=\sum_{1\leq i_{1}<\cdots<i_{r}\leq m}X_{i_{1}}\cdots X_{i_{r}}
is the
r
-th elementary symmetric polynomial of
(X_{i})_{i=1}^{m}
, and similarly for
e_{r}(Y)
. In particular, the difference operators
(\hat{H}_{r})_{r=1}^{m}
are precisely the Macdonald operators, which provide the quantum Hamiltonians of the trigonometric Ruijsenaars-Schneider model of type
GL(m)
.

In section
2.1.2
, we used the polynomial representation
\varphi_{m}\circ\sigma
obtained by composing (
249
) with the automorphism
\sigma
(
240
). This representation can be written explicitly as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&P_{0,r}^{(m)}\mapsto q^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}^{r},\qquad P_{0,-r}^{(m)}\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{m}X_{i}^{-r},\qquad r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\\
\hfil\displaystyle\begin{split}&P_{1,k}^{(m)}\mapsto q\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\prod_{j\neq i}\frac{t^{-1}X_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)X_{i}^{k}q^{-D_{X_{i}}}\\
&P_{-1,k}^{(m)}\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left(\prod_{j\neq i}\frac{tX_{i}-X_{j}}{X_{i}-X_{j}}\right)q^{D_{X_{i}}}X_{i}^{k}\end{split},\qquad k\in\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

(250)

B.3
Spherical DAHA as representation of quantum toroidal algebra

At each
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, there is a surjective algebra homomorphism
U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1))\twoheadrightarrow\text{\bf{S}}\boldsymbol{\ddot{\text{\bf{H}}}}^{(m)}_{q_{2},q_{1}}
given by
[
90
]

\displaystyle\begin{split}&E_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{q_{2}(1-q_{2})}P^{(m)}_{1,k},\quad F_{k}\mapsto\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{-1}}P^{(m)}_{-1,k},\quad H_{\pm r}\mapsto\mp\frac{1}{1-q_{2}^{r}}P^{(m)}_{0,\pm r}\\
&C\mapsto 1,\quad C^{\perp}\mapsto 1,\end{split}

(251)

with
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

Appendix C
Solving the R-matrix

The constraint for the R-matrix
R^{(c)}=(\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}})\mathcal{R}
,
c\in\{1,2,3\}
, reads

\displaystyle R^{(c)}\Delta_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}(g)=\Delta^{\text{op}}_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}(g)R^{(c)},\qquad\text{for any }g\in U_{q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}(1)).

(252)

We solve the constraint by making an ansatz,

\displaystyle R^{(c)}=\bar{R}^{(c)}K^{(c)}\in\text{M2}_{0,0,1}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}},

(253)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}&K^{(c)}=\left(q_{3}^{\frac{d}{2}}\otimes 1\right)\exp\left[\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{X_{1}^{-r}}{1-q_{3}^{r}}\otimes\frac{a_{r}^{(c)}}{q_{c}^{r/2}-q_{c}^{-r/2}}\right]\\
&\bar{R}^{(c)}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{m}\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}.\end{split}

(254)

Here, we set

\displaystyle\bar{R}_{m}^{(c)}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},\cdots,k_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}\right)\left(E_{k_{1}}E_{k_{2}}\cdots E_{k_{m}}\otimes F_{-k_{1}}F_{-k_{2}}\cdots F_{-k_{m}}\right)

(255)

so that
\bar{R}^{(c)}
is expanded with respect to the
d^{\perp}
-grading, where
\alpha_{m}\in\mathbb{C}
are coefficients to be determined.

Let us denote
\Delta_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1},\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}=(\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}})\Delta
simply by
\Delta
for brevity. Let us also omit explicit representations, so that
g_{1}\otimes g_{2}
is to be read as
\left(\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}\otimes\rho_{\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}}\right)(g_{1}\otimes g_{2})
. Moreover, we write the zero-mode term in the
q
-boson representation as
v\equiv e^{a_{0}^{(c)}\frac{\log q_{c+1}\log q_{c-1}}{\log q_{c}}}
.

First, we show that, for each
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

\displaystyle\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\Delta(K^{+}(X))=\Delta^{\text{op}}(K^{+}(X))\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}.

(256)

A straightforward computation gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\Delta(K^{+}(X))=\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\left(K^{+}(X)\otimes\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\right)\\
&=\frac{v^{-m}(1-q_{c}^{-1})^{m}}{\kappa_{1}^{m}(1-q_{3})^{m}}\left(X_{1}^{k_{1}}(q_{3}^{-1}X_{1})^{k_{2}}\cdots(q_{3}^{-m+1}X_{1})^{k_{m}}\otimes\left(\xi_{c}\right)_{k_{1}}\cdots\left(\xi_{c}\right)_{k_{m}}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times(Z_{1}^{m}\otimes 1)\left(K^{+}(q_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\right)K^{(c)}\\
&=\frac{v^{-m}(1-q_{c}^{-1})^{m}}{\kappa_{1}^{m}(1-q_{3})^{m}}\xi_{c}(X_{1})\xi_{c}(q_{3}^{-1}X_{1})\cdots\xi_{c}(q_{3}^{-m+1}X_{1})\left(K^{+}(q_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}+m}X)\otimes\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\right)Z_{1}^{m}K^{(c)}\\
&=\exp\left[\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{1-q_{3}^{-mr}}{1-q_{3}^{-r}}q_{c}^{\frac{r}{2}}\left(\frac{X_{1}}{X}\right)^{r}\right]\left(K^{+}(q_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}+m}X)\otimes\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\\
&=\left(K(q_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\\
&=\Delta^{\text{op}}(K^{+}(X))\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)},\end{split}

(257)

where we used (
236
) for the second equality and the normal-ordering relation

\displaystyle\begin{split}\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\xi(X^{\prime})&=\exp\left[-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\left(\frac{q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X^{\prime}}{X}\right)^{r}\right]:\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\xi(X^{\prime}):\\
&=\prod_{a=1}^{3}\frac{1-q_{a}q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{X^{\prime}}{X}}{1-q_{a}^{-1}q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{X^{\prime}}{X}}:\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\xi(X^{\prime}):,\end{split}

(258)

in the fourth equality.

In a similar way, we can show that the following relation holds for each
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
:
