\displaystyle\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\Delta(K^{-}(X))=\Delta^{\text{op}}(K^{-}(X))\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}.

(259)

A straightforward computation gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\Delta(K^{-}(X))=\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\left(K^{-}(q_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi^{-}_{c}(X)\right)\\
&=\frac{v^{-m}(1-q_{c}^{-1})^{m}}{\kappa_{1}^{m}(1-q_{3})^{m}}\xi_{c}(X_{1})\cdots\xi_{c}(q_{3}^{-m+1}X_{1})(Z_{1}^{m}\otimes 1)\left(K^{-}(X)\otimes\varphi^{-}_{c}(X)\right)K^{(c)}\\
&=\exp\left[-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{1-q_{3}^{mr}}{1-q_{3}^{r}}\left(\frac{X}{X_{1}}\right)^{r}\right]\left(K^{-}(q_{3}^{m}X)\otimes\varphi^{-}_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\\
&=\left(K^{-}(X)\otimes\varphi^{-}_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\\
&=\Delta^{\text{op}}(K^{-}(X))\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)},\end{split}

(260)

where we used (
236
) and the normal-ordering relation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{c}(X_{1})\varphi^{-}(X)&=\exp\left[-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{1-q_{c}^{-r}}{1-q_{3}^{r}}\left(\frac{q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X}{X_{1}}\right)^{r}\right]:V_{c}(X_{1})\varphi^{-}(X):\\
&=K^{-}(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)K^{-}(q_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X)^{-1}:V_{c}(X_{1})\varphi^{-}(X):,\end{split}

(261)

for the second equality; and the normal-ordering relation

\displaystyle\begin{split}\xi_{c}(X)\varphi^{-}_{c}(X^{\prime})&=\exp\left[-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\left(\frac{X^{\prime}}{X}\right)^{r}\right]:\xi_{c}(X)\varphi^{-}_{c}(X^{\prime}):\\
&=\prod_{a=1}^{3}\frac{1-\frac{q_{c}q_{a}X}{X^{\prime}}}{1-\frac{q_{c}q_{a}^{-1}X}{X^{\prime}}}:\xi_{c}(X)\varphi^{-}_{c}(X^{\prime}):,\end{split}

(262)

for the third equality.

Next, we turn to the relation

\displaystyle R^{(c)}\Delta(E(X))=\Delta^{\text{op}}(E(X))R^{(c)}.

(263)

Recall that
R^{(c)}
is expanded with respect to the
d^{\perp}
-grading, so that the relation has to be satisfied for each degree of the grading. The degree-
m
relation is found to be

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\left(E(X)\otimes 1\right)+\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m+1}K^{(c)}\left(K^{-}(X)\otimes\left(-v\frac{1-q_{c}}{\kappa_{1}}\right)\eta_{c}(X)\right)\\
&\qquad=\left(1\otimes\left(-v\frac{1-q_{c}}{\kappa_{1}}\right)\eta_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m+1}K^{(c)}+\left(E(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi^{-}_{c}(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}.\end{split}

(264)

Note that there is a recursive relation

\displaystyle\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}=\frac{\alpha_{m}}{\alpha_{m-1}}\frac{v^{-1}(1-q_{c}^{-1})}{\kappa_{1}(1-q_{3})}\xi_{c}(X_{1})Z_{1}\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m-1}.

(265)

Let us assume
\gamma\equiv\frac{\alpha_{m}}{\alpha_{m-1}}
, and look for its value in which (
264
) holds. We proceed by induction. Assume (
264
) holds for
0\leq m\leq m^{\prime}-1
. Then, for
m=m^{\prime}
, the left hand side of (
264
) gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\gamma\frac{v^{-1}(1-q_{c}^{-1})}{\kappa_{1}(1-q_{3})}\xi_{c}(X_{1})Z_{1}\\
&\times\left[\left(1\otimes\left(-v\frac{1-q_{c}}{\kappa_{1}}\right)\eta_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}}K^{(c)}+\left(E(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi^{-}_{c}(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}-1}K^{(c)}\right].\end{split}

(266)

The second term in the square bracket vanishes, due to the normal-ordering relation (
262
) and the delta function in
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}(E(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}q_{3}X))
. The remaining term is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&-\gamma\frac{(1-q_{c}^{-1})(1-q_{c})}{\kappa_{1}^{2}(1-q_{3})}\xi_{c}(X_{1})\eta_{c}(X)Z_{1}\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}}K^{(c)}\\
&=-\gamma\frac{(1-q_{c}^{-1})(1-q_{c})}{\kappa_{1}^{2}(1-q_{3})}\left[\frac{(1-q_{c+1})(1-q_{c-1})}{1-q_{c}^{-1}}\left\{\delta\left(\frac{q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X}{X_{1}}\right)\varphi^{-}_{c}(X_{1})-\delta\left(\frac{q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{1}}{X}\right)\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\right\}\right.\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\eta_{c}(X)\xi_{c}(X_{1})\Bigg]Z_{1}\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}}K^{(c)}\\
&=\left(1\otimes\left(-v\frac{1-q_{c}}{\kappa_{1}}\right)\eta_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}+1}K^{(c)}\\
&\qquad+\frac{\gamma}{\kappa_{1}}\left(E(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi^{-}(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)-E(q_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi_{c}^{+}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}}K^{(c)},\end{split}

(267)

where we used the commutation relation

\displaystyle[\xi_{c}(X_{1}),\eta_{c}(X)]=\frac{(1-q_{c+1})(1-q_{c-1})}{1-q_{c}^{-1}}\left\{\delta\left(\frac{q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X}{X_{1}}\right)\varphi^{-}_{c}(X_{1})-\delta\left(\frac{q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{1}}{X}\right)\varphi^{+}_{c}(X)\right\}

(268)

for the first equality. The second term in the last line of (
267
) vanishes, due to the normal-ordering relation (
258
) and
\rho_{\text{M2}_{0,0,1}}(E(q_{c}^{-\frac{1}{2}}X))=\frac{1}{1-q_{3}}\delta(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{1}/X)Z_{1}
. Once we pass
Z_{1}
to the right, the numerator of (
258
) vanishes due to the delta function. All in all, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}}K^{(c)}\left(E(X)\otimes 1\right)+\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}+1}K^{(c)}\left(K^{-}(X)\otimes\left(-v\frac{1-q_{c}}{\kappa_{1}}\right)\eta_{c}(X)\right)\\
&=\left(1\otimes\left(-v\frac{1-q_{c}}{\kappa_{1}}\right)\eta_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}+1}K^{(c)}+\frac{\gamma}{\kappa_{1}}\left(E(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi^{-}(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m^{\prime}}K^{(c)}.\end{split}

(269)

The desired relation (
264
) is exactly recovered if and only if
\gamma=\kappa_{1}
.

Finally, we check the relation

\displaystyle R^{(c)}\Delta(F(X))=\Delta^{\text{op}}(F(X))R^{(c)}.

(270)

The degree-
m
relation reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\left(1\otimes v^{-1}\frac{1-q_{c}^{-1}}{\kappa_{1}}\xi_{c}(X)\right)+\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m+1}K^{(c)}\left(F(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi^{+}_{c}(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\right)\\
&\quad=(F(X)\otimes 1)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m+1}K^{(c)}+\left(K^{+}(X)\otimes v^{-1}\frac{1-q_{c}^{-1}}{\kappa_{1}}\xi_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}.\end{split}

(271)

By the induction hypothesis, the left hand side gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\gamma\frac{v^{-1}(1-q_{c}^{-1})}{\kappa_{1}(1-q_{3})}\xi_{c}(X_{1})Z_{1}\\
&\quad\times\left[(F(X)\otimes 1)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}+\left(K^{+}(X)\otimes v^{-1}\frac{1-q_{c}^{-1}}{\kappa_{1}}\xi_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m-1}K^{(c)}\right].\end{split}

(272)

The first term in the square bracket gives zero, due to the normal-ordering relation (
86
). The remaining term is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\gamma\frac{v^{-2}(1-q_{c}^{-1})^{2}}{\kappa_{1}^{2}(1-q_{3})}\xi_{c}(X_{1})\left(K^{+}(q_{3}X)\otimes\xi_{c}(X)\right)Z_{1}\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m-1}K^{(c)}\\
&=\frac{\left(1-\frac{X}{X_{1}}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c}X}{X_{1}}\right)}{\left(1-\frac{q_{c+1}^{-1}X}{X_{1}}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c-1}^{-1}X}{X_{1}}\right)}\frac{\left(1-\frac{q_{c+1}^{-1}X_{1}}{X}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c-1}^{-1}X_{1}}{X}\right)}{\left(1-\frac{X_{1}}{X}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c}X_{1}}{X}\right)}(K^{+}(q_{3}X)\otimes F(X))\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\\
&=(K^{+}(X)\otimes F(X))\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\\
&\qquad-\left[\frac{(1-q_{c+1}^{-1})(1-q_{c-1})}{1-q_{c-1}q_{c+1}^{-1}}\delta\left(\frac{q_{c+1}X_{1}}{X}\right)+\frac{(1-q_{c-1}^{-1})(1-q_{c+1})}{1-q_{c+1}q_{c-1}^{-1}}\delta\left(\frac{q_{c-1}X_{1}}{X}\right)\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\frac{\left(1-\frac{q_{c+1}^{-1}X_{1}}{X}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c-1}^{-1}X_{1}}{X}\right)}{\left(1-\frac{X_{1}}{X}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c}X_{1}}{X}\right)}\frac{\left(1-\frac{q_{1}X_{1}}{X}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{2}X_{1}}{X}\right)}{\left(1-\frac{X_{1}}{X}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{3}^{-1}X_{1}}{X}\right)}(1\otimes F(X))\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)},\end{split}

(273)

where we used the normal-ordering relation (
86
) for the first equality and the relation

\displaystyle f(X)_{+}-f(X)_{-}=\sum_{\alpha}\underset{X=X^{(\alpha)}}{\text{Res}}\left(\frac{f(X)}{X}\right)\delta\left(\frac{X^{(\alpha)}}{X}\right)

(274)

for the second equality. Here,
f(X)
is a rational function with simple poles at
X=X^{(\alpha)}
, and
f(X)_{+}
(resp.
f(X)_{-}
) is the expansion of
f(X)
in the domain
|X|>\!\!>1
(resp.
|X|<\!\!<1
). Due to the delta functions, only one of the two terms in the square bracket survives, yielding

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{1-q_{3}^{-1}}\delta\left(\frac{q_{3}X_{1}}{X}\right)\frac{v^{-1}(1-q_{c}^{-1})}{1-q_{3}}\xi_{c}(X)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\\
&\qquad=\frac{1}{1-q_{3}^{-1}}Z_{1}^{-1}\delta\left(\frac{X_{1}}{X}\right)\frac{v^{-1}(1-q_{c}^{-1})}{1-q_{3}}\xi_{c}(X_{1})Z_{1}\bar{R}_{m}^{(c)}K^{(c)}\\
&\qquad=\frac{\kappa_{1}}{\gamma}(F(X)\otimes 1)\bar{R}_{m+1}^{(c)}K^{(c)}.\end{split}

(275)

Therefore, we arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}\left(1\otimes v^{-1}\frac{1-q_{c}^{-1}}{\kappa_{1}}\xi_{c}(X)\right)+\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m+1}K^{(c)}\left(F(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\otimes\varphi^{+}_{c}(q_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}X)\right)\\
&\quad=\frac{\kappa_{1}}{\gamma}(F(X)\otimes 1)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m+1}K^{(c)}+\left(K^{+}(X)\otimes v^{-1}\frac{1-q_{c}^{-1}}{\kappa_{1}}\xi_{c}(X)\right)\bar{R}^{(c)}_{m}K^{(c)}.\end{split}

(276)

Again, the desired relation (
271
) is precisely recovered if and only if
\gamma=\kappa_{1}
. By choosing the overall normalization by
\alpha_{0}=1
, we therefore obtain the solution
\alpha_{m}=\kappa_{1}^{m}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. This completes the derivation of the R-matrix between
\text{M2}_{0,0,1}
and
\text{M5}_{c}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}
.

If
c=3
, the derivation could have been simplified in fact, since the ansatz (
255
) truncates to the first two terms as a result of the normal ordering. In any case, the derivation we presented applies to all the cases
c\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Appendix D
Maximality of set of screening charges

In this appendix, we sketch a proof of the following proposition: the only operators in the tensor product of two
q
-bosons which commute with the generators produced by the Miura transformation are the screening operators of section
3.3.3
.

Let us first consider a single
\text{M2}_{0,0,1}
brane, with support on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{3}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
.
By definition, the Miura transformation is a product of Miura operators, which in the light of our results is a product of R-matrices:

{R}^{(c_{L+M+N})}_{L+M+N}\,\cdots\,{R}^{(c_{2})}_{2}\,{R}^{(c_{1})}_{1}\;,\qquad\qquad\qquad\;R^{(c_{I})}_{I}=\bar{R}^{(c_{I})}_{I}K^{(c_{I})}_{I},\;\;c\in\{1,2,3\}

(277)

To make this appendix self-contained, we rewrite the operator definitions here. The Cartan part reads

\displaystyle K^{(c_{I})}_{I}=\left(q_{c_{I}}^{\frac{d}{2}}\otimes 1\right)V^{(c_{I})}_{I}(X)=\left(q_{c_{I}}^{\frac{d}{2}}\otimes 1\right)\exp\left[\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{X^{-r}}{1-q_{3}^{r}}\otimes\frac{a_{r}^{[I]}}{q_{c_{I}}^{r/2}-q_{c}^{-r/2}}\right]

(278)

with
V^{(c_{I})}_{I}(X):=\exp\left[\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{X^{-r}}{1-q_{3}^{r}}\otimes\frac{a_{r}^{[I]}}{q_{c_{I}}^{r/2}-q_{c_{I}}^{-r/2}}\right]
, while the main part of the R-matrix reads

\displaystyle\bar{R}^{(c_{I})}_{I}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}q_{\bar{c_{I}}}^{m}e^{-ma_{0}^{[I]}\frac{\log q_{c_{I}+1}\log q_{c_{I}-1}}{\log q_{c_{I}}}}\left(\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{1-q_{c_{I}}q_{3}^{-j}}{1-q_{3}^{-j-1}}\right):\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}\xi^{(c_{I})}_{I}(q_{3}^{-j}X):q_{3}^{-mD_{X}}.

(279)

with vertex operator

\displaystyle\xi^{(c_{I})}_{I}(X)=\exp\left(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{q_{c}^{r/2}}{(q_{c_{I}}^{r/2}-q_{c_{I}}^{-r/2})^{2}}a_{-r}^{[I]}X^{r}\right)\exp\left(\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{\kappa_{r}}{r}\frac{1}{(q_{c_{I}}^{r/2}-q_{c_{I}}^{-r/2})^{2}}a_{r}^{[I]}X^{-r}\right)\;.

(280)

The main property of the Miura transformation is that it is a generating function of the current generators for the
q
-boson algebra. The expansion parameter of this generating function is the shift operator
q_{3}^{-D_{X}}
. The screening charges of the algebra are defined as the commutant of these currents.

Then, let us consider a general ansatz
43
43

43

It is a priori possible to write down a more general ansatz than (
281
). Our currents coincide with the ansatz proposed in
[
58
,
9
]
, and reduce to familiar conformal screening currents
[
155
,
7
]
in the limit where the
q
-deformation is turned off. While we do not prove it in this work, we conjecture that if the commutator of any endomorphism
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
(not necessarily of the form (
281
)) with our R-matrix is zero, then
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
is necessarily a linear combination of our screening currents ansatz.
for an endomorphism of the tensor product of two Fock spaces
\text{End}\left(\mathcal{F}_{c_{1}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{c_{2}}^{\mathbf{(1,0)}}\right)
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}(Y)&=e^{{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{[1]}_{0}\,d^{\perp}\otimes 1+{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{[2]}_{0}\,1\otimes d^{\perp}}\;{Y}^{\gamma^{[1]}_{0}\,a^{[1]}_{0}\otimes 1+\gamma^{[2]}_{0}\,1\otimes a^{[2]}_{0}+\gamma^{[0]}_{0}1\otimes 1}\\
&\qquad\times\exp\left(\sum_{r>0}\frac{Y^{r}}{r}(\gamma^{[1]}_{-r}\,a^{[1]}_{-r}\otimes 1+\gamma^{[2]}_{-r}\,1\otimes a^{[2]}_{-r})\right)\\
&\qquad\times\exp\left(-\sum_{r>0}\frac{Y^{-r}}{r}(\gamma^{[1]}_{+r}\,a^{[1]}_{+r}\otimes 1+\gamma^{[2]}_{+r}\,1\otimes a^{[2]}_{+r}\right)\;,\end{split}

(281)

with five zero mode unknowns
\gamma^{[0]}_{0},\gamma^{[1]}_{0},\gamma^{[2]}_{0}
, and
\widetilde{\gamma}^{[1]}_{0},\widetilde{\gamma}^{[2]}_{0}
, as well as an infinite number of nonzero mode unknowns:
\gamma^{[1]}_{\pm r},\gamma^{[2]}_{\pm r}
for
r>0
.

Our strategy will be to investigate
[{R}^{(c_{2})}_{2}\,{R}^{(c_{1})}_{1}\;,\;{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}(Y)]
at each order
m
in the shift operator
q_{3}^{-mD_{X}}
, and constrain the unknowns in the ansatz current
{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}(Y)
for the commutator to be a
q
-derivative. Note that it suffices to consider a Miura transformation for the product of two R-matrices
{R}^{(c_{2})}_{2}\,{R}^{(c_{1})}_{1}
, since the ansatz is defined on the tensor product of precisely two Fock spaces.

At order
m=0
, the commutator takes the form

[V^{(c_{2})}_{2}(X\,q^{1/2}_{c_{2}})V^{(c_{1})}_{1}(X\,q^{1/2}_{c_{1}}q^{1/2}_{c_{2}})\;,{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}(Y)]

(282)

Imposing its vanishing (up to
q
-derivatives), we find constraints:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bullet\text{For}\;c_{1}=c_{2},\;\;\gamma^{[1]}_{-r}=-q^{r/2}_{c_{1}}\,\gamma^{[2]}_{-r}\;,\\[8.53581pt]
&\bullet\text{For}\;c_{1}\neq c_{2},\;\;\gamma^{[1]}_{-r}=-\left(\frac{q^{r/2}_{c_{2}}-q^{-r/2}_{c_{2}}}{q^{r/2}_{c_{1}}-q^{-r/2}_{c_{1}}}\right)^{2}\,q^{r/2}_{c_{1}}\,\gamma^{[2]}_{-r}\;.\end{split}

(283)

Note that at this point,
c_{I}\in\{1,2,3\}
. At order
m=1
, the commutator is more involved:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left[q_{\bar{c_{2}}}\frac{1-q_{c_{2}}}{1-q^{-1}_{3}}\,\xi^{(c_{2})}_{2}(X)V^{(c_{2})}_{2}(X\,q^{-1}_{3}q^{1/2}_{c_{2}})V^{(c_{1})}_{1}(X\,q^{-1}_{3}q^{1/2}_{c_{1}}q^{1/2}_{c_{2}})\,e^{a^{[2]}_{0}\frac{\log q_{3}\,\log q_{\bar{c_{2}}}}{\log q_{c_{2}}}}\right.\\
&\;\;\;+\left.q_{\bar{c_{1}}}\frac{1-q_{c_{1}}}{1-q^{-1}_{3}}\,V^{(c_{2})}_{2}(X\,q^{1/2}_{c_{2}})\xi^{(c_{1})}_{1}(X\,q^{1/2}_{c_{1}})V^{(c_{1})}_{1}(X\,q^{1/2}_{c_{1}}q^{1/2}_{c_{2}})\,e^{a^{[1]}_{0}\frac{\log q_{3}\,\log q_{\bar{c_{1}}}}{\log q_{c_{1}}}}\;,\;{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}(Y)\right]\end{split}

(284)

After normal-ordering the currents and making use of the constraints (
283
) at order
m=0
, we find that all the unknowns are determined: a necessary condition for the commutator to be null or proportional to a
q
-derivative is that
c_{I}\neq 3
, so
c_{I}\in\{1,2\}
. Another necessary condition is that
{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}
must be one of the four screening currents
{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}_{1,1}
,
{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}_{2,2}
,
{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}_{1,2}
, or
{\mathcal{S}}^{(3)}_{2,1}
written in (
121
) and (
124
). The first two currents are the “bosonic” screening endomorphisms, on the tensor product of two identical Fock spaces, while the latter two currents are the “fermionic” screening endomorphisms, on the tensor product of two distinct Fock spaces. In more detail, we find that for these four solutions, the commutator is proportional to a
q_{3}
-derivative,

{\cal D}^{Y}_{q_{3}}\left[Y\,\delta\left(q^{j_{c_{1}}}_{1}\,q^{j_{c_{2}}}_{2}Y/X\right)\;:\ldots:\;\right]\;,

(285)

where the derivative operator is defined as
{\cal D}^{Y}_{q_{c}}(f(Y))=\dfrac{f(Y)-f(q_{c}\,Y)}{Y}
for any function
f
. The expression
:\ldots:
stands for the normal ordering of the product of all operators, and
j_{c_{I}}
is a half-integer which is uniquely fixed by the computation, for each
c_{I}\in\{1,2\}
.

There are again at most four solutions if the initial brane is
\text{M2}_{1,0,0}
, supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{1}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
, and four solutions if the initial brane is
\text{M2}_{0,1,0}
, supported on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathbb{R}_{t}
. These solutions are readily obtained by simply permuting the parameters
(q_{1},q_{2},q_{3})
, thanks to the triality of the quantum toroidal algebra. This is precisely the exhaustive list of screenings written in (
121
) and (
124
), for any
c\in\{1,2,3\}
. This completes the proof of the proposition.

Remark

The work
[
9
]
defines the Miura transformation slightly differently, as

\bar{R}^{(c_{L+M+N})}_{L+M+N}(X)\,\cdots\,\bar{R}^{(c_{2})}_{2}(X)\,\bar{R}^{(c_{1})}_{1}(X)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{m}\,T_{m}(X)\,q^{-mD_{X}}_{3}\;,

(286)

where
T_{m}(X)
is the
m
-th generating current of the
qY_{L,M,N}
algebra, expressed as a finite series in the operators (
118
).
In particular, the authors find that the screening charges of the
qY_{L,M,N}
algebra are in the commutant of the operators
T_{m}(X)
. In fact, it is equivalent to show that the screening charges commute with the product of two operators, say
\bar{R}^{(c_{2})}_{2}\,\bar{R}^{(c_{1})}_{1}
. In our work, we found that the
same
screening charges are also in the commutant of the Miura operators
{R}^{(c_{2})}_{2}\,{R}^{(c_{1})}_{1}
, with
R^{(c_{I})}_{I}=\bar{R}^{(c_{I})}_{I}K^{(c_{I})}_{I}
. The advantage of using the Miura operators
R^{(c_{I})}_{I}
is that they have a direct interpretation as R-matrices of the quantum toroidal algebra, unlike the operators
\bar{R}^{(c_{I})}_{I}
.

Appendix E
q
-boson intertwiners for non-transverse M5-brane intersections

The intertwiner
\Psi^{(\mathbf{n})}(v,v^{\prime})
satisfying (
210
) can be expanded in the dual basis of
\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}_{3}
as,

\displaystyle\Psi^{(\mathbf{n})}(v,v^{\prime})=\sum_{\{\lambda\}}\Xi_{\lambda}\Psi^{\mathbf{(n)}}_{\lambda}(v,v^{\prime})\langle v^{\prime},\lambda|

(287)

where the coefficients are given by

\displaystyle\Psi^{\mathbf{(n)}}_{\lambda}(v,v^{\prime})=t_{\mathbf{n}}(\lambda,v,v^{\prime}):\Psi_{\varnothing}(v^{\prime})\prod_{\Box\in\lambda}\eta(\chi_{\Box}):,

(288)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&t_{\mathbf{n}}(\lambda,v,v^{\prime})=(-vv^{\prime})^{|\lambda|}\prod_{\Box\in\lambda}\left(q_{3}^{1/2}/\chi_{\Box}\right)^{\mathbf{n}+1},\qquad\chi_{\Box_{(i,j)}}=v^{\prime}q_{1}^{i-1}q_{2}^{j-1},\\
&\Psi_{\varnothing}(v^{\prime})=\exp\left[\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r}\frac{1}{1-q_{3}^{-r}}a_{-r}^{(3)}v^{\prime r}\right]\exp\left[-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r}\frac{q_{3}^{-r/2}}{1-q_{3}^{-r}}a_{r}^{(3)}v^{\prime-r}\right].\end{split}

(289)

Here, we used the normalization of the dual states such that (our case is
c=3
)

\displaystyle{}^{(c)}\langle u,\lambda|u,\mu\rangle^{(c)}=\left(\Xi_{\lambda}^{(c)}\right)^{-1}\delta_{\lambda,\mu},

(290)

where we defined

\displaystyle\Xi_{\lambda}^{(c)}=\frac{(uq_{c}^{1/2})^{-|\lambda|}\prod_{\Box\in\lambda}\chi_{\Box}}{N^{(c)}_{\lambda\lambda}(1;q_{1},q_{2})},

(291)

with

\displaystyle N^{(c)}_{\lambda\mu}(u_{1}/u_{2};q_{1};q_{2})=\prod_{\Box\in\lambda}\left(1-\frac{\chi_{\Box}}{q_{c}u_{2}}\right)\prod_{\blacksquare\in\mu}\left(1-\frac{u_{1}}{\chi_{\blacksquare}}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\Box\in\lambda\\
\blacksquare\in\mu\end{subarray}}\frac{\left(1-\frac{q_{c+1}\chi_{\square}}{\chi_{\blacksquare}}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c-1}\chi_{\square}}{\chi_{\blacksquare}}\right)}{\left(1-\frac{\chi_{\square}}{\chi_{\blacksquare}}\right)\left(1-\frac{q_{c}^{-1}\chi_{\square}}{\chi_{\blacksquare}}\right)}.

(292)

The intertwiner
\Psi^{*\mathbf{(n)}}(v,v^{\prime})
satisfying (
212
) can be expanded in the basis of
\mathcal{F}^{\mathbf{(0,1)}}_{3}
as

\displaystyle\Psi^{*\mathbf{(n)}}(v,v^{\prime})=\sum_{\{\lambda\}}\Xi_{\lambda}|a,\lambda\rangle\Psi^{*\mathbf{(n)}}_{\lambda}(v,v^{\prime})

(293)

where

\displaystyle\Psi^{*\mathbf{(n)}}_{\lambda}(v,v^{\prime})=t_{\mathbf{n}}^{*}(\lambda,v,v^{\prime}):\Psi^{*}_{\varnothing}(v^{\prime})\prod_{\Box\in\lambda}\xi(\chi_{\Box}):,

(294)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&t_{\mathbf{n}}^{*}(\lambda,v,v^{\prime})=(vq_{3}^{1/2})^{-|\lambda|}\prod_{\Box\in\lambda}(q_{3}^{-1/2}\chi_{\Box})^{\mathbf{n}},\\
&\Psi^{*}_{\varnothing}(v^{\prime})=\exp\left[-\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r}\frac{q_{3}^{r/2}}{1-q_{3}^{-r}}a_{-r}^{(3)}v^{\prime r}\right]\exp\left[\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{r}\frac{1}{1-q_{3}^{r}}a_{r}^{(3)}v^{\prime-r}\right].\end{split}

(295)
